#5049. Bitcoin Crash Leaves Strategy $12B and Bitmine $10B Underwater: How Much Worse Can It Get?

Strategy MSTR stock hit a two-year low and trades below its bitcoin. We modeled MSTR and Bitmine if Bitcoin loses $55,000.

Strategy MSTR stock closed at $77.80 on Friday, November 20, its lowest close in at least two years, after a crypto sell-off sent Bitcoin (BTC) toward $55,000. Tom Lee’s Bitmine Immersion (BMNR) fell 14% the same day to $12.98.

Both stocks have fallen harder than the coins they hold. Since October 6, Strategy MSTR stock has lost 53% while Bitcoin fell 31% to Friday’s close. Bitmine dropped 51% as Ether (ETH) fell 40%.

The two companies are now sitting on big paper losses. Strategy is about $12 billion underwater on its Bitcoin, and Bitmine roughly $10 billion on its Ethereum.

The dots aren’t done. pic.twitter.com/y92ITprFKE

— Michael Saylor (@saylor) November 22, 2026

Both stocks bounced about 8% to 9% in pre-market trading on Monday. Yet the recovery remains uncertain, as Bitcoin still needs a daily close above $62K to confirm the reversal. So, how deep is the hole, and what happens to MSTR and BMNR if BTC loses $55,000?

Strategy owned 850,849 BTC as of November 15, bought for $64.20 billion at an average of $75,452 per coin, its latest SEC filing shows.

With Bitcoin at $61,345, that stack is worth about $52.2 billion. That leaves Strategy 19% below its cost, a paper loss of around $12 billion. Bitcoin needs to climb about 23% just to get the company back to break-even.

The bigger problem for shareholders is the valuation. Counting its debt and preferred stock, and netting out cash, the company is valued at about 0.94 times its bitcoin, our estimate shows. Strategy MSTR stock used to trade at a premium to its coins, and that premium paid for the buying.

Below one, the model works in reverse. Selling new shares to buy bitcoin shrinks each share’s claim on the coins. Strategy kept doing it anyway, selling 2.1 million MSTR shares in the week to November 15 to buy 485 BTC at $68,887. It also spent $228 million buying back its STRC preferred stock.

The debt is not the immediate threat. Strategy owes about $6.7 billion in convertible notes and has $14.1 billion of preferred stock, roughly $20.8 billion in all. Its bitcoin would only fall below that if BTC dropped to about $24,500, and it holds a $4.75 billion cash reserve to pay dividends.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Reveals Upcoming Binance Listings

Bitmine held 6,077,745 ETH as of November 15, about 5% of all Ether, the company said in its weekly update. It bought another 7,653 ETH that week.

Bitmine does not publish an average cost. On-chain analysts put it at about $3,353 per ETH, which means the stack cost roughly $20.4 billion. At $1,724, it is worth $10.5 billion. That is a paper loss of about $9.9 billion, or 49%.

“ETH tumbled 28% in the past two weeks,” Lee said in the update. He tied the drop to selling around Nvidia and the run-up to Anthropic’s listing.

BMNR stock trades at about 0.73 times the value of its crypto, cash and stakes. Bitmine carries no debt, only $350 million of preferred stock, so it faces no forced selling. But like Strategy, it can no longer raise money by selling shares without costing existing holders.

EXPLORE: BitMine’s Estimated $3.6B ETH Loss Overshadows Stock Edge

To see what another leg down would do, we ran both treasuries across Bitcoin prices from $40,000 to $80,000. ETH tends to fall harder than Bitcoin. Over the past year, it moved about 1.25 times as much as BTC on a typical day, so we scaled ETH by that.

A drop to $49,000, the August 2024 crash low, would take Strategy’s loss to $22.5 billion, 35% below cost. Bitmine would be down about 61%.

The stocks would likely fall further than that. Since October 6, Strategy MSTR stock has moved about 1.7 times as much as Bitcoin, and BMNR about 1.3 times as much as Ether. Neither company has to sell coins, but a longer stretch below their holdings would shut off the share sales that funded their buying.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Top Solana Meme Coins to Bid

BTC trades at $61,345, below its 50-day exponential moving average (EMA), an average that gives recent prices more weight, at $73,000, and its 200-day EMA at $75,000.

For Strategy and Bitmine, $55,130 is the number that matters. A daily close below it would push Strategy’s paper loss past $17 billion and put Bitmine down more than half.

#5048. Adam Back Explains Why Bitcoin (BTC) Fell to $17,000 on Kraken's Canadian Dollar Market

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Blockstream CEO Adam Back has explained the overnight sell-off on Kraken's Canadian-dollar markets that sent the exchange's BTC/CAD pair to C$24,250, or roughly $17,000, on Saturday morning, and said that the drop started with the exchange's local USD/CAD market maker.

The Blockstream CEO addressed the issue on X on Saturday in a reply to a post that tracked large sell orders hitting Kraken's BTC/CAD order book at regular intervals throughout the night.

The $17,000 figure is based on the Bank of Canada's Friday rate of C$1.4325 per U.S. dollar. Kraken's BTC/CAD pair printed the C$24,250 low on Saturday morning, according to TradingView data.

"No, the reason was USDCAD Kraken local spot FX market-maker ran out of money, as it has depegged away from market rate 1.434," Back replied.

In the same post, the Blockstream CEO said that it was "more accurate to say you could buy CAD discounted."

Later, another X user argued that the Canadian dollar was in fact overvalued on Kraken during the sell-off, since anyone who held CAD could buy anything at a discount. Back agreed with the correction.

"You are right. BTCUSD normal price, USDCAD CAD mispriced, like a shortage of CAD bid the price up," Back wrote.

On Sunday, the Blockstream CEO described the whole chain of events in two more posts on X.

According to Back, if the USD/CAD market maker failed, there were few buyers of BTC in Canadian dollars, and so the BTC/CAD price fell. In his view, traders would normally close such a gap with an arbitrage trade "by selling USD to CAD to buy if it BTCCAD doesn't track BTCUSD."

Back wrote that the drop "liquidated people" and triggered stop losses "based on a local false price."

"And then USDCAD followed because you could buy BTC cheaply in CAD, so it became logical to buy CAD way over par to take that arb," he added.

The BTC/CAD pair recovered later on Saturday, and on Sunday, it traded at C$86,236 on Kraken, according to TradingView.

Kraken said in a statement that its Canadian-dollar trading pairs faced heavy selling pressure starting around 20:00 UTC on Friday, and prices on these pairs fell significantly below those on other markets.

The Kraken sell-off started on the same day that Bitcoin dropped to $59,370, its lowest level since July 2, according to CoinGlass data. On Friday, CoinGlass also recorded $1.57 billion in liquidations across the crypto market over 24 hours, and long positions accounted for $1.43 billion of that amount.

Still, the exchange said that every order was processed correctly and that its security teams found no evidence of a breach or compromised accounts.

#5047. Hong Kong pushes to bring crypto custodians, advisers under licensing rules

Hong Kong’s securities regulator says the proposal would strengthen investor protections, with the bill set for its first reading in the Legislative Council on Dec. 2.

Hong Kong has published a bill that would bring crypto custodians, advisers and other virtual asset service providers under mandatory licensing or registration, extending oversight beyond trading platforms.

On Friday, the government officially published its Virtual Asset Services Legislation (Amendment) Bill 2026, proposing to regulate four activities, including virtual asset dealing, advisory, management and custody. Firms carrying out those activities would need to obtain a license from or register with the Securities and Futures Commission (SFC).

The proposed amendments to the Anti-Money Laundering and Counter-Terrorist Financing Ordinance would subject providers to fit-and-proper checks and other regulatory requirements. Banks and stored-value facility licensees supervised by the Hong Kong Monetary Authority would also have to register with the SFC to offer virtual asset services.

SFC chief executive Julia Leung said the proposal would strengthen investor protections and provide clearer paths for responsible innovation. The bill is set for its first reading in the Legislative Council on Dec. 2.

In October, Hong Kong said it would introduce legislation covering those four activities before the end of 2026, following consultations on the proposed framework.

The expanded regime has raised implementation concerns. In January, the Hong Kong Securities & Futures Professionals Association warned that introducing licenses without transitional arrangements could force existing businesses to suspend operations while their applications are reviewed. The association called for a six- to 12-month grace period.

#5046. Another XRP Record Ahead? Ripple CEO Predicts XRP Demand Surge as Inflation-Stricken Markets Seek Alternatives to Fiat

XRP could be on the verge of another major adoption milestone as inflation-hit economies increasingly look for alternatives to traditional currencies. At least that’s according to Brad Garlinghouse.

The Ripple frontman sees growing potential for the cryptocurrency, arguing that demand could rise over time as people in struggling markets seek ways to protect their wealth from weakening fiat currencies. His comments spotlight a potential new driver of XRP adoption and raise fresh questions about how far demand could grow.

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse believes inflation-hit economies could become an increasingly important source of demand for XRP as people seek alternatives to traditional currencies. Speaking during a recent interview with Bloomberg Television at the Singapore FinTech Festival, he explained how persistent inflation and declining purchasing power could encourage more people to explore digital assets.

“XRP is going to draw people’s interest over time, particularly in markets where you have more inflationary dynamics,” he opined.

Garlinghouse argued that the declining value of fiat currencies could make assets with predictable or limited supplies more attractive to investors. To illustrate his point, he cited the U.S. dollar, describing it as one of the world’s best-managed currencies while noting that it had lost approximately half its value over the past decade.

He added that the situation is considerably worse in countries with weaker monetary management, where some national currencies have reportedly lost between 80% and 90% of their value. Such conditions, in his view, could strengthen the appeal of digital assets whose supplies cannot be expanded at the discretion of central banks or governments.

However, scarcity alone is not enough to attract investors. Garlinghouse also highlighted liquidity as an important consideration, noting that people want assets they can easily hold, transfer, and exchange. This combination could work in XRP’s favor as interest in alternative financial assets grows across inflation-hit markets.

His remarks point to a potential source of XRP demand beyond speculative trading and institutional investment. While increased interest does not guarantee sustained buying pressure or higher prices, broader adoption in economies struggling with currency depreciation could ultimately boost XRP’s long-term appeal.

During the interview, Amin also asked Garlinghouse whether he could see Bitcoin eventually rocketing to the $1 million mark, a target previously floated by several prominent figures in the crypto industry.

The Ripple chief was receptive to the possibility, although he admitted that predicting when such a milestone might happen was difficult. His optimism was largely rooted in the declining purchasing power of traditional currencies rather than any specific Bitcoin price model.

Bitcoin has a hard supply cap of 21 million coins, while XRP’s total supply was established at 100 billion tokens. Their fixed supply structures distinguish them from government-issued currencies, which can be expanded through monetary policy.

Nevertheless, scarcity does not automatically translate into higher valuations or make either cryptocurrency immune to inflation. Both Bitcoin and XRP remain highly volatile assets whose prices are influenced by market demand and broader economic conditions.

Bitcoin surged above $64,400 as it extended its weekend recovery following Friday’s sharp drop below $56,000, while Ripple’s XRP also staged a rebound, briefly reaching $1.02, per CoinGecko. However, both cryptocurrencies have since surrendered part of those gains. At the time of writing, BTC had pulled back to around $61,271, while XRP had also retreated to $0.9435, reflecting renewed selling pressure after the brief recovery.

#5045. Three XRP Features Race for Last-Minute 2026 Activation: What Remains

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

In the final stretch of 2026, some XRP Ledger protocol features await mainnet activation, with validator support and amendment voting as the final steps to bring new functionality to the XRPL network.

In light of this, three amendments are racing against the 2026 calendar, with only a few votes left to reach a majority and enter the two-week activation period. These include SingleAssetVault, LendingProtocol and confidential transfers.

All three remain below the required validator approval threshold of over 80% support, making the final weeks of the year increasingly important for their activation prospects.

With about five weeks left in 2026, activation remains possible this year, but additional validator support for each amendment must arrive soon enough and be sustained to complete the required two-week activation window.

The lending protocol allows on-chain fixed-term, uncollateralized loans from pooled funds in a Single Asset Vault. There are two amendments: LendingProtocolV1_1 and the original Lending Protocol amendment.

The focus is on LendingProtocolV1_1, which extends the Lending Protocol and SingleAssetVault amendments with a new closed-ended vault and cash-basis accounting. With the amendment, new loan brokers can only be created against closed-ended vaults.

LendingProtocolV1_1 has reached 70.59% support with 24 out of 34 validators voting yes, according to XRPScan data. It needs 28 votes to reach a majority, or more than 80% of 34 votes, meaning it has just four votes left.

SingleAssetVault creates a structure for aggregating assets from multiple depositors and is intended to be used with the on-chain Lending Protocol. It has gained 73.53% support so far with 25 out of 34 validators voting in favor. Reaching more than 80% of 34 votes requires 28 votes; thus, three votes remain for it to enter the activation window.

#5044. 30K Bitcoin and A LOT of noise — It’s what a Tesla-SpaceX merger really means for crypto

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

“Do I think it would be a good idea? Yes.”

That’s what Elon Musk said about the idea to merge Tesla and SpaceX, back in October 2026.

Since then, however, it would seem that this somewhat fanciful idea has only accelerated. Over the last few weeks alone, both Tesla and SpaceX have constituted special exploratory committees consisting of independent directors.

Will it go through? Probably, yes. After all, despite the CEO of SpaceX and Tesla claiming that he is a “conflicted party,” his companies’ shareholders have often acceded to his wishes. That’s an opinion Walter Isaacson, author of “Elon Musk,” shares too.

Especially after the entrepreneur made his case by stating,

…there’s a lot of merit to combining the companies as we are pretty much in the Super Intelligence singularity.

Needless to say, such a merger will have some legal, regulatory, and financial implications. But what about the crypto sector?

Tesla, Inc., the automotive and clean energy company, holds 11,509 BTC valued at over $747 million right now. Similarly, SpaceX, the aerospace manufacturer, holds 18,712 BTC worth over $1.22 billion.

In simple terms, the merger will simply be a transaction that pools in digital assets owned by both these entities into a single balance sheet. This consolidation would allow the merged entity to hold 30,221 BTC in reserves.

Those are significant numbers. However, here’s where it’s also worth looking at the context and scale and the bigger picture.

Neither Tesla nor SpaceX are crypto companies. They are primarily tech manufacturing companies with valuations of $1.54 trillion and $1.89 trillion, respectively.

Yes, they have crypto holdings, but they aren’t significant enough for each individual entity to suggest that these are more than a passing interest and a diversification opportunity.

To put it in financial terms, for both Tesla and SpaceX, Bitcoin is a non-core balance sheet asset. For them, their BTC holdings are financially immaterial to their core CapEx. As Dr. Andrei Belonogov, Managing Partner at TechAccountingPro pointed out,

The size of treasuries is relatively small compared to the company’s debt maturities and non-cancellable commitments.

This will remain the case if the merger does go through. If and when it happens, the merged companies’ valuation would dwarf the value of its crypto holdings.

What about after, though? This is an important question because more often than not, non-core balance sheet assets go through a period of evaluation and restructuring and sometimes, disposal too to streamline operations and facilitate profitability.

Could this happen to the aforementioned Bitcoin holdings then? Well, that’s always a possibility. However, recent history suggests the odds are low.

Consider the purchase and sale history of both these companies, for instance. The last time Tesla sold any of its Bitcoin holdings was back in 2022. Since then, it has bought additional BTC while also holding on to the stash it already had.

Similarly, SpaceX last sold any of its holdings back in 2024. In the two years since, the aerospace manufacturer has only bought Bitcoin. Specifically, over 10K BTC in May 2026.

Now, it’s up for debate how much input the “conflicted party,” Musk, had in any of these sales or purchases. However, it is safe to assume that, given the consistencies between the two boardrooms so far, that will remain the case after the merger too.

What about later though? Well, MetaWin COO Rory Cartwright told AMBCrypto,

The merger doesn’t necessarily mean a sell-off or any spike in price. What it will do, however, is put a roughly $2bn position into a single company’s balance sheet. Bitcoin can be especially volatile, so that may make some investors seasick

Will the merged entity enjoy the same degree of flexibility Tesla and SpaceX enjoyed individually when it comes to crypto sales and purchases though? According to Kevin Constantine, Senior Associate at Caldwell Law,

Protections could become relevant to cryptocurrency holdings, particularly if management’s ability to purchase, liquidate, or transfer Bitcoin raised concerns about the companies’ valuations or the treatment of shareholders.

The fact that both Tesla and SpaceX refused to sell their Bitcoin in 2026, a year that has been particularly bearish for the crypto-sector, is testament to the confidence they have in this asset. It is also a sign of institutional conviction.

After the merger, the merged entity will have the potential to be the sixth-largest corporate Bitcoin holder in the world. Only behind crypto-native firms or treasury-focused players like MicroStrategy and Metaplanet. All while Bitcoin remains its non-core balance sheet asset.

That, more than anything, will be confirmation of Bitcoin’s value as a corporate treasury asset. That might not sound like much on paper, but that is still VERY bullish news.

What could be even better though? According to Chris Mack, CTO at Quote.Trade,

If Musk came out publicly bullish on Bitcoin and crypto, that would change things. Investors would start paying attention to whether he plans to buy more or make crypto part of the business.

That’s not all either. As it stands, both Tesla and SpaceX have significant investments in scaling AI infrastructure. This is likely to continue after the merger, with more capital and resources allocated to AI compute and energy grids.

In a very indirect way, such investments could have a positive impact on AI-focused crypto tokens like RENDER, TAO, and NEAR, especially in the long run.

At the time of writing, reports seemed to suggest that Tesla and SpaceX are targeting a potential deal announcement by January 2027 or even as early as December 2026.

That’s an accelerated timeline, one that may or may not have anything to do with Democrats’ victory in the midterm elections. Democrats, many of whom are also planning to probe Elon Musk’s corporate ties.

Even outside the political element, though, it probably won’t be all sunshine and roses. After all, this is a merger between a major U.S. defense and Starlink contractor with an automative company with a significant manufacturing and sales footprint in China.

The regulatory and legal questions involved alone will be a nightmare.

Will crypto be an irrelevant factor then? Speaking to AMBCrypto, Constantine claimed,

As it stands, a lot of things are still up in the air. It’s too early to say how Tesla and SpaceX will quell national security concerns while juggling regulatory challenges with this merger. That’s the unpredictable part.

What is not unpredictable is what will happen to their cryptos and the crypto sector at large if this merger goes through. Relatively speaking, that is.

#5043. Ripple USD (RLUSD) Breaks Into Global Top Five Stablecoins as Supply Hits New ATH

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ripple’s dollar-pegged stablecoin, Ripple USD (RLUSD), has now entered the top five stablecoins by market cap, reaching a new all-time high in its circulating supply, a major milestone for the stablecoin less than two years after its launch. RLUSD went live in December 2024.

RLUSD has surpassed World Liberty Financial USD (USD1), Global Dollar (USDG) and PayPal USD (PYUSD) to become the fifth-largest stablecoin in the world, according to data from CoinMarketCap. Only Tether’s USDT, Circle’s USDC, Ethena’s USDe, and Dai (DAI) are ahead of it.

The milestone is proof of the rapid growth RLUSD has experienced in a competitive stablecoin market dominated by established players like Tether’s USDT and Circle’s USDC.

RLUSD’s total circulating supply across the XRP Ledger and Ethereum has reached a new high, with $2.85 billion in circulation, according to Ripple’s stablecoin tracker.

Ethereum commands $2.089 billion of this total figure, while $768 million in supply sits on the XRP Ledger. While Ethereum currently holds the larger share of RLUSD’s supply, XRPL has seen significant activity in RLUSD settlement volumes.

In March 2025, during a Bloomberg TV interview, Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse projected that RLUSD would enter the top five stablecoins; that projection has now been fulfilled.

According to RWA Foundation, Ripple led weekly stablecoin growth, adding $75.4 million in the last seven days. This growth was reflected in RLUSD's market cap, allowing it to surpass World Liberty Financial USD to clinch the fifth spot in global stablecoin rankings.

Former Ripple executive Anthony Welfare highlighted this growth in a recent X post, saying, “RLUSD is the only major stablecoin growing right now.”

Welfare noted that RLUSD supply is up nearly 20% in three weeks, rising from $2.42 billion to $2.9 billion, while USDT has fallen by $5 billion and USDC by $3 billion over the same period.

The XRP Ledger accounts for the majority of RLUSD settlements: in an analogy on X, Welfare noted that XRPL holds about 27% of RLUSD’s supply but processed 57% of its volume last week, amounting to $6.7 billion versus Ethereum, which recorded $5 billion.

#5042. Privacy tokens face a new challenge – Balancing anonymity with oversight

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On the 1st of October 2025, entrepreneur and angel investor Naval Ravikant posted on X that,

Bitcoin is insurance against fiat. Zcash is insurance against Bitcoin.

Neither of the latter two tokens has managed to regain the highs they made back then.

Meanwhile, ZEC reached $1,700. The altcoin’s correction in recent months back below $1,000 has sparked bearish short-term sentiment, even though it was still trading above the previous year’s peak.

Sophisticated on-chain analysis can still narrow down to the 16 possibilities over multiple “hops”, or intermediate payments or asset transfers along a transaction trail.

Monero’s Full Chain Membership Proofs++ (FCMP++) eliminates the need for decoys. It replaces rings by providing zero-knowledge membership proofs across the entire blockchain output set. In other words, it mathematically proves that the spent XMR belongs to the Monero blockchain, making every previous output created part of the decoy set.

A mainnet activation date has not been confirmed for the FCMP++ and Carrot, the addressing protocol and wallet key hierarchy upgrade designed for the FCMP++ network upgrade. AMBCrypto reported that zkSecurity’s audit of FCMP++ was “complete, zero-knowledge, and sound”.

Using zero-knowledge cryptography, private ZEC balances and transactions are tracked in shielded pools. Zcash has five of these shielded pools, as well as its transparent pool. Once funds are inside, the older pools can not be shut down, but can be made spend-only.

The Ironwood (NU6.3) upgrade was launched on the 28th of July, 2026, addressing the Orchard “infinity” bug found in May. It also introduced a new shielded pool and restricted the Orchard pool. Funds exiting Orchard must now pass through Zcash’s turnstile, for supply integrity verification, to enter Ironwood.

The NU7 upgrade implemented in November aims to reduce block processing times by 67%, from 75 seconds to 25 seconds. It also reduced the wallet scanning workload required to perform private payments.

When transactions are shielded in some form, anti-money laundering (AML) tracing is hindered or prevented. This brings strict regulatory restrictions on shielded transactions, as they require visibility in fund flows to prevent illicit financing.

The latter argues that oversight is a necessity to prevent serious crimes and to detect and disrupt illicit activity.

Selective disclosure, such as in Zcash, Midnight, or using Identity Frameworks in Ethereum, can achieve this by sharing transaction details without revealing the entire financial history.

Zero-knowledge compliance is another approach that can prove that a transaction meets certain criteria, without exposing other relevant details.

#5041. BlackRock ETF Dumps 1 Million ETH Worth $1.75B as Ethereum Outflows Accelerate: Is ETH In Trouble?

BlackRock’s Ethereum ETF has lost over 1 million ETH worth $1.75 billion since October as outflows accelerate.

The BlackRock ETF has shed more than 1 million ETH since October 1, recording net outflows of 1,018,839 ETH worth approximately $1.75 billion. The selling continued on Monday, as BlackRock’s iShares Ethereum Trust (ETHA) transferred another batch of coins to Coinbase Prime.

Between 11:52 and 12:02 UTC, four separate transfers moved 42,631 ETH from BlackRock’s deposit addresses to Coinbase Prime, the brokerage desk handling the fund’s trades. The transfers were worth approximately $73 million at Monday’s prices.

Since October 1, #BlackRock has seen net outflows of 1,018,839 $ETH ($1.75B).https://t.co/YfaFJXVOID pic.twitter.com/tfljcmoSsM

— Lookonchain (@lookonchain) November 23, 2026

The outflows have taken a large bite out of the fund. Comparing Lookonchain’s data with BlackRock’s own filings confirms the match to the coin. ETHA now holds less Ethereum than it did at the end of June.

How much has the BlackRock ETF lost so far, and what might this mean for ETH?

ETHA held 3,675,697 ETH on September 30, its quarterly report shows. On November 20, the iShares fund page listed 2,656,857 ETH.

That is a drop of 1,018,840 ETH, or 28%, which lines up with Lookonchain’s count almost exactly. Shares outstanding fell by a similar amount, from 162.4 million to 119.1 million, as investors cashed out.

The timing stings. In the third quarter, the BlackRock ETF bought 1.19 million ETH for $2.71 billion as money poured back in. Nearly all of that has now left. ETHA’s 2,656,857 ETH is below the 2,695,456 it held on June 30.

The fund’s net assets stood at $4.31 billion on November 20. Its net asset value per share has fallen 41% this year.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

We traced the Arkham-labelled addresses in Lookonchain’s screenshot on the Ethereum blockchain. Two BlackRock deposit addresses, 0xC8c5…5b95 and 0x01c2…59E5, sent every transfer to two Coinbase Prime hot wallets.

Since October 1, the two addresses made 84 transfers totalling 1,128,667 ETH. Priced at the hour each one moved, that is about $2.54 billion, or an average of $2,254 per ETH.

The pace picked up in November. The weeks starting November 2 and November 9 each saw more than 200,000 ETH leave, compared with 120,000 to 170,000 a week in October. The biggest single days were October 7, November 10 and November 20, at more than 70,000 ETH each.

The on-chain total is higher than the net drop in holdings because some new money still came in. It also includes Monday’s transfers, which are not yet in the fund’s November 20 figures.

These moves do not mean BlackRock is selling for itself. Coinbase acts as the trust’s agent, and ETHA pays out redemptions either in ETH or in cash raised by selling ETH. When investors leave the fund, the coins go out through Coinbase Prime.

EXPLORE: BitMine’s Estimated $3.6B ETH Loss Overshadows Stock Edge

ETH trades at $1,724, below its 50-day exponential moving average (EMA), an average that gives recent prices more weight, at $2,131, and its 200-day EMA at $2,248. It fell to $1,538 on November 21, just above its June low of $1,506.

Monday’s 42,631 ETH shows the BlackRock ETF redemptions have not stopped. A pause in transfers to Coinbase Prime would be the first sign the selling is easing.

#5040. US lawmakers propose up to 30% rewards for prediction market whistleblowers

Whistleblowers could receive 10% to 30% of monetary sanctions in qualifying prediction market fraud cases exceeding $1 million in penalties.

Two US lawmakers have proposed paying whistleblowers up to 30% of monetary sanctions in qualifying prediction market fraud cases, seeking tips about suspected insider trading.

On Friday, Democratic Representatives Sean Casten and Ted Lieu announced the Policing Insider Trading in Prediction Markets Act of 2026. The bill would establish a Department of Justice program rewarding people whose original information helps resolve enforcement actions.

Under the proposal, whistleblowers would receive 10% to 30% of monetary sanctions from covered federal fraud cases involving prediction market platforms, provided that the sanctions exceed $1 million. The rewards would come from the DOJ’s Assets Forfeiture Fund, with the Attorney General determining payouts based on the significance of information and assistance.

The bill would permit anonymous submissions through lawyers and prohibit employer retaliation against whistleblowers.

The lawmakers cited concerns about government insiders profiting from bets on military operations and presidential speeches, including on platforms like Kalshi and Polymarket.

The latest proposal follows Senate passage of the DEATH BETS Act on Thursday, which would prohibit federally regulated prediction markets from offering contracts tied to war, assassination, terrorism and death.

In March, lawmakers introduced separate legislation to prevent officials from using nonpublic information to trade prediction market contracts. In May, House lawmakers sought records from Kalshi and Polymarket over suspected insider trading linked to military actions.

#5039. Bitcoin's (BTC) Worst Is Behind After $55,000 Low, 4-Year Cycle Theory Suggests

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Bitcoin (BTC) fell as low as $55,081 on Nov. 21, its lowest price since September 2024, according to TradingView data. The cryptocurrency then bounced back and was trading at $60,583 on Sunday, after it opened November at $77,425.

Bitcoin has lost 21.6% so far in November 2026, according to CryptoRank. The last time Bitcoin had a worse month was June 2022, when the cryptocurrency lost 37.9%, according to the same data.

The four-year cycle theory ties Bitcoin's market cycles to halvings, and its supporters point to 2014, 2018 and 2022 as the years when each bear market went through its final sell-off. In this context, 2026 is the next year in that row, and the next Bitcoin halving is expected in April 2028. The previous three halvings took place in July 2016, May 2020 and April 2024.

Bitcoin set its record above $1,100 in late 2013. In February 2014, the Japanese exchange Mt. Gox, which handled most Bitcoin trading in 2013, filed for bankruptcy after it lost about 850,000 BTC. In November 2014, Bitcoin gained 11.6%, according to CryptoRank, but December of that year brought a 15.3% drop, and the bottom below $200 came in January 2015.

In December 2017, Bitcoin reached a record near $20,000 on the back of the ICO boom, and during 2018, the ICO market dried up. In November 2018, the crypto market crashed after the Bitcoin Cash hard fork and the so-called hash war between the two camps of miners, and Bitcoin lost 36.4% that month. The cryptocurrency then lost another 6.4% in December 2018 and set its cycle low at about $3,200 in the middle of that month.

The 2021 record near $69,000 came in November 2021. In May 2022, the Terra ecosystem collapsed, and in July of that year, Celsius and Three Arrows Capital filed for bankruptcy. In November of that year, the FTX exchange went bankrupt. Bitcoin lost 16.1% in November 2022 and set its bear market bottom at around $15,500 on Nov. 21, 2022, four years to the day before the latest low. December of that year brought a 3.7% loss, and in January 2023, Bitcoin gained 39.9%.

In the current cycle, Bitcoin set its record above $126,000 in October 2025. In 2026, Bitcoin has lost value in six of the 11 months so far, according to CryptoRank. On Nov. 20, the cryptocurrency fell below $60,000 for the first time since July, and the decline continued the next day.

The latest drop comes amid a broad sell-off on Wall Street, where technology and AI stocks came under pressure, and amid expectations of further interest-rate hikes by the Federal Reserve.

#5038. ‘Price exposure, not the share itself’ — What do tokenized stock holders actually get?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Crypto exchanges spent the past week rolling out tokenized U.S stocks to buyers around the world. On an unrelated note, Nvidia slid by 30% from its ATH in a month following Jensen Huang’s departure. All while AMD rose — an early sign of decoupling.

One story is about tokenization, while the other is about AI stocks. However, both skirt around the same topic: a growing class of investors being handed exposure they don’t fully understand by markets that do not care to explain it.

For most people outside the U.S, buying an Apple or Nvidia stock is hard. It is either expensive, tedious, or simply unavailable. Pritam Dutta, Founder and CEO of Zoth, explained the same when he spoke to AMBCrypto, stating,

There’s a lot of excitement around tokenized stocks right now. Most people outside the U.S. still can’t easily buy shares in Apple, Nvidia and the rest of the Magnificent Seven. Tokenization changes that.

Undoubtedly, for a buyer in Nigeria or the Philippines, a tokenized stock could be their first realistic route into U.S markets. But Dutta’s next point is the one that should make an investor sit up and take notice.

A lot of what’s launching gives you price exposure, not the share itself. You don’t get voting rights, and dividends depend on the platform’s terms… Too few platforms are being upfront about it.

In a nutshell, investors are not buying Apple. They are buying a token that tracks Apple’s price, issued by a platform whose terms determine what else you get, subject to its discretion.

Dutta, then, reduced the entire conversation to three questions investors must ask themselves before running towards such stocks.

Who holds the real share? What happens to my dividends? What happens if the platform fails?

These are not clever questions. These are the questions you should ask before buying anything that is marketed as “tokenized.” Unfortunately, the fact that they need to be asked in the first place is a sure-shot sign of how little the platforms are disclosing.

In today’s age, the access is real, but the understanding might not be. And, that gap is the hardest on buyers with the least room to make an error.

Nvidia fell this week while AMD rose. The easy explanation is Jensen Huang’s exit. However, Ian Weisberger, CEO of CoinRoutes, believes that there is a larger cause. He told AMBCrypto,

The real problem is that Nvidia’s chips are becoming a commodity.

For years, Nvidia and the AI boom were not seen as two separate entities, simply because of the former’s chips. That is also how the company grew to roughly 8% of the S&P 500.

This week, though the market began pulling the two apart — and within the AI sector — the separation was underway long before anyone realized.

Anyone who has built on both stacks knows the gap is closing. As the chips become interchangeable, buyers will shop on price, and the profits will move to the companies building products on top of them.

That is the concentration problem in one paragraph. When a single stock is 8% of the AI sector, millions of retail investors own a large position in it through their index funds. That is exposure they never signed up to be a part of.

The AI boom is not ending, but the era of one stock carrying the index might be.

Both stories have the same problem — the people most likely to be hurt are unfortunately the least equipped to protect themselves. Here’s why.

Firstly, tokenized stocks are best for an overseas buyer for whom a U.S brokerage account is out of reach. Unfortunately, that buyer is also the first to suffer losses if their platform of choice fails.

Secondly, when it comes to artificial intelligence, the cracks are less noticeable. Most overseas retail investors own Nvidia through an index fund that was marketed to them as “diversified.” This causes the investor with the least information to hold one of the most important market bets.

As Nicolai Sondergaard, research analyst at Nansen, told AMBCrypto:

AI and crypto draw on the same pool of risk capital, so when Nvidia sells off, the money goes to cash, not into Bitcoin.

At the time of Sondergaard’s analysis, Nvidia was down about 32% from its 6th October high, Bitcoin [BTC] was 26% below its September peak, and gold was 17% off its August high. Meanwhile, the dollar was at a six-month high, and real yields were up more than 0.2 percentage points in two months.

So, the investor who thought they were spread across AI and crypto was actually holding double the risk. And in the worst case, they must have not figured it out before it was too late.

The same story can be crafted across different markets — the sophisticated investor can take on the risk, while the overseas investor cannot. Regardless, the product holds no difference when marketing to them.

The demand is real. The question is whether people are being sold what they think they’re buying.

The question at the end of it all is — were buyers sold a dream that was impossible to achieve? Looking further, was the promised portfolio diversification ever there at all?

In the end, the retail investor’s blind spot is not ignorance. It is the industry that keeps expanding faster than investors understand what’s going on, causing them to buy the narrative and allure. Not the reality.

Cryptocurrencies are of particular interest to Saman, with much of her writing centered around understanding how ideas like Momentum and Greater Fool theories apply to altcoins, specifically, memecoins.

#5037. Investor Bill Miller Says Bitcoin Is Easier to Value than AI

Famous Bitcoin (BTC) advocate and investor Bill Miller IV said that it is much easier to value the world's largest cryptocurrency than AI investments, even though Bitcoin does not produce any cash flow.

He said that this has to do with the expectations investors have when it comes to these two assets, specifically regarding their capacity to deliver future profits.

In a recent X post, Miller, who is the chairman and chief investment officer of Miller Value Partners, broke down why he thinks that it's easier to make an investment case for Bitcoin than for AI companies.

"Bitcoin and AI owners have different expectations. The owners of AI expect companies will one day produce free cash flow. Bitcoin owners never did or will expect this," Miller said.

And the end result is that "the valuation case is still much easier for Bitcoin than AI."

Simply put, when investors buy stocks in AI companies, there is an expectation that at some point these companies will deliver big profits and free cash flow.

Free cash flow is the money a company produces from its operations after capital expenditures. This is a significant metric for understanding the value of a company.

However, when it comes to AI companies, it can be difficult to assign a value because investors need to try to determine what kind of revenue these companies will generate, how much they will invest in infrastructure, and when these investments will start to deliver sufficient returns.

Miller has made it a point to push back against the argument that Bitcoin does not have value because it doesn't produce cash flow.

In a commentary from June 30, Miller explained that the traditional way of looking at intrinsic value, which revolves around future cash flows, doesn't really capture the potential of Bitcoin as a decentralized monetary network.

Instead, Miller said that Bitcoin's value could be determined by comparing its potential role within the global financial system to that of gold.

In his view, Bitcoin could theoretically reach about $1.4 million per coin if it achieved a valuation similar to gold's. At least, that's what he calculated at the time.

#5036. Rethinking crypto risk — Here’s why ONE factor doesn’t tell the whole story

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Investors have a habit of gauging crypto by a single risk framework. However, currently, that framework no longer captures how the market works.

Bitcoin’s [BTC] daily price fluctuations are about three times wider than the S&P 500’s, with far more extreme moves in both directions. However, the realized volatility of Bitcoin has declined over time to the level of individual large-cap U.S stocks.

So, investors should be aware of three things — where returns come from, how they fit inside a portfolio, and how crypto has grown far beyond Bitcoin.

The standard risk tools like the standard deviation or the Sharpe ratio don’t care how much money you make or lose. That leaves a blind spot for Bitcoin as most of its long-term gains come from just a handful of trading days.

Bitcoin has averaged 60% per year over the last three years. If you take away its ten best days, that number drops to 7%. If you take away its ten worst days, the number jumps to 197%. That’s because big drops are often followed by big rebounds, so investors who try to avoid bad days can easily miss the best ones.

Momentum counts as well. Bitcoin’s gains tend to pile on top of each other, and Bitcoin runs far better after months of winning than months of losing. During strong rallies, the Sortino ratio can rise well above normal risk scores. Mostly because it only measures returns against downside volatility, not upside volatility.

Notably, Bitcoin has dropped by 50% or more seven times since 2011. But when you include the volatility of starting, Bitcoin’s falls look more like stocks and bonds.

In fact, even “low risk” bonds have been falling for a while in recent years.

While big price moves can be alarming on their own, portfolio managers care most about how an asset behaves when put together with the other assets they own. Changes in stocks, bonds, and the private market are mostly driven by common economic factors. However, these factors explain the changes in Bitcoin much less.

Cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin, have historically shown low correlation with traditional measures than with each other. This is the case, even though correlations can increase during periods of market stress.

Large organizations have taken this into account too. For instance, J.P. Morgan Asset Management, an organization with $5 trillion in assets, introduced Bitcoin into its 2027 outlook, estimating a 10% per year return in the coming ten years. This correlates by only 33% with major U.S. equities, even less than international equities, corporate bonds, and hedge funds, even when there is 55% volatility.

This provides a unique combination of both high expected returns and little correlation with core equities. History proves that including a small portion of Bitcoin in an average institutional portfolio can increase returns and efficiency without increasing overall risk or maximum losses.

Notably, Mike Belshe, CEO of BitGo, recently pointed out JPMorgan’s changing perspective on crypto. He said,

Let that sink in. The world’s largest bank, long crypto-skeptical, is now publicly supporting crypto market structure legislation. This is a different ballgame.

Another mistake is treating crypto as a single Bitcoin bet. The modern landscape of crypto includes smart contract platforms, decentralized finance, consumer applications, and artificial intelligence. Each having its own unique users and economics.

Over the last three years, Bitcoin has accounted for just about 50% of volatility in returns on all major crypto sectors, while correlations among sectors have decreased.

Out of the top 10 biggest non-stablecoin crypto assets by market cap, six different tokens have been leading returns on a quarter-by-quarter basis over the last two years. Bitcoin has never led once.

None of this implies that crypto is risk-free though. Especially since a rally could unwind quickly. On the contrary, risk is not something that can be boiled down to one simple figure.

A uniform know-how about crypto implies understanding of multiple factors — volatility of the asset, distribution of returns, correlation with the rest of a portfolio, and the performance of various sectors separately.

Therefore, in reality, instead of trying to overlook any price fluctuations, it is important to view them with a proper perspective.

Rather than discussing the volatility of crypto per se, one should think of what kind of risks it introduces, what risks from one’s portfolio it offsets, and what additional sources of profit appear outside of Bitcoin.

Despite oversold conditions, DOGE's technical outlook remains bearish. Holding $0.054 could trigger a relief rally toward $0.06, but sustained recovery requires stronger buying volume.

Until Dogecoin decisively reclaims $0.06, downside risks remain elevated, with $0.05 emerging as the next critical support.

DUBAI, UAE, November 18, 2026 – WSDT is introducing its prop trading platform for cryptocurrency perpetual futures, gold and tokenized US stocks. Traders can access these markets through a single simulated account, with one-step evaluations starting at a one-time fee of $19 for a $1,500 account.

The platform combines crypto perpetual futures, including Bitcoin and Ethereum, with xStocks instruments tracking companies such as Apple, NVIDIA and Tesla, alongside stock-index ETFs. Perpetual futures are contracts without an expiry date; xStocks track the prices of underlying shares or ETFs. On WSDT, these instruments are traded in a simulated environment, without ownership of the underlying shares or tokens.

Positions across the markets share the same account balance and count toward the same evaluation. Traders can work across crypto and equity-linked instruments in one terminal while managing a single set of account limits.

A WSDT challenge tests trading performance against a 10% profit target, a 4% daily loss limit and a 6% maximum drawdown fixed against the starting account size. Traders must trade on at least four separate days, with no deadline to complete the evaluation. Account sizes range from $1,500 to $200,000, with fees varying by size.

Traders who pass move to a funded-stage account, which is also simulated and retains the loss limits. They receive 80% of eligible profits under WSDT’s Provider Service Agreement. The evaluation fee pays for access to the challenge; it is not a trading deposit.

Registered users can open a free 14-day demo with a $100,000 simulated balance, without providing a payment card or making a deposit. The demo uses the same terminal as challenges, allowing traders to practice before entering an evaluation. Demo results do not qualify for payouts.

WSDT also runs an ongoing weekly competition with free entry. Every participant starts with a $240,000 simulated balance, and the top three finishers receive challenge accounts. A new round opens each Monday at noon New York time. Winners must pass their awarded challenge before progressing to the funded stage.

Registration, account options and full challenge rules are available on the WSDT website.

WSDT provides trader evaluation and educational services across crypto, gold and tokenized equity instruments. Built by TEFS, a prop trading firm established in 2017, it is operated by TEFS Marketing Management L.L.C. in Dubai, UAE. All WSDT trading accounts are simulated.

#5033. ECB seeks industry partners for tokenized deposits, securities experiments

Industry groups have until Dec. 11 to apply for the ECB’s first experimental exercises under its Appia initiative, with later submissions reviewed on a rolling basis.

The European Central Bank (ECB) is seeking financial market participants to help explore tokenized deposits and securities as part of its efforts to build an integrated European financial ecosystem using blockchain technology.

On Friday, the ECB invited industry groups to participate in analytical work and experiments under its Appia initiative, identifying three priority areas, including tokenized deposits and related payments, tokenized securities and shared network governance.

According to the ECB, interested participants must apply as part of a collective initiative comprising independent legal entities rather than individually. Groups can apply for one or more workstreams, with proposals evaluated based on factors including expertise, practical experience, technical readiness and expected contributions.

Applications for the first experimental exercises are due by Dec. 11, while later submissions will be assessed on a rolling basis.

The initiative builds on the Eurosystem’s broader Appia roadmap, which aims to develop a unified European tokenized financial ecosystem anchored in central bank money. The ECB plans to publish a blueprint outlining findings and a proposed framework in 2028.

The invitation comes as ECB President Christine Lagarde warned that Europe risks missing an opportunity to establish an integrated market for tokenized securities if incompatible standards emerge across platforms.

Lagarde urged policymakers to establish common rules to prevent fragmentation as blockchain-based financial infrastructure develops across the region.

#5032. $50K next for Bitcoin? Examining whether this will be its ‘worst’ Q4 since 2018

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Is Bitcoin’s worst Q4 on record getting closer?

The last 24 hours may have served as a reality check for investors. Bitcoin broke below the $62,000-level for the first time since August. However, the sharp decline may not be an isolated move. The sell-off followed the release of the latest FOMC minutes, adding to concerns over the Fed’s next policy move.

So, with Q4 already shaping up to be one of Bitcoin’s weakest quarters, could the worst be yet to come?

The latest FOMC minutes could be an early warning sign for Bitcoin’s Q4 outlook.

According to the minutes, almost all Fed officials now expect more rate hikes to bring inflation back to the 2% target. Some even suggested that a 50-basis-point hike could be on the table if inflation data worsens. In this context, Bitcoin’s latest correction suggests that investors may be taking a bearish view of the report. It is set to lay the tone for the next Fed meeting, scheduled for 10th December.

In short, the Fed maintained interest rates at 3.75%-4% in October, but the minutes have now strengthened expectations of another December hike. Here, it’s worth pointing out that market expectations already reflect this shift.

According to CME data, for instance, the odds of a rate hike have reached 100%. Of this, 92.7% of traders expect a 25-basis-point hike, taking rates to 4%-4.25%. The remaining 7.3% expect a bigger, 50-basis-point hike to 4.25%-4.5%.

Meanwhile, the pressure is also showing up in traditional markets.

The Dollar Index rose to 103.82 on 18th November, its highest level since April 2025. As a result, yields on U.S 10-year Treasuries rose above 5.1%. Also, it seems increasingly likely that if this continues, traditional assets will become more appealing than riskier assets. This is likely due to the hawkish tone of the FOMC minutes which said,

Almost all participants judged that further action to raise the target range for the federal funds rate would likely be appropriate.

Bitcoin’s [BTC] Q4 decline is getting closer to its 2018 record now.

As the chart below highlights, Bitcoin is down 24.45% in Q4 2026, with November’s 18.48% decline accelerating the correction. This puts BTC on track for one of its weakest fourth quarters on record. The closest historical comparison is Q4 2018, when Bitcoin fell by 43.5% during the bear market.

Sure, the prevailing decline is still 19.05% short of that record. However, this is where the latest FOMC minutes come into play. The release has added to concerns across the crypto market, with traders now weighing the possibility of further rate hikes.

If these expectations continue to rise, Bitcoin could face more selling pressure, pushing its Q4 decline closer to the 2018 record.

However, the bigger question is whether this selling pressure can continue or not.

With rate hikes now almost fully priced in, the focus will shift to whether Bitcoin has enough demand to absorb further selling. This would make on-chain activity and institutional flows key indicators to watch. At the time of writing though, the signals were not encouraging.

In fact, according to a recent Wintermute report,

With flows negative and the 200-week lost, December Fed hike is priced at about 92% after core CPI printed 0.3% for a third month, and spot Bitcoin ETFs are $1.9B net negative for 2026. We expect pressure into the $50s, with Saturday’s $62K low the first level and the $60K put cluster on Deribit the next magnet.

To understand how the macro environment could affect Bitcoin’s Q4 performance, the focus should also be on key on-chain data.

According to Glassnode’s latest report, Bitcoin has been facing strong selling pressure. Investors are booking losses at levels last seen near major market bottoms in 2018, 2020 and 2022. However, the $62K-66K range is acting as a key support zone, with nearly 20% of short-term holder supply sitting in this range.

That’s not all though. According to CryptoQuant, Bitcoin’s 30-day Net Realized Profit/Loss dropped to -$8.4 billion recently, its lowest level since the summer. Selling has accelerated too, with investors realizing $5.4 billion in losses between 15th-18th November alone. Even long-term holders have been selling at a loss, with their SOPR falling to 0.73 — its lowest level since August.

The key takeaway? The ongoing losses are still lower than those seen during the February (30 billion) and June (18.4 billion) bottoms. This means that Bitcoin may not have reached full capitulation “yet.”

So, if Bitcoin falls below $60K, this could lead to liquidations of nearly 20% of the short-term supply.

Considering the hawkish Fed, bearish technicals, and negative on-chain signals, a further downtrend cannot be ruled out. In fact, Wintermute now anticipates a further decline, with $50K now becoming a possible downside target for BTC.

The comparison with gold adds another layer to the picture.

From a technical perspective, Bitcoin has been flashing signs of some positive momentum relative to gold. On 18th November, the Bitcoin-to-Gold rate rose above its 200-day simple moving average to 17.13 ounces of gold for one Bitcoin. However, the broader trend might tell us a different story.

Despite this recovery, however, the BTC/XAU ratio was still down nearly 16% in Q4 at press time.

In other words, while Bitcoin has bounced against gold in the short term, it continues to underperform over the quarter. So, for long-term holders, gold still has the upper hand, while Bitcoin needs a stronger recovery to close the performance gap.

This finding also brings the cautious FOMC minutes back into focus.

Bitcoin now needs stronger spot demand, ETF inflows and a recovery in the BTC/XAU ratio to limit further losses. However, ongoing capitulation raises concerns about whether buyers can absorb the pressure. With the next FOMC meeting adding to market uncertainty, Wintermute’s $50K downside target will be in play.

Technically, that would pull Bitcoin nearly 40% lower in Q4. If selling pressure continues, the possibility of Bitcoin’s Q4 correction surpassing 2018’s record cannot be ruled out.

Bitcoin’s Q4 decline hit 24.45%, with weak on-chain data, ETF outflows, and hawkish Fed expectations adding to selling pressure.

A drop to $50K could push Bitcoin’s Q4 losses close to 40%, putting 2018’s record within reach.

#5031. Top Weekly Crypto News: Bitcoin Crashes to $55,000, Ripple's RLUSD Enters Top Five Stablecoins, Shiba Inu Sees 684 Billion SHIB Move

This past week resulted in one of the biggest correction of 2026. Bitcoin drop to a two-year low. Billions of positions were liquidated across crypto exchanges on November 22.

Ripple has achieved an important milestone, with RLUSD reaching top five stablecoins by market cap.

On the contrary, XRP dropped below $1.00, a significant psychological level that XRP held since November 2024.

Bitcoin trading volume has surged to its highest level since February as the world’s largest cryptocurrency stages a dramatic recovery from two-year lows, triggering hundreds of millions of dollars in liquidations.

The massive spike in trading volume comes after Bitcoin tanked to two-year lows on Nov. 21 before bouncing back sharply to regain ground toward the psychologically crucial $60,000 level.

The massive spike in trading volume comes in the wake of one of the biggest sell-offs in BTC this year. On Nov. 21, the world’s largest cryptocurrency dropped as low as around $55,100, a level last seen in September 2024.

The sharp decline in Bitcoin prices came as risk assets tumbled amid rising concerns over the U.S. stock market and the artificial intelligence (AI) sector, which weighed on investor sentiment.

Ripple’s dollar-pegged stablecoin, RLUSD, has now entered the top five stablecoins by market cap, reaching a new all-time high in its circulating supply, a major milestone for the stablecoin less than two years after its launch. RLUSD went live in December 2024.

RLUSD has surpassed World Liberty Financial USD (USD1), Global Dollar (USDG) and PayPal USD (PYUSD) to become the fifth-largest stablecoin in the world. Only Tether’s USDT, Circle’s USDC, Ethena’s USDe, and Dai (DAI) are ahead of it.

RLUSD’s total circulating supply across the XRP Ledger and Ethereum has reached a new high, with $2.85 billion in circulation, according to Ripple’s stablecoin tracker.

Shiba Inu's exchange activity keeps on bringing more to the table. This time, 372.39 billion tokens flowed into exchanges.

Inflows recorded an increase of 0.36% over 24 hours, which once again raises the chances of a surge in market pressure, data from CryptoQuant suggests.

While the inflow number to exchanges is quite high, the total netflow doesn't hit the value of 100 billion, suggesting that the amount of inflows after withdrawals doesn't really disrupt the overall picture for Shiba Inu.

Outflows accounted for 280.82 billion SHIB, leaving a net inflow of 91.57 billion. About three quarters of the deposits were offset by withdrawals.

U.S. and Canadian authorities have launched a massive cross-border crackdown on cryptocurrency fraud, bringing together the U.S.

Secret Service, Canadian law enforcement agencies and financial regulators to dismantle scam networks

According to the Canadian securities regulator, the operation involves investigators, securities specialists and financial crime experts from both countries.

The initiative will use advanced technology and enhanced investigative capabilities to identify fraudulent activity and potential victims in near real time.

#5030. MEXC’s Hacken-Audited November 2026 Proof of Reserves Shows Reserve Ratios Above 100% Across Major Assets

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Mutsamudu, Comoros, November 19, 2026 – MEXC, a pioneer in 0-fee digital asset trading, has released its November 2026 Proof of Reserves (PoR) report, independently audited by Hacken. The report shows MEXC’s solid reserve position, with user assets in every major asset backed by reserves above 1:1, demonstrating MEXC’s ongoing commitment to safeguarding user assets.

Spanning Proof of Liabilities, Proof of Ownership, Reserves Calculation, and Merkle Tree verification, Hacken’s November 2026 audit confirms substantial over-collateralisation across core holdings, standing at 318% for BTC, 122% for USDT, 112% for ETH, and 110% for USDC. Guided by its user-first philosophy, MEXC continues to enhance transparency to protect user assets and build trust. Since introducing Proof of Reserves in February 2023, MEXC has kept its wallet addresses and reserve data public, and its Merkle Tree system lets users verify at any time that their balances are included, without exposing other users’ data.   Reserves provide an extra layer of user protection. MEXC also manages user assets across cold and hot wallets under multi-signature authorization and layered risk controls, and maintains two dedicated funds. The Guardian Fund is set aside for major security events such as exploits and targeted attacks. It holds both USDT and BTC, with BTC added in May and September this year, with plans to reach $500 million within two years. The Futures Insurance Fund absorbs losses exceeding a user’s margin following liquidation, acting as a vital capital buffer during extreme market volatility. #5029. Sui crypto’s Hashi launch boosts Bitcoin DeFi – But why are traders cautious?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Sui [SUI] crypto token is making headlines for various reasons, with the most important being Hashi’s launch on the Sui mainnet, an attempt to make Bitcoin [BTC] more useful in decentralized finance (DeFi).

Earlier, Bitcoin holders had limited options when it comes to earning returns or accessing liquidity without selling their BTC. However, now with Hashi, there’s an option that allows users to use their Bitcoin as collateral for loans and other financial products while retaining their exposure to BTC.

The process works through a token called hBTC. When a user deposits Bitcoin into Hashi, the protocol mints an equivalent amount of hBTC on the Sui blockchain at a 1:1 ratio.

To make the process even more seamless, Hashi uses Sui validators and a 2-of-2 multisignature setup to prevent any single entity from moving deposited Bitcoin independently. It also requires six Bitcoin network confirmations before issuing hBTC.

Its first major use case is lending through Fluid, where users can deposit hBTC as collateral and borrow USDC without selling their BTC. However, falling Bitcoin prices could trigger collateral liquidations.

Remarking on the same, Michaël van de Poppe, CIO & Founder at MN Fund, said,

It’s one of the reasons why $SUI is my largest position in my Altcoin portfolio.

Besides this, the Sui crypto token has also made waves in the ETF ecosystem, wherein Grayscale’s Sui Staking ETF attracted $5.33 million in inflows during the week ending 20th November. Even as U.S. spot crypto ETFs collectively recorded $1.51 billion in net outflows.

In fact, its 19th November inflow of more than $5.43 million was reportedly its strongest single-day performance since its February launch.

That said, Sui is also showing growth in its stablecoin ecosystem as its stablecoin supply increased by 7.36% to approximately $584.26 million.

In fact, according to a previous report by AMBCrypto, Sui’s stablecoin market cap outperformed the broader crypto stablecoin market, which contracted by around 5%.

All these coincided with SUI trading at $0.6589 at press time, after a hike of 3.4% in the past 24 hours. Yet, despite the price hike, Open Interest has steadily declined to approximately $350-$400 million by late November.

This indicates that traders are closing their positions in SUI derivatives, pointing to a decline in speculative activity and leveraged exposure.

#5028. Robert Kiyosaki Reveals Why He Is Buying Bitcoin and Ethereum

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Renowned financial guru and Rich Dad Poor Dad author Robert Kiyosaki has offered compelling insight into the reasons behind his buying spree of Bitcoin (BTC) and Ethereum (ETH) despite the current crypto market crash.

Kiyosaki, who is an esteemed investor, predicts that a financial crisis could be on the horizon, and it might be even worse than the 2008 market crash. He believes that crypto assets and other hard assets are an effective hedge against the repercussions of government-induced overborrowing and money printing.

The financial guru's latest X post explains his rationale behind investing in Bitcoin, Ethereum, gold, silver, real estate, and oil instead of traditional financial assets.

"Why I am buying real (no ETFs) gold, silver, Bitcoin, and Ethereum," Kiyosaki noted in his latest post.

He attributed his views to the Federal Reserve, the U.S. Treasury and the colossal debt obligations of the U.S.

According to Kiyosaki, the government's inability to curb spending will lead to massive money printing in the future. This might erode the purchasing power of the U.S. dollar.

"The BIG PRINT is about to begin," Kiyosaki warned.

This is the core reason for his investments in Bitcoin and Ethereum. He believes that these assets will rise in value when the government is forced to aggressively print money in response to escalating financial issues.

Moreover, he believes that holding these crypto assets directly would be better than investing in exchange-traded funds (ETFs). However, he did not offer a reason for his opposition to ETFs in his post.

Furthermore, Kiyosaki stated that the next financial crisis could be much bigger than the global financial crisis of 2008.

"This crash we are just now sliding into is much bigger than 2008. Possibly a global depression," he commented.

He also mentioned that his gold, silver, Bitcoin and Ethereum holdings could make him one of the ultra-rich if his predictions come true.

However, Kiyosaki mentioned some figures that were not entirely accurate. For instance, he stated that the U.S. national debt stood at $49 trillion. But according to Treasury data, the national debt was about $40.7 trillion in November 2026. Also, his claim that the U.S. is printing $1 trillion every 90 days conflates government debt with money printing.

Although Kiyosaki is a strong believer in Bitcoin, he recently advised investors not to buy Bitcoin immediately amid the digital asset's current price slump.

On Nov. 20, the Rich Dad Poor Dad author cautioned against catching a 'falling knife.'

"Never grab a falling knife." This means that investors should wait for the market crash to end before buying.

"I started buying bitcoin around $20k after the market crashed last time," Kiyosaki recalled.

"Will wait for Bitcoin bottom again," he added.

#5027. OmenX announces $2 million in funding from Informed Ventures and launches on-demand 30-second Bitcoin predictions

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Toronto, November 19, 2026 – OmenX, a Base-native leveraged prediction market platform, today announced $2 million in funding from Informed Ventures alongside the launch of its on-demand, 30-second Bitcoin price prediction product.

The new product lets users predict whether Bitcoin’s price will rise or fall over the next 30 seconds, with each trade starting its own prediction window. A position opened at 10:00:12 runs until 10:00:42. Users can act when their view forms, with a full 30-second horizon, rather than adapting to the remaining time in a shared market round.

Featuring zero-slippage execution and rapid settlement, the product extends the familiar five- and fifteen-minute crypto prediction format to a shorter, individually timed experience. It adds to OmenX’s intraday offering, which spans prediction horizons from 30 seconds to 24 hours, depending on the market.

Beyond crypto, OmenX offers directional prediction markets linked to more than 100 global assets, including U.S. equities, futures, ETFs and indices. Its event markets also cover live sports and esports, with sports coverage spanning major football leagues, basketball, American football, ice hockey, baseball and UFC.

Leverage has been central to OmenX’s platform from the outset, allowing traders to amplify their exposure on supported event markets. The latest launch builds on that foundation while advancing a broader goal: making prediction markets a practical financial tool for retail users.

OmenX organizes the trading decision around a defined question: will an asset’s price be higher or lower at the end of a specified period? Users can express a directional view without purchasing the underlying asset, targeting an exact closing price or constructing an options position that requires decisions about strikes, implied volatility and time decay.

“Leverage has been part of OmenX from the start, but our ambition goes beyond adding leverage to prediction markets,” said James, founder and CEO of OmenX. “We want to make global markets easier to participate in. A trader should be able to take a view on Bitcoin, a stock or a sporting event and express it through a clear contract and a timeframe that fits their judgment.”

The platform’s emphasis on short, recurring markets provides faster feedback on trading decisions and can reduce the time capital remains committed to individual outcomes. OmenX plans to expand its coverage of traditional and crypto assets while deepening the trading experience across supported markets.

OmenX is a Base-native leveraged prediction market platform covering crypto, global financial assets, live sports and esports. Through defined outcomes, short settlement cycles and leverage on supported markets, OmenX is building a more accessible way to trade market views.

Learn more at omenx.com. Media contact James Zhang

#5025. Chainflip confirms $12M hack, offers attacker 10% bounty

Chainflip offered the attacker a 10% bounty to return 90% of the stolen funds by Nov. 23, promising to treat the return as a white-hat recovery and withdraw law enforcement complaints.

Cross-chain swap protocol Chainflip confirmed that an attacker stole about $12 million in Bitcoin, Ether and stablecoins from its vaults on Saturday, expanding the initially reported $4.86 million loss.

In a statement addressed to the attacker, Chainflip Labs said 122.86 Bitcoin (BTC), 520.22 Ether (ETH), 3.25 million USDC and 758,141 USDT were stolen in the Saturday incident.

The company offered the attacker a 10% bounty to return the remaining 90% of stolen assets by 18:38 UTC on Nov. 23. Chainflip said it would treat the return as a white-hat recovery and withdraw its reports and complaints to law enforcement if the funds were returned before the deadline.

According to CoinGecko data, Chainflip’s FLIP token plunged 42.9% over the past 24 hours.

Chainflip said it had traced preparations for the attack to Tornado Cash funding on Nov. 11 and a validator account registered on Friday.

The company said it had identified the mechanism behind the cryptographic key exposure and taken steps to prevent similar attacks. It also said it obtained identifying information linked to the attacker that could assist law enforcement.

“We hold identifying information on the validator and servers used, your addresses are flagged across every major analytics firm, and every Tornado Cash withdrawal is being watched,” Chainflip said.

#5024. Former Mt. Gox CEO Warns Pump.fun Users Their Private Keys Are at Risk

Former Mt. Gox CEO Mark Karpelès has strongly warned users of Solana-based memecoin launchpad Pump.fun about potential major security risks associated with the new wallet migration process, which could put users' private keys at great risk.

Karpelès’ warning comes as Pump.fun has set a Nov. 30 deadline to stop allowing external wallet logins to its website. The platform is moving to email and social logins, which will enable users to access their accounts via Google, Apple and GitHub.

But to maintain their current accounts, Pump.fun is asking users to import their private keys. This is what Karpelès thinks could compromise the security of their crypto assets.

In a recent X post, Karpelès said he studied Pump.fun's JavaScript code to understand the nature of the new migration process.

The analysis has raised questions about the security of users’ private keys, despite the platform's claims.

“At best, it's a misnomer to refer to it as 'end-to-end encryption,'” said Karpelès.

According to Karpelès, users' private keys are first encrypted in their browsers before being sent through Pump.fun’s servers to the platform’s wallet infrastructure provider, Privy.

Also, Pump.fun's backend provides the public key used for encryption during migration. Users’ browsers have no way to check whether the public key is authentic, according to Karpelès.

The possibility of a security breach involving Privy’s infrastructure is another major concern.

“Your money also now relies on Privy never getting hacked,” Karpelès warned.

One of the most important aspects of Karpelès' warning is the need for migration in the first place.

He said Pump.fun could use signature-based authentication without forcing users to expose their private keys.

Pump.fun already uses a "Sign in with Solana" message to prove ownership of a wallet, according to Karpelès.

So theoretically, users can prove ownership of their wallets and execute transactions without having to import their private credentials into a new system.
