#5022. XRP Might Have Reached Bottom, Finally

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

XRP's sharp price decline might have finally stopped, as the asset has started to form a trading range that could provide a foundation for a recovery going forward. The most likely support level here is $0.9, as the price has already tested it three times.

The rapid downfall of XRP began on November 5, essentially marking the beginning of a sharp decline for the asset. The failure to break above the 100 EMA effectively eliminated the possibility of an uptrend. The price failed to break the mid-term trend and declined rapidly.

The declining trading volume is yet another signal. As the downtrend accelerated, investors reacted to the rapidly deteriorating market conditions by selling some of their holdings, further fueling the downward movement.

With the divergence of moving averages, volatility is decreasing, but a more established bearish trend is forming, unfortunately. The most likely outcome could be a deceleration of the trend and a halt to the current decline.

Fundamentally, the cryptocurrency market remains weak. Risk-on sentiment among investors has disappeared, while the crash in tech and AI stocks could further fuel outflows from Bitcoin and crypto. The recoveries we are witnessing right now remain largely speculative.

It is important to note that the $0.9 support level does not necessarily confirm that XRP has reached its bottom. The asset is still trading substantially below its major moving averages, with the 50 EMA and 100 EMA remaining out of reach for now.

Until XRP manages to reclaim at least the $1 psychological price level, any upward movement will most likely remain a short-term recovery rather than an actual trend reversal.

At the same time, the formation of a narrow trading range around $0.93 could give buyers some breathing room. If selling pressure continues to decrease and volume stabilizes, XRP might try recovering and taking a chance at breaking the $1-$1.05 price level, where the first serious resistance is most likely.

#5021. Crypto casinos in Canada: How to check payout speed, fees and game fairness before you deposit

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

It’s possible to see a blockchain transfer within minutes, but that doesn’t mean a casino will process a withdrawal request that fast. Various factors, including fees and the game being played, impact how quickly a withdrawal can be processed. With that in mind, readers should evaluate claims beyond the headline.

When reviewing a crypto casino in Canada, the first step should be to analyze the payment terms and ensure the casino is approved to take your business in your province. Just because a casino supports crypto payments doesn’t mean it’s licensed to take your business, offers instant payouts, or has games that have been vetted by an independent testing agency. Each of those should be supported by additional evidence.

Internal approval and blockchain processing are separate steps in a withdrawal request.

Look at each step of the withdrawal process in turn. Determine if a guaranteed timeframe covers the entire process or only certain requests.

A payment transaction hash is the best way to trace a payment. Prior to the payment being transmitted, account status or support can give information about the payment processing.

There are several costs associated with a crypto payment that should be taken into consideration. A wallet or exchange may charge a fee to send funds, a blockchain may charge a fee to process the transaction, and the casino may charge a withdrawal fee.

Conversion also needs to be considered. Deposits converted to a Canadian dollar balance need to consider exchange rates, conversion timing, and spread. Leaving funds in cryptocurrency means their Canadian dollar value can change, while the coin balance remains the same.

Look at minimum withdrawals and fees. A small payout can incur a high cost, especially with fixed fees. Promotions can also impact which funds are available for withdrawal.

Evaluate evidence supporting claims of game fairness. Consider how outcomes are determined and whether published rules are implemented. Look for game providers who are independently tested and named, rather than relying on a badge.

A stated return to player is based on the long-term average and does not prohibit large losses.

Some games offer “provably fair” verification. Note what the verification actually checks, and whether you can reproduce the check yourself. Verifying a specific outcome does not mean you can validate withdrawal reliability, the legality of the game, or the security of the components of the game.

Readers from Canada should check their local gambling authority. In Ontario, for example, iGaming Ontario has a directory of licensed operators, and OLG operates separately. Usually, the actual website is more important than recognizing the brand.

Look at account currency and payment totals to determine withdrawal terms. Keep a copy of the terms to show and explain discrepancies to support your case.

It’s important to assess speed of payout, fees, and fairness of a service separately. Assessing a service by the acceptance of cryptocurrency, in general, is a poor quality standard. It’s important to set limits on time and money spent, and to set funds apart, for example, for gambling, from funds that should be for saving or for daily expenses.

#5019. Here’s How SWIFT Will Adopt XRP In Their Back-End

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Here’s How SWIFT Will Adopt XRP In Their Back-End

SWIFT’s own framing confirms a multitude of XRP-tied banks are inside the core design of this pilot.

A popular software engineer has voiced his opinion on Ripple’s XRP Ledger’s ties to SWIFT and the possibility of integration for cross-border remittances. XRP’s chain is designed to primarily address cross-currency conversion and liquidity challenges via XRP as a bridge asset, rather than basic payments.

That’s exactly what SWIFT is looking for. The ability to process transactions in a matter of seconds instead of several business days serves a crystal-clear advantage over traditional financial messaging systems. The fast on-ledger cross-currency payments in 3-5 seconds using its built-in DEX and AMM for conversions.

XRP abd the ledger solve cross currency y conversion problems, not payment problems. SWIFT will eventually adopt XRP currency settlement in its backend, using XRP as a settlement intermediary. https://t.co/RKHWWDubeu

Another crucial factor is Ripple’s On-Demand Liquidity (ODL), constantly providing on-demand liquidity without pre-funded nostro accounts. That kind of duality enables XRP’s use both as a bridge crypto currency and an infrastructure layer for immediate payment settlements. SWIFT started their own 24/7 ledger in November, testing it out on multiple Ripple-affiliated banks.

HSBC, Standard Chartered and BNP Paribas and DBS Bank have all participated in on-chain transactions via Ripple’s tech stack one way or the other. These Ripple connections relate to custody infrastructure & tokenized assets. The SWIFT pilot itself uses bank-issued tokenized deposits orchestrated on SWIFT’s permissioned ledger not via XRP.

Most notably, the native coin’s price isn’t performing well: the OG altcoin has broken below the $0.99 lower price boundary of the channel, hinting at the high chance of a deeper correction underway. Analyst Ali Martinez expects XRP’s price to maintain the support bubble between $0.64 to $0.80, judging from the latest technical analysis.

#5018. Elon Musk Backs Binance Founder's Use of Grok AI Assistant

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Elon Musk has reacted to a post by Binance co-founder Changpeng Zhao (CZ), in which the latter shared that he used Grok Bot to tidy up his X following list.

According to CZ, the bot was tasked with removing inactive and abusive accounts from the list of people he follows, though accounts with constructive criticism were supposed to stay. CZ was not fully sure about the results, adding "AI did it" with a shrug emoji.

Musk replied with "Cool." Later, CZ added in the same thread that it "worked well so far."

Back in September, CZ had to address rumors about his account being hacked after users noticed that he had unfollowed a number of profiles. Per CZ, at that time he removed accounts that were inactive for more than 30 days.

Grok Bot, the agent product by Musk's xAI, was launched on Aug. 11, with X integration added at the end of that month. The bots run on separate cloud computers and can log in to a user's apps and accounts.

Since then, the product has received praise from Nvidia head Jensen Huang, who said in October that Grok Bot usage was "really skyrocketing." Musk himself claimed on SpaceX's Q3 earnings call that he had never seen a product grow this fast.

On Coinbase, Grok was responsible for 59.54% of AI-agent trading volume from Sept. 14 to Sept. 21. For comparison, Claude had 13.37% and ChatGPT only 1.54%. Coinbase CEO Brian Armstrong then named Grok the leading client for agentic traders on the platform, to which Musk also responded with "Cool."

At the same time, there were complaints. NewCore researchers reported last week that Grok Bot made 31 unrequested access attempts to email, calendar, Slack and GitHub accounts on two employee laptops within five days, ignoring the command to stop.

It was only last month that Musk was trolling Binance after the exchange lost about $6.3 billion in a hack on Oct. 29, the largest one in crypto history so far. Among other things, Musk posted "Everyday is Zeroday" and replied with fire and laughing emojis to CZ's earlier warning about phishing links.

CZ then asked Musk to stop, calling it a "bad time to troll," and reminded him that Binance's $500 million investment in Twitter in 2022 has become a stake in SpaceX worth more than $1 billion.

Now, the two seem to be on good terms again.

#5017. Banks Are Adopting Stablecoins: Here Is Why Crypto-Native Dollars Still Win on User Freedom

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

As traditional giants rush into digital dollars, USDD’s Yvonne Chia explains why 24/7 access, self-custody, and transparent yield give decentralized stablecoins an edge banks can’t replicate.

20 November – Road Town, Totorla, British Virgin Islands – Decentralized stablecoin protocol USDD is accelerating its multichain utility across TRON, Ethereum, and BNB Chain—combining automated sUSDD yield via its Smart Allocator with GasFree zero-TRX transfers—as traditional financial giants race to build competing stablecoin rails.

In a recent industry interview with instant exchange partner ChangeNOW, USDD Head of Communications Yvonne Chia outlined why self-custody, 24/7 settlement, and transparent onchain reserves give decentralized digital dollars a structural advantage over bank-issued alternatives.

For billions of people globally, opening and maintaining a traditional bank account still involves paperwork, minimum balance requirements, geographic restrictions, and approval delays. Even when banks integrate stablecoin rails behind the scenes, those services remain gated behind legacy compliance walls and intermediary control.

Crypto-native stablecoins flip that model on its head. As Yvonne Chia highlighted during the interview:

“Compare this to a stablecoin like USDD, where all you need is a phone, a working internet connection, you can immediately start a wallet and you have access to your funds right away.”

With USDD, users can access and manage their digital dollars directly through their own wallets, without depending on traditional financial intermediaries.

This represents a fundamental shift in financial access: users are no longer limited to the services, operating hours, or account structures of a single institution. Instead, they have the freedom to hold, manage, and transfer their assets on their own terms.

Self-custody also gives users greater autonomy over their financial decisions. They can choose which wallets to use, where to transfer their assets, and which supported decentralized applications to interact with, without needing to route every transaction through a centralized financial institution.

With USDD, users’ digital dollars can move with them across an open blockchain ecosystem, rather than remaining tied to a particular bank or service provider.

Legacy payment networks still operate on decades-old schedules: weekend cutoffs, bank holidays, and multi-day cross-border settlement delays accompanied by hefty wire fees.

Blockchain networks never close. Whether it is a Sunday night or a public holiday, USDD moves globally without depending on traditional banking operating hours or settlement windows.

“We are able to have transfers 24/7 and we’re not limited to any traditional banking hours. So I think that in some ways, stablecoins are not just becoming banks, but exceeding what traditional banks can offer to users.”

To make everyday transfers even smoother than fintech apps, USDD introduced GasFree integration on TRON, allowing users on supported wallets to pay network fees directly in USDD without needing to hold TRX.

This removes another practical barrier to stablecoin adoption: having to acquire and maintain a separate gas token just to transfer digital dollars. With GasFree, eligible users can pay approximately 0.5 USDD per transaction, compared with around 1.5 USDT for a standard USDT transfer under the referenced fee conditions.

These improvements matter because the usefulness of a stablecoin is not determined by its peg alone. Low-cost transfers, straightforward wallet experiences, and around-the-clock availability are what make digital dollars practical for everyday payments and global value transfers.

Perhaps the sharpest contrast between banks and decentralized stablecoins lies in how yield is distributed.

In traditional banking, institutions take customer deposits, deploy them into yield-generating markets, and retain a significant portion of the returns. Everyday depositors may receive relatively low interest rates, while higher-yielding products often come with eligibility requirements, minimum deposits, or fixed maturity periods.

Stablecoins introduce another way to participate in digital-dollar opportunities. Rather than keeping capital entirely within a traditional deposit account, users can access onchain yield opportunities through decentralized protocols and transparent investment mechanisms.

USDD was built to return that value directly to holders. Through USDD Earn, users can deposit USDD to receive sUSDD, earning automated, competitive yield powered by our Smart Allocator system, with no fixed lock-up periods restricting access to their capital.

For users, the distinction is important: USDD Earn provides access to a productive stablecoin while maintaining flexibility, rather than requiring users to commit their capital to a conventional fixed-term product.

Traditional banks ask customers to trust closed-door balance sheets and periodic regulatory filings—until a crisis proves otherwise.

USDD takes a different approach through its “Don’t trust, verify” ethos, emphasizing publicly accessible onchain information and independent verification.

Transparency does not eliminate financial or technical risks, but it changes how users can evaluate them. Instead of relying exclusively on an institution’s assurances, users have access to information that can be independently examined and verified.

The bigger picture is an interconnected financial ecosystem in which users can move between holding, transferring, exchanging, and deploying their stablecoins according to their own needs. These are not isolated functions. Together, they make stablecoins more useful as everyday financial infrastructure.

Banks will undoubtedly continue using stablecoin rails to modernize their back-end settlement. However, true financial freedom comes from open networks where assets flow freely across protocols without intermediaries.

For USDD, the opportunity goes beyond offering a dollar-pegged asset. It is about making digital dollars accessible, transferable, and productive across an interconnected financial ecosystem. From self-custody and 24/7 transfers to flexible yield opportunities and multichain DeFi access, these capabilities give users more ways to put their money to work.

Banks may be bringing stablecoins into traditional finance. Crypto-native protocols are showing what stablecoins can make possible beyond it.

Watch the full interview at https://x.com/USDDecentralize/status/2121126175099773054.

#5016. Crypto crash ahead? Why the next TWO weeks could rattle markets

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The next two weeks could be crucial for investors.

The macroeconomic calendar has several important data releases this week, with the October PCE Inflation report on Wednesday being the most significant. With only two weeks until the next FOMC meeting, these releases may have an impact on market expectations for the Fed’s next potential rate move. However, investor positioning already suggests that crypto assets remain vulnerable.

Investor Bill Ackman said in a post on X that given increased spending on AI technologies and increased borrowing costs at SpaceX (now at 8.259%, compared to less than 6% pre-IPO), a 3% inflation target may be a more realistic target than 2% as set by the Federal Reserve.

In addition, Elon Musk also raised concerns around the increasing cost of components (chips, energy, data centers, etc.) in AI systems, and how this is putting pressure on company balance sheets, reinforcing concerns that AI may cause prolonged inflationary pressures.

In this light, markets are still leaning towards the Fed holding rates at the upcoming FOMC meeting, even if October’s inflation data comes in hotter than expected. However, some investors believe rate hikes are already being priced in as a way to cool the AI boom and curb rising valuations.

Naturally, such uncertainty in Fed policy is putting more pressure on crypto markets. As such, given that risk appetite is also low, the question is whether a year-end crypto crash is becoming a bigger possibility.

Over the last 48 hours, the U.S. market has seen significant selling pressure.

The sell-off has triggered a broad de-risking across equities, metals, and crypto, wiping out trillions in market value. U.S. equities alone have shed $5.5 trillion in market cap, while the crypto market has lost around $2.2 trillion. Bitcoin [BTC] slipped below $56k as selling pressure spread across the market.

Meanwhile, U.S. President Donald Trump warned that things “will only get worse,” blaming Democratic midterm election wins for wiping out nearly $6 trillion in stock market value since the election. However, investors aren’t entirely buying this explanation. Instead, concerns around Fed policy, sticky inflation, and the rising U.S. debt ceiling are increasingly driving market fears.

The result? Some investors now see the possibility of further rate hikes as a “deliberate” move to cool an overheated market.

This relates to the debate around the likely decision of the Fed. While some are concerned that further interest rate rises may cause a recession within the economy, others believe tighter monetary conditions are required to put inflated market values back into line.

In this scenario, the next two weeks could prove crucial for crypto markets. With AI bubble concerns building, major macro data ahead, Trump’s latest warning, and growing uncertainty around the Fed’s next move, crypto’s recent dip could be the start of a much broader correction.

Seven key macro releases, rising AI costs, and uncertainty around the Fed’s next move could keep crypto markets under pressure.

A $5.5 trillion U.S. market wipeout, Trump’s warning, and growing rate hike fears raise the risk of a deeper crypto correction over the next two weeks.

#5015. South Korean exchanges halt CYBER transfers amid suspected bridge exploit

Cyber reported unexpected minting of CYBER tokens on Solana, followed by tokens moving out of its Ethereum bridge lockbox, prompting an investigation.

South Korean crypto exchanges Upbit and Bithumb suspended deposits and withdrawals of CYBER after the project reported suspicious activity involving its cross-chain bridge.

On Sunday, Bithumb announced it would suspend deposits and withdrawals because of suspected security issues. The exchange said trading will continue, while transfers would resume once network stability was assured. Upbit also announced a temporary suspension of CYBER deposits and withdrawals.

On Saturday, Cyber said it detected unexpected minting of CYBER tokens on Solana, followed by a transfer of tokens out of its bridge’s Ethereum lockbox.

Cyber, formerly CyberConnect, operates an Ethereum layer-2 network designed for social applications.

The project said it was investigating the incident and assessing which assets and bridge routes were affected. It advised users to avoid using the bridge or depositing CYBER to exchanges while the investigation continues.

The suspensions come days after South Korea’s Financial Services Commission announced plans to allow exchanges to halt deposits and withdrawals when hacking is suspected, rather than waiting for a confirmed breach.

Earlier in November, South Korean exchange GOPAX also suspended trading, deposits and standard withdrawals after receiving a hacking threat, despite saying no breach had occurred.

#5014. Zcash (ZEC) Might Become More Secure on iPhones Soon

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

On Zcash Community Forum, a new grant application has emerged which is aimed at making Zcash even more private on iPhone-based wallets. The author seeks a $25,000 grant in order to implement a private way of retrieving encrypted transaction memos.

Essentially, the proposal allows hiding which transactions users have been looking up, which is a potential deanonymization vector. Considering how Zcash placese itself on the market, revealing any kind of external information is a step which might shorten the distance between a random transaction and a specific user.

Zcash transactions today contain encrypted data called memos. Various wallets read those memos in order to receive information about the transaction. The message itself stays encrypted, so is retrieved data, however, the query itself is not protected by any means which enables potential tracing.

Even when using Tor, the transaction ID stay out in the open and can be read by anyone.

With the integration of private information retrieval into SDK, hiding the query itself wil be easier, messages would be decrypted locally and wallets themselves will verify the returned information.

The first tests have already shown promising results. During testing in an iOS Simulator, the modified SDK managed to retrieve a 512-byte memo through an encrypted query, without revealing the transaction ID to the wallet server.

The developer expects the implementation of the proposal by January 30, 2027, with testing on physical iPhones and a public SDK release planned for the final stage.

However, the proposal is still seeking funding, and its actual adoption will depend on individual wallet developers.

#5013. 5 Best VPNs Worth Using for Crypto

Home » Cryptopedia » 5 Best VPNs Worth Using for Crypto

As global tracking and exchange geofencing tighten, choosing the right VPN matters more than ever.

If you’re involved in crypto, you know how quickly things can go wrong: one wrong click, an unsecured public Wi-Fi connection or a data leak can expose your wallet, trading activity or identity.

In 2026 the stakes are higher. Crypto adoption is growing, DeFi is expanding, AI capabilities are advancing, and more jurisdictions are looking to monitor financial activity.

Your internet service provider (ISP), exchange or local network may be able to see your IP address and traffic patterns. Public Wi-Fi remains a common security risk, while geo-restrictions on exchanges are still widespread. If your IP address is linked to a wallet or trading account, the risk of further identification increases.

A good VPN encrypts your connection, hides your real IP address and reduces this exposure. It does not replace hardware wallets, two-factor authentication (2FA) or sound operational security. It is just one layer of protection.

To identify the strongest options for 2026, we evaluated providers against these key criteria:

Instead, Mullvad generates a random 16-digit account number. You can add funds using Bitcoin (BTC), Bitcoin Cash (BCH), Monero (XMR) or even physical cash sent by mail. The price is a flat €5 per month, with no long-term discounts or renewal price increases. Cryptocurrency payments qualify for a 10% discount.

Mullvad runs its own full node for each supported cryptocurrency and hosts all its wallets, so no third party is involved.

Mullvad has undergone multiple independent security audits. In a 2023 police raid, authorities reportedly found no customer data to seize. The service uses RAM-only servers, open-source apps and the WireGuard protocol. It supports up to five devices.

Best for: Users who want to provide as little personal information as possible when signing up.

Proton VPN has one of the most consistent public audit records among the providers reviewed. The Swiss-based company passed its fifth consecutive annual no-logs audit by Securitum in June 2026.

Payment options are more limited. Bitcoin is available only after you create a free account and upgrade to a paid plan. Proton also accepts cash in CHF, USD and EUR.

Proton VPN operates more than 20,000 servers in over 130 countries. Its apps are open source, and paid plans support up to 10 devices.

Unlike providers that use RAM-only servers, Proton VPN relies on full-disk encryption. For leak protection, it offers a standard kill switch and a permanent kill switch on Windows and Linux, which blocks internet access if the VPN connection drops.

Best for: Users who value audited transparency and Swiss jurisdiction and are comfortable providing an email address.

NordVPN stands out for its performance and range of security features. Based in Panama, where no data-retention laws apply, it uses RAM-only servers. Its no-logs policy has been independently verified six times, most recently by Deloitte Lithuania in late 2025.

Features include an internet kill switch, obfuscated servers, Dedicated IP options and Threat Protection, which blocks malicious websites and phishing links. NordVPN supports up to 10 simultaneous connections.

Payment options include Bitcoin, Ethereum, Litecoin and stablecoins.

Best for: Active traders who prioritise speed, Dedicated IP options and a broad set of features.

Surfshark combines competitive pricing with a growing record of independent audits. Its Trust Center lists Deloitte audits from 2023 and 2025 that verified its no-logs policy. KPMG audit also confirmed Surfshark does not store browsing history, IP addresses, connection timestamps or outgoing traffic.

Crypto users can pay with Bitcoin, Ethereum, Cardano, Dogecoin, XRP and Shiba Inu through supported third-party wallets. However, an email address is required to register.

Surfshark is based in the Netherlands. The company says local law does not require it to log or retain user activity. Its entire server network uses RAM-only infrastructure.

Security features include an automatic kill switch and Everlink, which switches users to another server during maintenance without interrupting their session.

Signup generates a random account ID and requires no email address or other personal information. Payment options include Monero, Bitcoin, physical cash, credit cards and PayPal. The company also runs its own nodes for cryptocurrency payments.

IVPN’s apps are fully open source. Built-in features include AntiTracker, a kill switch/firewall and V2Ray obfuscation.

The company has commissioned annual security audits by Cure53. These reviews assess its apps and infrastructure; they are not equivalent to a Big Four audit specifically designed to verify no-logs claims.

In August 2026, a vulnerability in BTCPay Server allowed an attacker to drain 0.6168 BTC from IVPN’s Lightning node. IVPN says no customer data was accessed.

A VPN hides your IP address, protects traffic on public Wi-Fi and limits what your internet provider can see. But it does not make your crypto wallet anonymous.

It can if you use a shared IP address that jumps across countries or lands on an exchange’s blacklist. Centralized exchanges (CEXs) frequently flag sudden IP changes as suspicious login attempts or sanctions-evasion risks. If you trade regularly on a CEX, stick to a single server location or use a Dedicated IP add-on so your login address stays consistent.

Almost never. Most free VPNs monetize users by logging traffic, injecting ads, or selling connection metadata to third parties—which defeats the purpose of crypto operational security. The only exception on this list is Proton VPN’s free tier, which has no data caps, no ads, and is covered by the same independent no-logs audits as its paid plans, though it limits server locations and doesn’t support direct crypto payments without upgrading.

Yes. HTTPS encrypts the contents of your connection, but it does not hide your IP address or the domains you connect to. Without a VPN, your internet provider can still see that you are connecting to crypto exchanges or DeFi front-ends, and your wallet’s default RPC node can still log your home IP alongside your public wallet address.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Pump.fun [PUMP] is up 4.74% over the past 24 hours as of writing, a slightly more positive gain compared to the rest of the crypto market, which rose 2.34%. While there is no verified catalyst behind the rally, PUMP’s reported cross-margin and Pro mode rollout may have sparked interest in the token again. Furthermore, the broader market’s recovery and a bid-heavy order book could be another reason for the surge.

However, the recent rally is looking cautiously optimistic, as the recovery is yet to overcome the bearish trend, leaving PUMP at considerable risk of another pullback in the near term.

On the daily chart, PUMP was trading at $0.002568 at the time of writing and approaching the 23.6% Fibonacci resistance at $0.002745. However, the broader price structure remains firmly bearish, with the token continuing to make lower highs and lower lows.

Reclaiming this Fibonacci level would be an initial sign that buyers are gaining ground, but the chances of that happening in the near term appear to be slim to none.

The indicators were also pointing to a potential near-term reversal with an RSI of 31.53 and OBV continuing its downtrend, indicating that sellers are still in control of the market. This makes it difficult for the recent price recovery to develop into a sustained reversal.

The four-hour chart offered a slightly more encouraging picture. After recovering over the past two days, PUMP reclaimed the 61.8% Fibonacci level at $0.002554 and entered the golden pocket. However, it has since started to retest the support, making this an important area to defend. Holding above it could open the door to $0.002654, the next Fibonacci resistance.

The MACD showed improving momentum, with its line above the signal line, although both remained below zero. H4 OBV also maintained its broader upward trend despite a recent dip, suggesting buying interest had eased slightly.

It’s absolutely vital that the bulls defend $0.002554 successfully, as it would extend the recovery toward $0.002745. Losing this support, on the other hand, would expose $0.002484 and potentially further losses.

#5010. 'I Hate Risk’: Ripple CTO Emeritus Explains XRP and Bitcoin Sell-Off

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

David Schwartz, CTO Emeritus at Ripple and one of the creators of the XRP Ledger, has once again commented on his XRP and Bitcoin sales. This time, the reason was a viral post on X that claimed he had sold almost all of his crypto over the last few months.

According to the post, Schwartz's portfolio reportedly went from 26 million XRP, 1,500 BTC and 40,000 ETH to just 3 million XRP, 1 BTC and 6 ETH. The author called it "an insane reduction" and also referred to Schwartz as the current CTO of Ripple, while he now holds the emeritus title.

Schwartz replied that the timeline in the post was wrong. "That wasn't in the last few months, that was in the last decade," he wrote. As proof, he shared his own post from April 21, 2019. In that post, Schwartz said he was still sticking to the "de-risking strategy" that he and his wife agreed on when they first decided to put their own money into crypto. He also admitted back then that selling during the bull run "still hurt."

At the same time, Schwartz did not dispute the numbers themselves. "But it's basically true. I hate risk. I can't help it," he wrote in a follow-up post. According to him, pretty much every big risk he has ever taken has worked out well, and rationally he should be taking more risk as he gets wealthier. "But I hate it. I just hate risk and can't do anything about it," Schwartz added.

That wasn't in the last few months, that was in the last decade. You can find me talking about this in many posts over the years, like this one:https://t.co/5MgcPZ5qyG

At press time, XRP was trading at around $0.939, Bitcoin at $61,477 and Ethereum at $1,722. At these prices, the remaining 3 million XRP, 1 BTC and 6 ETH are worth about $2.89 million, and XRP accounts for more than 97% of this sum.

For comparison, at current prices, the peak holdings mentioned in the post would be worth over $185 million, with Bitcoin alone accounting for about half of it.

Schwartz has already explained his sales several times this year. In May, he said he did not have much XRP left and had tried to move most of his assets, other than Ripple stock, away from crypto exposure. In July, he said he regrets selling XRP at $0.10 and ETH at around $1, but noted that he and his wife had agreed to sell at every new all-time high.

Earlier in November, Schwartz disclosed that he sold about 2.5 million XRP in 2025. Per Schwartz, most of these tokens were received in 2020 and 2023, and he paid approximately $1.7 million in capital gains tax on those sales. He shared this in response to a user who called XRP a "terrible investment."

Among other things, Schwartz has previously said that his main exposure to the crypto sector now comes through Ripple stock, not XRP. In March, Ripple launched a share buyback of up to $750 million that valued the company at $50 billion.

#5009. Why SpaceX Stock Is Surging—and Why Wall Street Sees $210 Next

Home » Blitz news » Why SpaceX Stock Is Surging—and Why Wall Street Sees $210 Next

Jensen Huang’s reinstatement at Nvidia sparks a broad AI rebound, adding fresh momentum to Jefferies’ 64% upside call on SpaceX stock.

SpaceX stock (SPCX) is rallying sharply today, surging nearly 8.09% to around $138 as broader AI and tech markets rebound following Nvidia’s unanimous decision to reinstate Jensen Huang as CEO.

The momentum adds fresh fuel to a bullish call from Jefferies, which just initiated coverage on SpaceX with a Buy rating and a $210 price target—implying roughly 64% upside from pre-rally levels

In a research note titled It’s Just Rocket Science, Jefferies identified Starlink as the core engine behind SpaceX’s long-term valuation.

Jefferies projects Starlink could generate $92 billion in annual revenue by 2029—growing at a 70% compound annual rate—with operating margins exceeding 60%, according to Investing.com.

The core launch division is forecast to deliver $9 billion in revenue and $4 billion in EBITDA by 2029 as Starship scales toward 315 flights per year.

As hyperscalers race to secure electricity, data centers, and compute capacity, SpaceX’s orbital network and massive capital deployment position it to capture growing AI infrastructure demand.

While Jefferies’ thesis originally framed SpaceX as a resilient play during the broader AI selloff, market sentiment flipped overnight. Nvidia’s board reinstated Jensen Huang just 11 days after his resignation—sending NVDA shares up roughly 30% and lifting the Nasdaq nearly 7%

On Friday, the S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average slipped more than 10% below their record highs into correction territory, while the Nasdaq dropped roughly 5%, erasing its 2026 gains.

Nvidia sat at the epicenter of the rout, plunging roughly 13%–14% on Friday following CEO Jensen Huang’s resignation amid scrutiny over an alleged Chinese chip-smuggling link, alongside reports that SEC enforcement staff are reviewing potential accounting issues.

Newly public Anthropic also surrendered most of its first-day IPO gains during its second session, while expectations of a December Federal Reserve rate hike added pressure across high-growth tech.

For crypto traders, SpaceX’s stock trajectory matters on two fronts. First, high-beta tech and AI equities continue to dictate institutional risk appetite—sharp drawdowns in Nasdaq heavyweights often trigger short-term deleveraging across Bitcoin and AI-linked tokens.

Second, Jefferies’ thesis reinforces one of crypto’s strongest narratives: compute and bandwidth scarcity, a bottleneck driving capital toward both orbital infrastructure and decentralized physical infrastructure networks (DePIN).

#5008. Fusionist ACE Bridge Exploit: $5.7M Loss Estimate, Bithumb Freezes Trading

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

The Fusionist ACE bridge exploit has produced an estimated $5.7 million in losses across two stages, according to SlowMist’s incident tracker. After unbacked ACE tokens were minted on BNB Chain and sold, traders reportedly used the resulting price collapse to withdraw real ACE from the project’s Endurance bridge reserves. The fallout reached centralized exchanges on Monday, November 23: Bithumb halted ACE trading after finding indications that abnormally issued tokens had entered crypto exchanges.

This was not one attacker walking away with $5.7 million in a single transaction. The distinction matters. Roughly $1.87 million was traced through the attacker’s proceeds, while another 53.7 million genuine ACE tokens, valued around $3.7 million at the incident’s reference prices, were reportedly withdrawn through the bridge. SlowMist lists a combined $5.7 million loss estimate, not an audited final total. The bridge’s most basic promise, that a token issued on one chain corresponds to something real on another, is precisely what now needs proving.

Security firm Blockaid flagged the Fusionist bridge-minter exploit on November 22. Its alert described the mechanism bluntly:

A privileged token-bridge minter minted unbacked ACE and sold it for USDT.

The firm initially estimated that about $1.04 million had been extracted while the attack was still ongoing. That was an interim snapshot, not a cap on the damage.

Blockchain-monitoring account Onchain Lens subsequently reported that an attacker had routed 1,091.5 ETH, valued at about $1.87 million at the time, through Tornado Cash. That observation traces a movement of proceeds, not an extra $1.87 million to add on top of the initial extraction estimate. The values describe different points in the same suspected attacker’s fund flow.

Fusionist acknowledged an apparent ongoing ACE exploit on BNB Smart Chain on Sunday and asked users to suspend transfers, trading and bridging on that network. It has not, in the material reviewed, provided a reconciled loss figure or a technical post-mortem identifying how the privileged minting authority was abused.

A bridge minter is supposed to create a token representation only when the corresponding value is properly accounted for. Once the right to mint is misused, a chain can show perfectly ordinary-looking balances that have no matching backing.

It is a familiar failure mode: NewsBTC previously covered a bridge-role exploit that minted unbacked tokens at CrediX. That earlier case does not establish Fusionist’s root cause, but it illustrates why elevated bridge permissions are such a consequential security boundary.

The subsequent drain may prove more important than the initial minting spree.

According to SlowMist’s November 22 incident entry, traders bought the sharply discounted ACE created by the market disruption and used the cross-chain bridge to withdraw about 53.7 million genuine ACE from the Endurance-side reserves. SlowMist valued that withdrawal at approximately $3.7 million.

Here is the mechanism without the jargon. A representation of ACE on BNB Chain suddenly became cheap because unauthorized supply had flooded the market. A bridge that would still accept those tokens in exchange for ACE on Endurance offered an obvious difference in value.

Anyone able to complete that conversion could receive real tokens in return for deeply discounted ones. The trading opportunity existed because the backing and the circulating representation were no longer in a trustworthy relationship.

The $3.7 million figure is a valuation of the ACE reserve outflow, not proof that every recipient sold those tokens for $3.7 million in cash. Nor does it mean every trader involved helped compromise the minter.

There is a meaningful difference between initiating the security breach and trading through a system whose defenses have already failed.

Those distinctions should not obscure the core problem. A bridge can have technically valid transactions and still produce an economically disastrous result. If unbacked supply can be redeemed for genuine reserves, the checks have failed where they matter most.

Bithumb suspended ACE deposits and withdrawals early on November 23. It then halted trading effective 7:30 a.m. Korea time, according to its follow-up ACE security notice.

The exchange said, in a translation from its Korean announcement, that it had:

confirmed indications that abnormally issued ACE had entered cryptocurrency exchanges.

It identified two related BNB Chain transactions and a second transaction.

Those records are important evidence leads, but a full independent decoding of the transaction trail remains outstanding.

Bithumb’s wording is careful: it reported signs of abnormal supply entering exchanges, not a finding that every ACE token in customer accounts is invalid. The trading suspension is a protective response, not a finalized determination of customer losses.

Still, it creates a practical problem for holders who never used the bridge. If tokens are fungible within a market, exchanges and the issuer need a credible method for identifying affected supply and deciding how deposits, balances and trades will be treated.

There is an understandable temptation to focus on the enormous minting figures. The more consequential question is what happened when those tokens met the bridge’s reserves.

A system does not need to lose control of its entire blockchain to suffer a serious failure in one privileged contract role. Nor should a successful-looking bridge transaction count as evidence that the asset being transferred was fully backed.

The response cannot end with a pause notice and a promise to investigate.

Fusionist needs to show which minting permissions were affected, whether they have been revoked, how much ACE was created without backing, how the Endurance-side reserve changed and what claims remain outstanding.

Exchanges need a defensible token-treatment plan, and holders need to know whether there is a shortfall and who is responsible for covering it.

There is a fair counterargument: the $5.7 million figure is an external estimate, market prices moved during the incident, and the precise compromise method is not established.

SlowMist categorizes the incident as private-key leakage, but that classification is not a substitute for an official technical finding. It would also be premature to treat a cautious exchange halt as proof that every affected customer suffered a loss.

Those qualifications change how the numbers should be reported. They do not make the underlying bridge-accounting failure less serious.

The next credible milestone is not a restored interface or a reassuring social post. It is a transparent reconciliation of the tokens minted, the reserves withdrawn and the assets still available to back legitimate ACE claims.

Until that exists, the estimated $5.7 million fallout remains a warning about both token security and the risks of trusting a bridge’s accounting.

#5006. Cardano (ADA) Hits Major Death Cross, Will 2026 Year-End Prices Surprise?

The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Cardano just saw a death cross on its daily chart, with the 50 MA falling below the 200 MA. The timing of this signal remains significant, as it marks the second consecutive November in the last two years that Cardano has marked a death cross. Cardano saw a death cross in November 2025, and the same is happening this November.

The signal in November 2025 was followed by months of falling prices, which has led some to speculate that the cryptocurrency may have further downside or a recovery ahead of the end of 2026. The current uncertainty in the market has added to concerns. A volatile weekend saw Cardano fall as low as $0.134, its weakest level since December 2020, before rebounding. This recovery has entered its third day, with ADA up 2.05% in the last 24 hours to $0.146.

Whether that rebound holds is the test traders will keep an eye on. The $0.18 area represents an initial resistance level, while a stronger recovery toward the $0.198 to $0.20 range would bring Cardano closer to its daily moving averages.

Another possibility is sideways trading as ADA attempts to establish a sustainable bottom. Its daily RSI is flirting with oversold territory that may trigger a relief bounce, but that’s not enough to confirm a trend reversal. In a bearish scenario, continued selling pressure could push ADA below its recent $0.134 support area, exposing lower price levels.

Work progresses on a node version 11.2.x with Mainnet clearance; until then, 11.1.3 remains the recommended version. Node 11.2.0 introduced a new “envelope” config file format, which will be the format published on the config file pages for networks running 11.2.0 and above.

#5005. Trump’s Meme Coin Dinner Exposes a 94% Collapse in Holder Commitments

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Donald Trump’s latest meme coin dinner came with a striking financial comparison: wallets displayed on the official $TRUMP dinner leaderboard held approximately $9.1 million in tokens at the November 16 cutoff, compared with roughly $148 million associated with the first dinner in May 2025. That puts the decline in the publicly measured dollar value at close to 94%. It does not mean investors withdrew 94% of their cash, or that 94% fewer people turned up for dinner.

Trump hosted the third event for major $TRUMP holders at his Virginia golf club on Sunday, November 22. The gathering itself was new, but the more interesting story is what the leaderboard reveals about a token that was once able to turn a private invitation into a market-wide buying contest. NewsBTC previously covered an earlier $TRUMP meme coin dinner rally. This time, the numbers tell a considerably less flattering story about the value attached to that privilege.

The roughly $9.1 million estimate comes from Public Citizen’s November 22 review of the 130 wallets displayed on the final public leaderboard. Its researchers counted 9,794,832.87 $TRUMP tokens and valued them at approximately $0.93 each at the November 16, 9 a.m. Eastern cutoff. The older $148 million comparison refers to the first gala’s leaderboard in May 2025.

Source: Official $TRUMP Gala Dinner Leaderboard (November 16, 2026). Rankings based on time-weighted token holdings. Wallet entries do not necessarily represent individual attendees.

That works out to a decline of roughly 93.9%, rounded to 94%. But the events did not have identical leaderboard sizes or necessarily identical valuation methods. More importantly, the token’s price fell dramatically between the two events. A lower marked-to-market balance can reflect falling prices even if some holders retain comparable token quantities.

Calling this a 94% collapse in new investment would therefore be wrong. The narrower conclusion is still hard to ignore: the value represented by the public competition for dinner access is a small fraction of what it was at the first event.

The leaderboard also should not be mistaken for a headcount. While the public display showed 130 wallets, organizers said 185 wallets qualified under the event’s rules. One person can control multiple wallets, and a wallet can be held for an organization. Neither figure identifies exactly who attended Sunday night’s dinner.

The smaller overall value is only part of the picture. Public Citizen found that the top 10 wallets held about 81% of the tokens on the public board. The top 29 accounted for approximately 95%. Those top-ranked wallets were also eligible for a special VIP reception.

The concentration matters because a leaderboard is marketed as a contest between investors, but its aggregate value can be dominated by a very small number of positions. Looking at the $9.1 million as if it represented broad, evenly distributed enthusiasm would be as misleading as treating all 130 wallet entries as separate people.

There is a revealing detail near the bottom of Public Citizen’s review: the wallet ranked No. 130 held only 9.71 $TRUMP, worth about $9 at the snapshot. It qualified because the competition measured time-weighted holdings over the scoring period, rather than the balance left in the wallet at the very end. That is perfectly compatible with the rules. It also shows why a dinner ranking cannot be read as a simple list of fresh purchases or long-term conviction.

There is another market point here. A buyer who wants dinner access can acquire the token and hedge some of the price risk elsewhere. Holding a spot position long enough to earn a place does not necessarily make someone a directional believer in the coin. In that sense, the competition measures an incentive to qualify, not a clean vote of confidence in future returns.

Public Citizen’s researchers traced 66 of the 130 displayed wallets, or 51%, to direct transfers from exchanges that restrict US customers. Those wallets represented about 4.35 million $TRUMP, roughly 44% of the public board’s token holdings. Among the top 29 VIP-ranked wallets, 15 had received tokens directly from such exchanges, according to the group’s November 22 findings.

That is useful on-chain information. It is not proof that 66 dinner guests were foreign nationals, that all those exchanges served prohibited customers, or that any particular attendee broke a rule. An exchange withdrawal identifies a source of tokens, not a passport or the identity of the person controlling the receiving wallet. Public Citizen put a basic transparency problem plainly: “The names of the invitees have not been disclosed.” That sentence matters because a wallet address, however transparent its transaction history, cannot supply the accountability that a named guest list would provide.

GetTrumpMemes.com is not political and has nothing to do with any political campaign or any political office or governmental agency.

That is the organizers’ characterization. It does not remove the transparency question created when holders of a president-linked digital asset can compete for personal access to the president. Neither does that question, by itself, prove an improper exchange of favors.

There is a credible bullish counterargument. An exclusive dinner is a limited, experience driven reward. Its value to attendees need not move in lockstep with the coin’s market capitalization, and falling token prices can make it cheaper for new participants to qualify. The gala went ahead, and its promoters continue to offer other experiences to holders.

But that is precisely why we should be careful about treating an invitation contest as evidence of a durable investment base. Access is an incentive. It is not a claim on business earnings, and it does not put a floor under the price of the coin.

The most uncomfortable part of this comparison for $TRUMP holders is not that the dinner was smaller or less glamorous. The available figures do not establish either claim. It is that the token’s most recognizable promotional feature remains personal access, while the dollar value visible on the public leaderboard has fallen dramatically since the first event.

In our view, that weakens the argument that repeated celebrity events, by themselves, can sustain the market’s appetite for the token. A more persuasive sign of recovering demand would be durable, independently observable holdings beyond a contest period, with less dependence on a few top-ranked wallets. The next useful data would be post-event wallet balances and exchange flows, measured against the November 16 snapshot, not just another photograph from the dinner.

For now, the 94% comparison is a warning about the dollar value of the visible leaderboard, not proof of a 94% investor exodus. That distinction makes the story more credible, and no less troubling for anyone relying on the dinner promotion as an investment thesis.

#5004. Ethereum Outflows Hit All-Time Low on Binance

As demand for major crypto assets has increased, Ethereum remains on hold amid growing market fear and uncertainty, while exchange activities across the broad crypto market are showing a bearish trend.

While Ethereum remains one of the most affected cryptocurrencies during the prolonged crypto market volatility, the latest data from the crypto analytics platform CryptoQuant shows a massive slowdown in its exchange outflows.

Amid growing market fear, Ethereum outflows from Binance over the last month have hit a historic low as investors continue to move fewer ETH tokens away from the world’s largest crypto exchange, Binance.

The data shows that about 3.58 million ETH have been withdrawn from Binance over the last 30 days, marking an all-time low in Ethereum outflows on the exchange.

Further data shows that the decline has come as Ethereum continues to struggle to stabilize its price and continues to weaken, trading around the $1,700 mark.

It is important to note that the decline in exchange outflows is expected, considering the growing market weakness and negative shift in investor sentiment over the period.

However, the metric has drawn different attention from market participants, given expectations that the massive security breach Binance suffered should trigger large withdrawals from the exchange.

Although blockchain trackers recently identified multiple large Ethereum withdrawals by the Binance hacker, it appears that the moves did not materialize into increased Ethereum outflows.

Apart from the attackers' withdrawals, it appears that Ethereum holders on the exchange did not respond to the attack in the conventional way, as security incidents like this often make investors more cautious and wary about keeping their assets on the exchange.

While this typically prompts them to transfer the assets into private wallets where they have greater control, it appears that Ethereum holders remained comfortable with keeping their funds on the exchange.

#5003. Bitcoin drops to $55K after $670M liquidations – What changes now?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Last week, Bitcoin [BTC] fell to a low of $55,130 on Saturday, the 20th of November. This was lower than $57,800, which the leading crypto registered on the 1st of July.

It led to debate around whether the bear market was truly over, or whether what we saw in August-September was only a pause within the longer-term bearish trend.

Over the past two weeks, Bitcoin liquidations exceeded $600 million on four separate days.

The 11th and the 20th of November, when BTC breached key support levels at $73k and $61.4k, saw the heaviest long liquidations. Also, contextually speaking, these two days witnessed $690 million and $670 million in long liquidations, respectively.

The persistent downward price action was accompanied by relentless selling. Since the 5th of November, every trading day bar one has seen negative spot ETF flows.

Last week’s net outflows alone reached $1.245 billion. This incessant selling would need to ease before Bitcoin could find room to recover.

As things stand, a weekly trading session close below $57,800 is needed to flip the structure bearishly. The previous week’s correction, though deep, might be just a liquidity sweep before a recovery.

Bitcoin holders are under severe strain as prices approach or drop below their cost basis, and Open Interest has plunged dramatically. It was a result of tightening monetary policy and the global equity market downturn.

Over the past year, the BTC Open Interest was down by $4.5 billion.

In July, this figure fell as low as $6.5 billion. It was the largest OI downturn in Bitcoin’s history in dollar terms, observed crypto analyst Darkfost.

There have been eight trading days in 2026 with over $1.5 billion in liquidations across the crypto market. A decline in risk appetite among investors, several liquidation cascades, and the overarching bearish price action for most of the year helped explain the OI drop.

A leverage reset can be healthy for the market.

Alongside the weekly BTC chart, there was hope for a bear market bottom. Even so, it remains risky to buy, as a bear market continuation cannot yet be fully ruled out.

#5002. Big XRP Holders Add $72 Million While Supply Hardens

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Big XRP Holders Add $72 Million While Supply Hardens

Big-time holders draw attention to XRP’s liquidity pockets sub-$1: is a bounce back signal getting delivered?

Large XRP holders have added roughly $72 million worth of tokens in recent days, even as major Bitcoin and Ethereum investors have reduced exposure during the broader market pullback.

The buying has coincided with a drop in available XRP on Binance, where reserves have reportedly fallen below levels last seen in July.

The move does not yet signal a clear bullish reversal. XRP was trading near $0.97, down about 3.6% over the previous week and still below its 200-day moving average near $1.26.

The combination of whale accumulation and thinner exchange balances has drawn attention to whether modest returning demand could produce a sharper price response.

An exchange supply-shortage measure for XRP rose to 2.4 on November 19, 2026 above its July reading of 2.09. Usually, readings above 2 are generally associated with unusually limited immediately available supply on the venue.

#XRP supply on Binance is getting tight. The Supply Shortage Index spiked above 8 in November and sits at 2.4 now, while price is still near $0.96.Supply is tightening and price has not responded yet. pic.twitter.com/xMTksTkKim

Large wallets also pulled more than $127 million in XRP from Binance on a net basis between November 10 and 16, 2026.

Some of those movements may reflect internal exchange reserve management rather than pure investor withdrawals, particularly after operational disruption at the platform, so the flows are not a definitive accumulation signal on their own.

Lower balances can still matter if spot demand returns. Fewer tokens sitting on exchanges may reduce readily available selling pressure, especially if a portion of the withdrawn XRP is moving into longer-term storage rather than being repositioned for immediate trading elsewhere.

US spot XRP ETFs have gone 15 consecutive trading days without a net inflow, their longest dry spell since launch. Since their last positive day on October 29, the funds have recorded net withdrawals of about $26.3 million.

Total XRP ETF assets have fallen to roughly $1.08 billion from $1.77 billion in late September, though cumulative inflows since launch remain close to $1.78 billion. Existing holders have not abandoned the products in large numbers, but fresh capital has paused.

The divergence is the central feature of the current setup: large holders have been willing to buy into weakness, while ETF demand has stayed on the sidelines.

Tighter exchange supply could amplify a recovery if broader buying returns, but without renewed fund inflows and a reclaim of key technical levels, scarcity alone is unlikely to reverse the trend.

#5000. Bitcoin briefly drops to $17K on Kraken, exchange upholds trades

Kraken said all orders were processed correctly and found no evidence of a security breach or compromised accounts.

Crypto exchange Kraken said it would honor trades executed during a sharp selloff across its Canadian-dollar markets that briefly pushed Bitcoin to the equivalent of about $17,000 on Saturday.

In a statement, Kraken said its Canadian-dollar trading pairs experienced heavy selling pressure starting around 20:00 UTC on Friday, causing prices to fall significantly below those on other markets.

The exchange said every order was processed correctly and its security teams found no evidence of a breach or compromised accounts. “Executed trades stand,” Kraken said.

Kraken’s BTC/CAD pair fell to $24,250 Canadian during the 15-minute interval beginning at 08:00 UTC on Saturday, according to exchange trading data.

On Friday, the Bank of Canada’s reference rate showed C$1.4325 per US dollar, putting Bitcoin’s low on Kraken’s Canadian-dollar market at about $16,900. This was about 71% below Bitcoin’s price of around $57,650 on Kraken’s BTC/USD market during the same five-minute interval.

Blockstream CEO Adam Back suggested that a market maker on Kraken’s USD/CAD spot market may have exhausted its funds, causing prices to diverge from broader foreign exchange rates.

Back said arbitrage subsequently spread the pricing distortion to other Canadian-dollar trading pairs. Kraken has not confirmed Back’s proposed explanation.

Cointelegraph reached out to Kraken for comments but did not receive a response before publication.

#4998. Mysterious XRP Ledger Metric Spikes 3,062.1%: What Does This Function Do?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

One rather unusual metric on XRP Ledger has seen more than a 3,062% increase in usage which raises a lot of questions. This is one of the largest spikes throughout the year in a function usage that mostly stays low.

The function allows users to manage their secondary keys for signing transactions on their wallets and/or accounts. Their main private keys stay offline, while a certain key issued by them is used in daily transactions.

It allows safer work and utilization of their wallets without potentially exposing their private keys. So even if someone grabs hold of your signing credentials, you'll be able to protect your funds by nullifying the issued keys, provided you still retain authorized access.

One way or another, the surge is bizarre. Most likely "legit" scenarios are: automated key rotations among large custodians like exchanges. Some security warnings that triggered massive migration and then, the illicit one: generation of function calls from bots or automated scripts.

The flatlined chart actually represents tens or hundreds of calls daily, but a surge to nearly 4,000 is certainly out of the blue. Unfortunately, we were unable to identify the source of those transactions.

The feature itself is legitimate, so the spike alone does not indicate that users' funds or wallets are at risk. However, an undisclosed exploit or security issue cannot be ruled out without further investigation.

One concerning scenario would involve malicious actors gaining access to wallets and assigning new regular keys to maintain control over them. This would not necessarily mean that the XRP Ledger itself has been compromised, but rather that individual accounts may have been targeted.

For now, there is no evidence pointing toward such an attack, and the activity could just as easily be attributed to routine security operations.

Still, XRP holders should keep an eye on any unexpected changes to their account settings, especially regular keys they did not authorize.

Whether this was a coordinated security upgrade, automated activity, or something more concerning remains unclear. What is certain is that such an unusual spike deserves closer attention, particularly if similar activity continues over the coming days.

#4997. XRP’s Below $1 Not For Long? Supply Tightens

Home » Ripple (XRP) » XRP’s Below $1 Not For Long? Supply Tightens

XRP has slipped below the $1 mark even as the amount available on Binance is tightening sharply.

XRP has fallen below the $1 level even as available supply on Binance appears to be shrinking, leaving the token between constrained exchange inventory and a lack of fresh institutional buying.

Recent reports put XRP near $0.95 after a steep monthly decline, with the token briefly trading under $0.90 during November’s broader sell-off. Intermittent rebounds have occurred, but price has not yet established a durable hold above the $1 mark.

US spot XRP ETFs have gone through their longest stretch without a net inflow since launch, according to SoSoValue data. The funds last recorded an $8.43 million net inflow on October 29 and have since seen roughly $26 million in withdrawals, though flows on November 18 and 19 were described as flat rather than net negative.

The pause matters because ETF buyers had previously provided incremental demand. Total net assets in the funds stood at about $1.08 billion by November 19, down from roughly $1.77 billion in late September, while cumulative inflows since launch remained near $1.8 billion.

XRP’s price action reflects that gap. The token was down about 3.6% over one recent seven-day period and roughly 28% over the prior month in another snapshot. Its 30-day Sharpe ratio was reported near negative 0.41, indicating that recent returns have not compensated for the asset’s volatility.

CryptoQuant’s XRP Supply Shortage Index for Binance rose to 2.4 on November 19, above its July reading of 2.09. Readings above 2 are described as severe tightness, and net withdrawals from the exchange exceeded $127 million between November 10 and 16.

Lower exchange balances can reduce immediately available selling inventory, but they do not form a standalone bullish signal. One analyst noted that part of the movement could reflect internal Binance reserve management rather than accumulation by long-term holders.

XRP’s price action has not yet confirmed stronger demand. XRP remains below its 200-day moving average, sitting very near $1.26, and repeated closes below $1 have turned that former support into nearby resistance.

Traders are watching the $0.94 area first, followed by support around $0.88. A sustained break below the latter could open a path toward $0.80, while a recovery would need to reclaim roughly $0.985 and then $1 before a broader reversal appears more credible.

The more relevant signal for the market is whether ETF flows resume as Bitcoin and broader risk sentiment stabilize. Tighter supply can amplify a move higher, but without returning demand it can also coexist with a fragile price floor.

#4996. Shiba Inu Dev Flags Security Concerns as Pump.Fun Migration Asks Users for Private Keys

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Long-time Shiba Inu community member and SHIB developer, Mazrael, has reacted to Solana-based memecoin platform Pump.fun's recent wallet login changes, which are sparking concerns about private key security.

In a new announcement, Pump.fun stated that external wallet sign-in is being retired on November 30, 2026. Starting November 30, 2026 (15:00 UTC), Pump.fun on the web will only allow users to sign in with an email account or a Google, Apple, or GitHub account instead of an external wallet such as Phantom, Solflare, or Ledger.

Pump.fun reassures users that nothing will happen to their funds or account, as their wallet address, positions, callouts, followers and username remain unchanged. The only change is how users sign in.

Users who sign in to their accounts with Phantom or any other wallet are urged to take action before the November 30, 2026 deadline.

Pump.fun outlined a number of steps for users on how to switch their sign-in; of particular concern is the step that asked users to enter their wallet details and paste their private key when prompted. Pump.fun noted that this step encrypts the key in the user's browser and that the key is end-to-end encrypted by its login provider, Privy, allowing users' wallets to continue working for everything else.

Mazrael reacted to the development, drawing attention to the potential security implications for users.

pumpfun is retiring external wallet logins on web starting Nov 30. From then on you sign in with Google, Apple, email or GitHub. To keep your existing account..... they want you to paste your private key into their Privy flow 😬 Also note their own terms says they won’t ask for private keys...

The standard security practice across the cryptocurrency industry is that exchanges, wallet providers and other platforms do not ask users to disclose their private keys or recovery phrases. Such warnings are routinely issued to protect users from unauthorized access to their funds.

The Pump.fun wallet login change process appears to contradict this widely promoted security principle, Mazrael noted.

Private keys are highly sensitive credentials that give holders direct access to their cryptocurrency wallets. Whoever gets their hands on them might be able to access those wallets and move the funds. In general, it is recommended never to share private keys with individuals, support agents or third-party services.

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Bitcoin Hits $63K on Nvidia News—Could It Be a Trap?

Risk appetite returned in force on Monday—but big players aren’t acting like the bottom is in.

Bitcoin price climbed approximately 4% to around $63,200 on November 23, staging a sharp recovery after plunging to a Saturday low of $55,081 over the weekend.

The rebound followed a sudden turnaround in U.S. tech equities and a wave of forced liquidations across crypto derivatives markets.

US markets staged a sharp rebound on Monday, November 23, led by a stunning 26% surge in Nvidia (NVDA) shares, after the company’s board reinstated Jensen Huang as CEO.

NVIDIA Board reinstates Jensen Huang as President, CEO and Board Director.Read the release: https://t.co/x4bnjiSVil

The move helped reverse the recent tech and AI sell-off, sending Nasdaq 100 futures up 3.1% and S&P 500 futures up 2.4%, lifting risk appetite across global markets, including crypto.

Bitcoin price recovery was further amplified by a short squeeze. More than $440 million in leveraged crypto positions were liquidated over 24 hours, including $370.2 million in shorts. Bitcoin short liquidations alone accounted for $172.91 million, according to CoinGlass.

Despite Monday’s bounce, underlying institutional data reveals ongoing caution. U.S. spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) recorded $669.16 million in net outflows on Friday—their largest single-day capital exit since June 25, according to SoSoValue.

Over the past week, spot Bitcoin ETFs lost roughly $1.8 billion, marking their fourth consecutive week of net outflows.

Corporate accumulation also showed signs of restraint. Strategy (MSTR), the largest corporate holder of Bitcoin, purchased 457 BTC for $28 million last week—a modest addition compared to the $212 million it spent repurchasing its own STRC stock over the same period.

“The biggest Bitcoin corporate holder isn’t capitalizing on the dip,” crypto investor Ted Pillows noted on X.

Market analysts remain divided on whether the weekend drop marked a final floor or a temporary pause before another leg down.

Prominent trader Astronomer (@Astronomer_zero) suggested on X that while Bitcoin approached a macro bottom near the $56,000 level over the weekend, the current move to $63,200 is likely a relief bounce rather than a confirmed bottom, warning that a downward retrace remains probable.

$btc 56k bottom looking really good now, with a 10%+ bounce so far.Even though I think this will retrace and I deem it just a bounce, it is a very important bounce and a strong bounce like this has significance and is exactly what we wanted to see.If you remember my short… https://t.co/TzN9MxF9tc pic.twitter.com/WitozYCG5Z

However, the trader argues that a durable bottom is forming soon and expects Bitcoin to hold above $50,000.

His thesis centers on Bitcoin’s estimated electricity production cost—currently around $60,300—which has historically served as a reliable long-term accumulation zone during cycles in 2018, 2020, 2022, and 2026.

When prices dip below mining electricity costs, inefficient miners shut down, network difficulty adjusts downward, and supply pressure eases, establishing a technical and economic floor near $50,000.
