#4992. Nvidia, AMD, Micron Sink as Anthropic Closes 17% Above IPO Price

Nvidia, AMD, Micron Sink as Anthropic Closes 17% Above IPO Price

Anthropic made stock market history Thursday, raising $81.34 billion in the largest initial IPO share sale ever. But Wall Street's response was considerably less enthusiastic than the demand for the Claude developer's shares.

The stock opened at $111.50 on Nasdaq under the ticker ANT, a 34% premium over its $83 offering price, briefly pushing the company's fully diluted valuation past $2 trillion.

The rally faded quickly, however, with shares dropping toward $93 before closing today at $97.17.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Anthropic with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The offering involved 980 million shares and surpassed SpaceX's original $75 billion IPO in June. As Decrypt previously reported, SpaceX's expanded final offering reached $86.25 billion, meaning Anthropic has not surpassed that completed deal.

Investors initially welcomed the listing, sending technology stocks higher before the opening bell. By the afternoon, however, the Nasdaq Composite was down 2.06%, the S&P 500 had fallen 1.9%, and the Dow Jones Industrial Average was off 2.17%.

The selloff extended across the AI supply chain. A Thursday afternoon market heatmap showed Nvidia falling 3.4%, Broadcom losing 2.07%, and memory chipmaker Micron slipping 2.17%. Microsoft was down 1.97%, while cloud infrastructure and software provider Oracle declined 2.97%.

AMD was among the hardest hit, down 7.73% in the day, although its decline had a separate catalyst. Reuters reported that the chipmaker came under pressure after investigative outlet Hunterbrook disclosed that its affiliated investment fund had taken a short position in the company.

The picture was not uniformly negative. Palo Alto Networks rose 6%, CrowdStrike gained 2.24%, and Intel added 2.25% in the same snapshot. The divergence shows that investors were distinguishing between companies rather than selling every technology stock indiscriminately.

The broader weakness comes as investors question the cost of building AI infrastructure and the returns companies can generate from that spending. Nvidia's latest earnings beat expectations, but uncertainty following Jensen Huang's resignation has continued to weigh on the semiconductor giant.

Anthropic also faces an expensive expansion. Its IPO disclosures detailed approximately $900.9 billion in infrastructure and computing commitments extending through 2045, alongside another $150 billion to $175 billion in estimated payments under less precisely defined arrangements.

"Anthropic's record-breaking IPO has failed to rekindle enthusiasm for tech stocks," Joe Maher, market economist at Capital Economics, told Reuters.

Nvidia's weakness was notable given its latest results. The chipmaker reported $115.8 billion in quarterly revenue on Tuesday, up 103% from a year earlier, and forecast stronger-than-expected sales for the following quarter.

Even those numbers failed to erase concerns over the departure of longtime CEO Jensen Huang after allegations of extramarital affairs and possible leaks of corporate secrets and the sustainability of the AI spending boom.

The pressure also reached companies that rent out computing capacity. Neocloud operators CoreWeave and Nebius fell 5.0% and 7.8%, respectively. Software companies were weak as well: Salesforce declined 3.17% and Adobe lost 2.03% in the afternoon heatmap. From chips and data centers to enterprise software, the losses extended beyond any single corner of the AI trade.

Anthropic's IPO prospectus shows how closely these companies are tied together. Nvidia and Google each committed roughly $10 billion to a conditional private share placement alongside the IPO.

AMD agreed to invest $2.5 billion; Broadcom, Micron and Microsoft committed $1 billion apiece; and TSMC pledged $500 million. The $26 billion placement is separate from the public offering. Several of these investors are also key suppliers or infrastructure partners in the AI ecosystem.

That creates a central question for investors: How much of the money flowing into AI companies will ultimately translate into profitable demand for chips, cloud capacity and software?

Anthropic's blockbuster debut demonstrates demand for its shares, but Thursday's broader decline shows that Wall Street is not ready to extend that confidence to every company financing or supplying the AI boom.

#4990. Shiba Inu (SHIB) Santa Rally Hopes Meet a Grinch: Four Red Decembers in Five

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Shiba Inu (SHIB) gained 5.96% over the past 24 hours and traded at $0.0000038 on Monday, according to CoinMarketCap, as talk of a December "Santa rally" returned to the crypto market. Still, SHIB is down 8.86% over the past seven days, and the token has lost 18.5% in November so far, according to CryptoRank.

Last week, SHIB dropped to $0.00000337, its lowest level since 2021.

The hopes for a Santa rally come after Goldman Sachs analysts said last week that most of the December gains on the stock market usually come after Dec. 15. SHIB's own December history, however, shows that the Santa rally came for the token once in five years.

According to CryptoRank, SHIB lost 29.5% and 13.5% in December of 2021 and 2022, respectively. In December of 2023, the token gained 24.6%. In December of 2024 and 2025, SHIB lost 21% and 17.6%, respectively.

SHIB's median December return is -17.6%, and its average December return is -11.4%, according to CryptoRank data.

The green December of 2023 came after SHIB gained 6.04% in October and 6.62% in November of that year, according to CryptoRank. In 2026, SHIB lost 18.6% in October, and November is also in the red so far.

The Goldman note, titled "GS Flow of Funds: Chopsgiving," came from Gail Hafif, Brian Garrett and Lee Coppersmith, who looked at S&P 500 data going back to 1928. According to the note, the index usually gains 1.1% from mid-November to early December and then gives back about two-thirds of that gain by mid-December, and around 80% of the full 2.4% gain comes after Dec. 14-15.

Since 1990, the S&P 500 has risen after Dec. 15 in 27 of 36 years, according to Goldman.

The Goldman team called these figures a historical map. In the years when the VIX volatility index was above 18 in the middle of November, the S&P 500 gained 1.34% on average, and the index ended the year higher in 59% of cases, according to the note. According to Goldman, the VIX was above that level in mid-November of this year as well.

Goldman also said that retail investors have been buying big tech and chip stocks. Large funds and systematic traders have been cutting their positions, according to the note.

The Stock Trader's Almanac counts the last five trading days of December and the first two trading days of January as the Santa Claus rally. This season, the window will open on Dec. 24 and close on Jan. 5.

In the window that ended on Jan. 5, 2026, the S&P 500 declined 0.11%, according to the Almanac. "Santa was a no-show for the third year in a row," the Almanac wrote. Before that, the S&P 500 also ended the Santa Claus rally windows of 2023 and 2024 lower.

#4989. The future of onchain FX could start with users, not institutional liquidity

Onchain FX does not need to recreate the trillion-dollar institutional market to become useful. Real demand can make smaller markets work first.

Opinion by Michael Egorov, founder of Curve Finance and Yield Basis

For a long time, the obvious way to think about onchain foreign exchange (FX) was to start at the top. Banks tokenize currencies, large financial institutions move their FX activity onchain and institutional liquidity naturally follows.

The end result? Blockchain-based FX eventually grows large enough to resemble its traditional counterpart. But that’s not necessarily how it is going to happen.

In fact, there are growing signs that onchain FX will develop in the exact opposite way — from the bottom up.

Stablecoins like Brazilian Digital (BRZ) or Monerium’s EURe can be quickly redeemed onchain and easily moved from digital wallets to bank accounts. They generate healthy trading activity as decentralized finance (DeFi) users swap them in decentralized exchange (DEX) pools.

Markets like this end up developing in real time around a practical user need, which is moving directly between digital currencies and fiat.

This is still just an early-stage example, but the use case and demand are real enough. Keyrock data shows that the majority of onchain stablecoin trades tied to emerging market currencies fall in the $1,000 to $10,000 range — modest amounts compared to institutional scale — but that’s precisely the point.

It shows how the market is building in the general direction of onchain FX to meet practical payment needs, not just large traders.

People and businesses need to move their money between currencies, and clearly, they are not content to wait for major traditional finance (TradFi) institutions to move their FX operations onto blockchain rails.

Related: Paraguay bank collapse shows stablecoins can offer a lifeline... of sorts

There’s a pretty common assumption about onchain FX in that a market needs huge liquidity before it can become really useful. In practice, though, that’s not entirely true.

If there is genuine demand from someone who needs to exchange dollars into euros, Swiss francs or another currency, even a smaller pool can process those transactions and serve a real economic purpose.

Stablecoin DEX Trade Volume. Source: Keyrock Research

Onchain FX doesn’t need to become big before it becomes useful; instead, it can grow precisely because people are actually using it.

As a matter of fact, this is more or less what happened with stablecoins. They began as a relatively niche crypto-native use case, and yet today we already know them to be part of a much broader financial infrastructure.

Chainalysis recently shared data that shows cross-border stablecoin activity jumped to over $220 billion by mid-2026. That’s an almost 80% growth over the course of one year, proving that demand and activity are constantly on the rise. There is no reason onchain FX can’t follow a similar path.

This is actually the most practical side of this issue. Onchain FX obviously won’t be able to compete with the entire global foreign exchange market anytime soon; that’s still years away. But the more interesting question is what problems it can solve for someone who already has money onchain.

Today, you can exchange a USD stablecoin into a EUR stablecoin (or some other alternative) onchain and use that money for payments with relatively little trouble. In fact, doing it this way is often faster and cheaper than going with a classic international bank transfer.

Related: Circle’s ARC token sale lifts earnings as lower yields weigh on USDC income

The same logic applies to onchain FX. A fintech or crypto-card company does not need to recreate the entire institutional market; what they need is to move their customers’ funds efficiently between currencies.

When someone is trying to pay in a different currency, converting it can add costs to the operation, since it usually involves Visa or Mastercard, which charge around 1% in processing fees.

Onchain exchanges are a much better option here. If user funds are already living on blockchain rails, swapping USD stablecoins into EUR stablecoins can be done directly, without going through any extra intermediaries, which is both faster and less costly.

Banks will most likely remain important entry and exit points where digital money needs to interact with the real world. Issuing and redeeming local currencies, accessing bank accounts, dealing with compliance and such is where banks will take the lead.

But when the money is actually onchain, many of the traditional “in-between” steps can be dropped for greater efficiency.

From the perspective of a user who just wants to conduct a financial transfer with minimal complications, that’s a highly positive improvement. How big or small the overall market matters little in this case.

Real demand is crucial, but it does not magically solve the problem of liquidity. The challenge is not simply to create a EUR/USD or CHF/USD pool; it’s to make them economically sustainable.

This is where automated market makers can make a difference. Traditional market-making firms may not find it attractive to provide liquidity for a small FX market; there may simply not be enough volume to justify the operational costs.

Cross-border stablecoin flows. Source: Chainalysys

Automated liquidity changes that equation because software can provide liquidity continuously without requiring a market-making firm to manually manage every trade.

Imagine two dollar stablecoins, USDC (USDC) and Tether (USDT). From the perspective of a traditional exchange, moving from one dollar stablecoin into another might look like a fairly boring business. Both assets are supposed to be worth roughly $1, so there isn’t much price movement to trade around.

But if there is still a real need for people to switch between stablecoins like that, an automated market maker can create a pool where users can make that swap directly. Each trade generates a small fee for the liquidity providers, and when enough people need the same conversion, a market can exist even when it doesn’t look particularly exciting to a traditional market maker.

This kind of adoption cannot exactly be called explosive, true, but there’s also nothing wrong with slow, natural growth. Sometimes, new markets just need a small group of people who have a problem the existing infrastructure does not solve particularly well. If those users can make the market economically viable, liquidity can grow from there.

About the author: Michael Egorov is the founder of Curve Finance and Yield Basis. He is also a physicist, entrepreneur and crypto maximalist. Michael transitioned from the scientific field to the crypto industry and holds a bronze medal from the International Physics Olympiad and a PhD in ultracold atoms.

#4985. Citi Says Customers Are Responsible When AI Banking Agents Go Rogue—Even After a Hack

Citi Says Customers Are Responsible When AI Banking Agents Go Rogue—Even After a Hack

Citi has updated its online banking terms to make customers responsible for the actions of third-party AI agents accessing their accounts, including when an agent leaks their credentials and someone else uses them to get in.

The change, reported by TechCrunch Wednesday, makes explicit how the bank intends to handle an increasingly awkward question: Who pays when software handling your money makes a mistake?

Under Citi’s new terms, actions taken using credentials supplied to an outside agent are treated as authorized by the customer, even if the tool ignores instructions, exceeds its permissions, or shares those credentials with another party.

Citi says it has no obligation to investigate whether the agent followed the customer's original prompts, subject to applicable law.

A Citi spokesperson told TechCrunch that the November revision clarifies the risks of sharing banking credentials or granting outside tools account access. The provision does not apply to AI agents offered by Citi itself.

Citi is not acting alone. JPMorgan and Bank of America announced similar policies in October, while Citizens Bank warned customers this week by email that they could be responsible for what their AI assistants do.

PNC also changed its digital agreement on Wednesday. Its rules distinguish bank-approved connections, which can provide limited access, from handing an agent unrestricted access to an account.

The move follows a banking security scare from October 22. Major banks had detected AI agents logging into customer accounts, sometimes taking steps that appeared to go beyond their users' instructions, and executives said they struggled to determine which agents had been properly authorized.

An AI agent differs from a conventional chatbot because it can act on a user's behalf, potentially navigating websites, accessing financial records, or initiating payments. That makes a mistaken instruction or compromised login more consequential than a bad answer about a bank balance.

The Office of the Comptroller of the Currency has since issued AI examiner guidance that addresses outside agents, payment controls, and human approval. The framework is nonbinding and does not itself create new requirements for banks.

Citi's language does not automatically eliminate consumer fraud protections. Under U.S. federal consumer protection rules, banks generally must investigate unauthorized electronic transfers and limit customers' financial liability for fraud. Those protections cannot simply be waived through a banking agreement, although their application to transactions performed by customer-authorized AI agents remains less clear.

The Consumer Financial Protection Bureau says banks cannot use agreements to waive those legal protections, although the rules distinguish theft by an outsider from transfers actually authorized by a customer or someone given access.

Whether a particular AI-related loss qualifies as unauthorized depends on how the transaction occurred.

PNC's updated agreement takes a stricter operational approach: Customers may use certain connection methods approved by the bank, but cannot give third-party AI agents their passwords or one-time security codes, and agents generally cannot initiate payments unless PNC expressly permits it.

While the negative trend has continued, Solana has just taken over the leadership in the broad ETF market after recording its first inflow in a while.

Notably, the data shows that Solana spot ETFs have recorded a net inflow of $4.38 million over the week.

Although the net amount of capital received by the ETFs appears decent, it is significant considering the growing crypto market weakness.

While this marks an end to the Solana ETFs' six-week outflow streak, this is the first positive weekly performance they have recorded since October 9.

#4982. Will Bitcoin’s big rebound hold? More tampered Ledgers found: Hodler’s Digest

Bitcoin rebounds sharply from its new cycle low, but is $55,111 really the bottom? Ledger investigates new reports of tampered devices in Berlin and the UAE.

Bitcoin saw a sharp recovery on the weekend to $61,034, marking an 11% rebound from its new cycle low around $55,111 on Coinbase. But will it hold?

It wasn’t a crypto only crash however, as concerns grow over the sustainability of the debt-financed AI build out. The tech-heavy Nasdaq fell another 5.3% on Friday and is on the verge of a bear market, having dropped almost 20% from its October peak. The S&P 500 fell 8.6% last week, for the 12th worst week since 1952, and gold lost another 4.3% in the past 24 hours, to be 21.6% down since September.

At the time of writing Bitcoin’s price was holding steady around $60,750 but there are plenty of predictions it has further to fall. Many point to historical parallels with previous four year cycles, which bottomed after the US mid-terms.

Earlier in the week veteran trader Peter Brandt noted that:

The future arrived early on Kraken’s Canadian dollar markets when the BTC price briefly crashed to around US$17,000. Despite the price being 71% below its USD market, Kraken said everything was fine, every order was processed correctly and its security teams found no evidence of a breach or compromised accounts. “Executed trades stand,” Kraken said.

Crypto influencer Cryptobull offered a tiny bit of hopium when he pointed out that Bitcoin’s Market Value to Realized Valu (MVRV) peak this cycle only reached 2.29 times above its cost basis, and if you extrapolate that to the cycle low, it would come in at around 1.03 times the cost basis of $53,700. Which would be almost exactly the $55,130 price that Bitcoin just hit.

Unfortunately, cycle theorist Benjamin Cowen says that in bear markets Bitcoin usually ends up much lower than the realized price — and drops down to the balanced price, which Coinglass puts at $38.7K.

Ledger has warned customers who purchased hardware wallets in Berlin since January 2026 to contact its support team as the company investigates reports of tampered devices in Germany and the United Arab Emirates.

Several suspicious devices have been reported in Berlin, while two customers in Dubai reported losing a combined $604,000 in cryptocurrency.

Ledger said the incidents were connected to the same distribution-channel tampering campaign first investigated in Southeast Asia in October. Ledger is also offering physical inspections, including X-ray analysis, at its factory in Vierzon, France, for customers concerned their devices may have been compromised.

Last week Ethlabs was pushing to make Ethereum post quantum and AI proof in 2027. This week’s All Core Devs Call suggested the chain may not even see Glamsterdam activated until May 2027.

However the devs sound as if they’re leaning towards testing it out without making any changes to the gas repricing Ethereum Improvement Proposals (EIPs), and then activating it in mid-January to give companies and projects time to upgrade affected contracts. They punted a decision until next week about testing the fork on Hoodi in December.If they do decide to make modifications to the gas pricing EIPs activation could be pushed until late February or early March, or even April or May.

The call also discussed the possibility of modifying the EIPs in the Hegota fork to prioritize post quantum features, although several devs argued that reducing the upgrade’s scope would offer little benefit unless it also shortened the rollout timeline.

Wallets linked to the Binance hack moved nearly $600 million in Ether over two days, with some of the stolen funds routed through cross-chain swap protocols, according to blockchain analysts.

The attackers moved about $248 million ETH to new wallets on Thursday and another $346 million ETH, on Friday. Attackers routed part of the funds through cross-chain protocols THORChain and Chainflip, according to blockchain data provider Onchain Lens.

THORChain’s 24-hour swap volume soared to $239 million on Friday, pushing the protocol’s earnings to $243,000, according to THORChain’s dashboard.

Retail investors traded $772 million in Anthropic shares on its Nasdaq debut Thursday, but their net purchases totaled just $60 million, about half the $118 million they put into SpaceX on its first day of trading in June.

Anthropic’s first-day retail trading volume topped SpaceX’s $669 million and was the highest for any market debut in Vanda Research’s data, which go back to 2012, Yahoo Finance reported.

Volume counts both purchases and sales, while net buying measures how much purchases exceeded sales.

ANT closed its first day of trading up 17% on the IPO price, but the stock fell to just 7% above the IPO price on day two amid a broad market sell off.

At the end of the week, Bitcoin (BTC) is down 6.5% to trade at $60,815, Ethereum (ETH) is down 5.9% to trade at $1,713 and XRP (XRP) is down 4.3% to $0.02. The total market cap is at $2.08 trillion according to CoinMarketCap.

Among the biggest 100 cryptocurrencies, the top three altcoin winners of the week are Pieverse (PIEVERSE) with a 91.2% gain, Venice Token (VVV) on 28.74%, and Falcon Finance (FF) on 4.1%.

The top three altcoin losers of the week are Sky (SKY) which was down 22.6%, Artificial Superintelligence Alliance (FET) down 18.4% and Aptos (APT) down 16.8%.

Bitcoin carry trades could regain their edge over US Treasury bills in 2027 as demand for leverage recovers, according to digital asset bank Sygnum.

In its 2027 outlook, it forecasts that the annualized premium on three-month Bitcoin futures at the Chicago Mercantile Exchange would average more than 2% above three-month US Treasury bill yields next year.

Fabian Dori, chief investment officer at Sygnum, told Cointelegraph that returns could recover as investors become more willing to take risks and fewer hedge funds compete for the same profits.

Senator Ruben Gallego says the crypto industry put too much money into Republican election campaigns.

The International Organization of Securities Commissions (IOSCO) warned that upgrading blockchains to withstand quantum attacks could slow transactions and raise fees.

In a report released Thursday, IOSCO said some quantum-resistant digital signatures take up far more space than those used to authorize transactions today. Larger signatures mean fewer transactions can fit in each block, which could increase congestion, push up fees and require more storage.

IOSCO cited an experiment in which a test version of Solana ran about 90% slower with quantum-resistant signatures.

The US Senate passed legislation Thursday that would prohibit federally regulated prediction markets from offering contracts tied to war, terrorism, assassination and events primarily determined by a person’s death, sending the bill to the House for consideration.

The DEATH BETS Act, introduced by Democratic Sen. Adam Schiff in March, passed by voice vote on Thursday after lawmakers adopted an amendment by unanimous consent.

Sky Protocol processed more than $2 billion in stablecoin redemptions on Thursday, the same day a CoinDesk investigation linked $518 million in transactions through the protocol to illicit activity.

The withdrawals came amid concerns raised by the investigation that Circle could freeze USDC held by Sky. Sky said on Friday in a statement that USDS maintained its $1 peg throughout the withdrawals and challenged the investigation, pointing to a review by Block Analitica.

Dragonfly’s Haseeb Qureshi says Bitcoin could reach $400,000 by 2030 but doubts it will return to $100,000 by year-end with the rout in AI stocks weighing on crypto.

Phylax’s system lets decentralized protocols block potentially harmful transactions, but raises questions about potential censorship and Ethereum’s concentrated builder market.

As geopolitical and technological threats multiply, the big question is whether you can hold on to your wealth and access funds when the system fails.

Monday's advance returned Bitcoin to within roughly $173 of that previous high, with more than $372 million in forced short closures accompanying the move.

Veteran trader Peter Brandt has softened the tone of his recent Bitcoin warnings and said on X that the cryptocurrency held at the level it needed to hold after its drop to $55,150 over the weekend.

"Bitcoin held exactly where it had to hold -- for now anyway, but perhaps for more than now," Brandt wrote on Monday.

Brandt also shared a monthly chart of Bitcoin together with the post. The chart goes back to 2018, and Brandt marked on it the same patterns that he had already pointed to in previous years. According to the chart, the price of Bitcoin was back above $63,000 at the time of the post and stood at $63,338.

On Saturday, Bitcoin fell as low as $55,150. It was the lowest price of the cryptocurrency since September 2024, and before that, the lowest price of Bitcoin in 2026 was $57,718 on July 1, according to Coinbase.

On Nov. 15, Brandt posted a weekly chart with a possible head-and-shoulders top and said that, if the pattern proves valid, Bitcoin's October 2025 record of about $126,000 may not be surpassed in his lifetime. Two days before that, on Nov. 13, Bitcoin dropped below $70,000 for the first time since August.

Brandt has also pointed to the history of Bitcoin's bull market cycles more than once. Late last year, he posted a chart showing that each of Bitcoin's five major bull runs began after drawdowns of 75% or more, and he wrote that if the decline carried to $50,000, "the next bull market cycle should carry to $200k to $250K."

In April, Brandt said that Bitcoin could form an "investable low" in September or October 2026. According to Brandt, this low may or may not go below the February low near $60,000, and the next cycle peak could land between $300,000 and $500,000 in September or October 2029 if the four-year cycle continues to hold.

"Real bottom will not occur until October 2026," Brandt said in an interview in February.

The latest Brandt post comes amid a rally in U.S. tech stocks on Monday. Earlier that day, Nvidia announced that its board voted unanimously to reinstate Jensen Huang as president, chief executive officer and board member, effective immediately.

According to the Nvidia announcement, the board received input from senior levels of the U.S. government before the decision, and it is confident that Huang's return "poses no national security risk." Shares of Nvidia surged 33% after the news, according to CNBC.

Bitcoin went up together with tech stocks and briefly climbed above $64,000 on Monday. Before the Nvidia news, Bitcoin had already recovered from the Saturday low to around $61,500.

Huang resigned from Nvidia on Nov. 12. On Nov. 17, Nvidia board chair Harvey Jones said on the company's earnings call that the board "does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO."

#4979. CryptoUK Urges FCA Not to Treat DeFi Devs as Regulated by Default

CryptoUK Urges FCA Not to Treat DeFi Devs as Regulated by Default

The UK's main crypto trade association has called on the Financial Conduct Authority (FCA) to make sure that developers and governance participants in decentralized finance protocols are not treated as regulated firms by default.

CryptoUK set out its position in a response to the FCA's consultation paper CP26/36, which proposes guidance on the "perimeter" of the UK's new crypto regime: which activities fall inside it and need FCA authorization, and which don't. The group, which says it represents more than 150 crypto companies, submitted its response on Wednesday, November 18, the day the consultation closed, and published it a day later.

Yesterday, CryptoUK submitted its response to the Financial Conduct Authority’s Consultation Paper CP26/36, which proposes amendments to the Perimeter Guidance Manual (PERG) to reflect the FSMA 2000 (Cryptoassets) (Miscellaneous Amendments) Regulations 2026.

The consultation… pic.twitter.com/oOrZgAWscP

— CryptoUK (@CryptoUKAssoc) November 19, 2026

"Software development, maintenance, security work and participation in decentralised governance should not, by themselves, establish that a person carries on a regulated activity," CryptoUK wrote. That would be consistent with the FCA's "clear controlling person" approach to DeFi, under which regulation should hinge on who effectively controls a financial service, it argued.

CryptoUK said it "broadly" supports the draft guidance, which reflects further amendments to the UK's crypto legislation that the government laid in September. The FCA aims to publish its final guidance in January 2027. It welcomed new exclusions from the regime for temporary settlement and payment arrangements, assets backing UK-recognized stablecoins, proprietary trading and market making, and technical service providers.

But in several places, the draft "goes further than the legislation," the group said. It pointed to a proposed link to the UK based on where a protocol is developed or governed, requirements in the exclusions for payments and proprietary trading, and indicators that would narrow the exclusion for technical services. The FCA has acknowledged that the timing of the latest amendments created uncertainty for some firms, and said it will give applicants extra time where questions over the technical services exclusion held up their applications for authorization.

The group asked the FCA to publish a public record of stablecoins that qualify under the UK regime, to clarify how stablecoins differ from electronic money, and to include more examples of activities that typically fall outside the perimeter.

CryptoUK also urged the FCA to raise with the Treasury outcomes that put UK firms at a disadvantage, citing the treatment of UK technical providers that offer access to regulated overseas trading venues, and of payments using stablecoins issued abroad. The UK should align with the EU's MiCA framework "where appropriate," it said. The response was prepared with law firm CMS and signed by CryptoUK policy director Nick Brooks.

The consultation is part of the FCA's work to build out the UK's crypto rulebook. The regulator finalized its core rules for crypto firms in June, ahead of a regime due to take effect on October 25, 2027, and later issued guidance two weeks before its authorization window for crypto firms opened. It has also set out a shared vision for tokenization with the Bank of England.

#4978. XRP ETF Shares Surface in Schwab’s Repo Collateral

Home » Trendsetter News » XRP ETF Shares Surface in Schwab’s Repo Collateral

XRP ETF shares just appeared in collateral baskets pledged by JPMorgan & Bank of America to a Schwab money market fund.

XRP exchange-traded fund shares appeared in collateral baskets posted by JPMorgan and Bank of America to a Schwab money market fund, according to wealth coach Kamilah Stevenson.

In her latest YouTube episode, she argues that the reported holdings point to a less visible source of institutional demand: securities pledged against loans rather than bought for price exposure.

The distinction matters. Stevenson says Schwab did not buy XRP or the ETFs outright; its fund lent cash and received baskets of securities backing the transactions.

Citing monthly SEC disclosures, Stevenson identifies XRP ETF shares from Grayscale, Canary, Franklin and Bitwise among collateral supplied by the banks’ trading arms.

She puts their combined value at roughly $36,000 in April, $2 million in June and more than $8 million by August, an increase exceeding 200-fold.

Those figures remain claims presented in the video. The transcript does not specify the reporting year or filing identifiers, limiting verification of the sequence.

Stevenson acknowledges that the positions were small relative to the underlying loans. Their inclusion would demonstrate acceptance of particular ETF securities within collateral baskets, not necessarily an institutional commitment to holding XRP directly.

The YouTube video episode connects those reported positions to BlackRock’s effort to expand acceptance of tokenized fund shares as collateral. Dr. Stevenson says the appeal is straightforward: investors could pledge income-producing fund shares instead of redeeming them to provide cash.

She also points to Binance’s acceptance of BlackRock’s BUIDL fund as collateral and describes a wider shift toward blockchain-based collateral transfers. Her thesis is that assets used to secure financing can generate more persistent demand than assets held solely for trading.

“Trading creates excitement; collateral creates demand that stays,” Dr. Stevenson says.

That argument needs a boundary. Acceptance of XRP ETF shares does not establish that lenders accept XRP itself, and tokenized collateral does not automatically require XRP to move between markets. Stevenson presents direct XRP-backed lending and cross-currency settlement as potential next steps, not completed developments.

#4977. Bitcoin Bloodbath Fails to Stop Michael Saylor's Buying Spree

Despite the sharp fall in the cryptocurrency market, Michael Saylor-led Strategy has not stopped its Bitcoin (BTC) buying spree, as it picked up 457 BTC worth around $29.6 million.

The firm's recent purchase comes as the leading cryptocurrency has been struggling to rebound from the heavy sell-off that recently dragged it to a two-plus-year low.

The latest data from Strategy's SEC filing reveals that the firm has bought 457 Bitcoin at an average price of $64,898 per coin.

The new purchase brings Strategy’s Bitcoin holdings to around 851,305 BTC, which accounts for over 4% of the cryptocurrency's maximum supply of 21 million coins.

The firm has now spent around $64.23 billion to build its Bitcoin holdings at an average price of $75,446 per Bitcoin.

It is worth noting that Strategy has been buying BTC at gradually decreasing prices as the cryptocurrency market continues to bleed heavily.

On Nov. 2, the firm announced that it had bought 811 BTC, worth around $65.5 million, at an average price of $80,846 per Bitcoin.

A week later, on Nov. 9, Strategy announced the purchase of 1,036 BTC worth $81.9 million at an average price of $79,012.

The firm’s Bitcoin purchase a week later, on Nov. 16, was relatively smaller at 485 BTC, worth $33.4 million, with an average price of $68,887.

The new 457 BTC purchase continues this pattern. The latest Strategy purchase was made at an almost 20% lower price than the firm’s Nov. 2 acquisition.

Overall, the firm has made four weekly purchases, scooping up around 2,789 BTC worth $210.4 million.

However, these purchases have been much smaller than before. The latest $29.6 million purchase is almost 64% lower than the $81.9 million purchase announced just two weeks ago.

Although Saylor is doubling down on Bitcoin, Strategy’s massive holdings in the cryptocurrency are underwater as the market has been struggling to regain its footing.

On Nov. 21, Bitcoin hit a low of around $55,130, its lowest level since September 2024, before rebounding above $61,000.

The firm’s recent purchases have helped reduce its overall average Bitcoin acquisition cost, but the drop has not been that significant.

For example, with the latest purchase, the average cost of the firm’s Bitcoin holdings dropped from $75,452 to around $75,446, a decline of just $6 per Bitcoin.

At the same time, the firm has also been putting a lot of resources into supporting its preferred securities.

In the latest reporting period, Strategy sold around 1.93 million common shares, raising net proceeds of $176.7 million.

#4976. Why Bitcoin surged toward $64,000 before pulling back as trading slows 54% into Thanksgiving

Bitcoin recovered from a weekend low near $55,100 to approach $64,000 before retreating toward $62,500. Earlier Coinbase data showed trading volume falling 54% in a comparable morning hour.

Bitcoin traded near $61,200 early Monday, up 0.5% over 24 hours after falling to approximately $55,100 early Saturday. The recovery subsequently accelerated as U.S. markets reopened, carrying Bitcoin toward $64,000 before a volatile pullback toward $62,500. Wednesday's U.S. inflation report and fresh ETF flow data will provide further tests of the rebound.

Bitcoin had already reclaimed $61,000 on Sunday. Through Monday’s morning reading, about $4.65 billion changed hands in 24 hours across the spot markets CoinGlass tracks, where investors trade Bitcoin directly. That was about 24% less than in the previous 24 hours.

The Coinbase trading data showed a sharper slowdown in one comparable hour. About 333 bitcoin changed hands against the dollar from 7 to 8 a.m. ET Monday, compared with about 730 in the same hour Sunday. That is a decline of about 54%. Bitcoin ended the Monday hour at $61,253 on CryptoSlate's Bitcoin price page.

From 7 to 8 a.m. ET, 729.99 BTC traded Sunday and 333.38 BTC Monday in Coinbase Exchange’s dollar market.

Source: Coinbase Exchange hourly candles · · Coinbase candle field definitions

Bitcoin extended its recovery during Monday's U.S. session, climbing from about $61,200 earlier in the day toward $64,000 after NVIDIA announced Jensen Huang's return, before retreating toward $62,500.

The rally briefly put Bitcoin approximately 16% above Saturday's low near $55,100. Even after the pullback, the cryptocurrency remained approximately 13% above that weekend trough.

The volatile price action followed an earlier slowdown in spot trading. Coinbase recorded approximately 333 BTC changing hands against the dollar during the comparable morning hour Monday, down 54% from Sunday's 730 BTC.

That comparison captures trading conditions before the subsequent rally. The later advance and reversal add a new development to the recovery, while leaving open whether trading activity strengthened as U.S. investors returned.

The next U.S. spot Bitcoin ETF flow figures will provide another measure of demand. Farside Investors' latest completed tally had shown $669 million in net outflows on Friday, before the weekend rebound.

Stocks were coming off a rough Friday. The S&P 500 fell 3.7% in that session, and futures tracking the index were up 2.1% early Monday before the U.S. stock market opened, according to Libella’s morning market update. Around 7 a.m. London time, the same report put Brent crude futures near $90 a barrel, gold futures up about 3% and the U.S. dollar index down about 0.2%.

A separate test comes from funds that hold Bitcoin and trade on stock exchanges. Farside Investors’ flow data showed $669.2 million in net outflows from U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs on Friday, the latest completed tally available Monday morning. The next full report will show whether investors continued withdrawing money from the funds or returned as Bitcoin recovered. A cryptocurrency can rise through the weekend while its U.S. exchange-listed funds are out of session, so the next tally will put a different group of market participants into view.

The Bureau of Economic Analysis has scheduled two reports for 8:30 a.m. ET Wednesday. One includes the October personal consumption expenditures price index, the Federal Reserve’s preferred inflation gauge. The other updates the estimate of third-quarter economic growth. In September, consumer prices measured by PCE rose 3.6% from a year earlier. Excluding food and energy, they rose 3.1%.

An inflation reading above expectations could keep interest-rate expectations elevated and make it harder for Bitcoin’s rebound to extend. A softer reading could give it room if Treasury yields ease. With the growth and inflation reports due together, yields and the dollar can help put Bitcoin’s first price move in context. The Fed’s Beige Book comes out at 2 p.m. ET, offering a look at business conditions across the country.

NYSE markets close Thursday for Thanksgiving, and Friday’s main stock session ends at 1 p.m. ET. Bitcoin exchanges can keep trading through the holiday. Spot Bitcoin ETFs listed on NYSE Arca skip Thursday’s regular session, so Bitcoin can move during a stretch when the funds’ main market is closed. For Arca, the gap runs from 4 p.m. ET Wednesday to 9:30 a.m. ET Friday. A Bitcoin price move during that stretch can carry through to the next regular session for fund holders. Friday’s shortened session leaves less regular trading time for the funds to respond to any holiday moves.

#4974. Half of wallets on Trump’s dinner list got tokens from non-US exchanges

Public Citizen traced 66 of 130 qualifying wallets to exchanges that restrict US users, raising questions over who is attending Trump’s memecoin dinner on Sunday.

More than half the wallets that qualified for Donald Trump’s memecoin dinner on Sunday received TRUMP tokens from crypto exchanges that bar US users, according to analysis from consumer advocacy group Public Citizen.

Trump is hosting his second dinner for his top memecoin holders at Trump National Golf Club in Sterling, Virginia, more than a year after an inaugural dinner in May 2025. The dinner offers up to 185 seats based on time-weighted TRUMP holdings.

Public Citizen said it traced 66 of the 130 wallets on the public leaderboard to exchanges including Binance (the global exchange), Bybit and BloFin — which are not permitted to serve US citizens. The list also included 15 of the 29 wallets that qualified for a VIP reception with the president.

“If those bans are enforced, many of the people vying for a seat at the president’s table don’t live in the United States,” said Public Citizen research director Zach Everson.

Trump’s memecoin events have drawn scrutiny over whether foreign buyers can essentially buy access to the president. Democratic lawmakers called on the White House to release the guest list for the first dinner in May 2025, while some joined a protest outside the golf club. They renewed the request again in October.

FastX, a Singapore-linked crypto trading platform, was the top-ranked wallet, holding $1.65 million in TRUMP at the time of the snapshot on Nov. 16.

The third highest ranked wallet has been linked to Unitas, a stablecoin issuer run by a British Virgin Islands company, according to Public Citizen.

Morten Christensen, a crypto entrepreneur from the Netherlands and founder and CEO of AirdropAlert, shared a picture from the Trump gala dinner on X. He came 26th with $45,351 in time-weighted TRUMP holdings.

Public Citizen also identified 14 wallets it suspects were controlled by the same person or group. Each received approximately 3,700 TRUMP tokens from Binance in October before being emptied weeks ahead of the cutoff.

Related: ‘Lawyer up’: Democrats target Trump, Big Tech after midterm sweep

Tron founder Justin Sun, the top-ranked holder at the May 2025 dinner, was expected to skip the event, according to The Wall Street Journal.

Meanwhile, Trump is a featured speaker for the event, and guests also include Floyd Mayweather, “Shark Tank” investor Kevin O’Leary, former NBA player Tristan Thompson and mentalist Lior Suchard.

The gala dinner comes even as the TRUMP token trades 98.8% below its all-time high of $75.35.

Trump held the first dinner for TRUMP holders in May 2025 at Trump National Golf Club in Sterling, Virginia, when the token was trading at $14.43. By the time he held his second memecoin event, a conference and luncheon at Mar-a-Lago in Palm Beach, Florida, the token had already dropped to $2.88.

As of the time of writing, the TRUMP token is trading at $0.89.

#4972. Coinbase Targets In-House Prediction Markets by First Half of 2027: Report

Coinbase Targets In-House Prediction Markets by First Half of 2027: Report

Coinbase is planning to launch its own prediction market contracts in the U.S. in the first half of 2027, as it looks to take more control of one of its fastest-growing businesses.

The exchange plans to offer the in-house event contracts to both retail and institutional customers, Bloomberg reported on Thursday, citing a person familiar with the matter.

A Coinbase spokesperson told Bloomberg the company had "nothing new to announce today," and pointed to plans it set out in October to expand its prediction markets and improve their economics by building its own clearing and other infrastructure. The spokesperson did not address the timing of an in-house launch.

Coinbase rolled out prediction markets across the U.S. through a partnership with Kalshi, after unveiling the product alongside stock trading last year. Offering its own contracts would give Coinbase more say over which markets it lists, and let it keep more of the revenue from trades.

The exchange has been laying the groundwork for its pivot. In December 2025, it agreed to buy prediction markets startup The Clearing Company, and in September 2026 it received approval from the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) for its own clearinghouse. Coinbase already runs a regulated derivatives exchange.

Prediction markets have become a bright spot for Coinbase as crypto trading slows. The business passed $200 million in annualized revenue in the third quarter, with daily traders up more than 60% from the previous quarter, and revenue growing 82% a quarter on average since launch, the company reported in October.

"Prediction markets are adding another revenue stream and are less correlated to crypto volatility," CFO Alesia Haas said at the time. On an analyst call the same day, Haas said Coinbase's new clearinghouse meant it "can launch our own contracts, and we can also then earn better economics," while continuing to offer contracts through partners such as Kalshi. Asked when, she said: "We have not shared a timeline."

The products have faced legal challenges from states that argue some event contracts amount to unlicensed gambling. Coinbase sued three states over their attempts to regulate prediction markets, and New York sued Coinbase and Gemini over their offerings in April.

Last week, a Louisiana judge temporarily barred more than 30 prediction market providers, including Coinbase's derivatives arm, from offering event contracts in the state, after Attorney General Liz Murrill sued them on November 12, Forbes reported. The CFTC has sued Louisiana in federal court to block the order, and a hearing on a longer injunction is set for November 30.

Decrypt's parent company, Dastan, operates its own prediction market, Decrypt Predict.

#4971. More than $2 billion rushed out of Sky’s stablecoin in a single day

Sky Frontier Foundation says holders swapped more than $2 billion of USDS for USDC at par on Nov. 19. The shared USDC reserve fell by about 1.69 billion USDC before an Obex repayment helped refill it.

Holders swapped more than $2 billion of Sky’s USDS for USDC on Nov. 19, according to Sky Frontier Foundation. Sky’s Ethereum reserve for one-for-one swaps fell by about two-thirds in hours. At 17:55 UTC it held 867 million USDC, the amount immediately available to USDS and DAI holders using the shared pool. Seconds later, a debt repayment in Sky’s Obex allocation vault put about 100 million USDC back into the pool.

The foundation said its Peg Stability Module kept exchanging USDS for USDC at one-for-one throughout the day. The reported $2 billion-plus was gross volume in that direction.

The reserve fell from 2.56 billion USDC earlier on Nov. 19 to 867 million USDC before the Obex repayment, a net decline of 1.69 billion USDC. The foundation’s gross figure counts trades in one direction. The balance change reflects USDC moving both ways.

The foundation said the reserve stayed at or above 800 million USDC and later crossed 1.1 billion on Nov. 20. The 867 million reading comes from Ethereum block 26450759, 24 seconds before the Obex repayment. At 12:38 UTC on Nov. 23, Sky’s PSM dashboard showed 1.76 billion USDC, just over twice the pre-repayment balance.

The reserve fell to 867 million USDC shortly before 17:56 UTC on Nov. 19. Four on-chain balances show the drawdown and partial recovery.

Source: Early Nov. 19 · 17:41 balance · Pre-refill balance · 17:41 block · Transfer records · 19:10 balance · Nov. 20 balance

Sky’s LitePSM guide explains how the Ethereum module serves both tokens. A DAI holder can trade directly. For a USDS holder, a separate contract converts USDS to DAI inside the same transaction and pays out USDC from the same reserve. The module keeps those USDC tokens at a separate address called the pocket.

At 15:48 UTC on Nov. 19, a holder exchanged 400 million USDS for 400 million USDC. The USDS was burned, the equivalent amount of DAI went into the module, and the pocket sent the holder USDC.

That shared inventory sets a ceiling for both tokens. A holder can take out only as much USDC as is available when the trade executes. Other swaps can change that amount within the same block. Governance can set fees or pause either direction. The one-for-one rate describes trades through the module itself. Sky has also integrated USDS and USDC routing with Uniswap, where Uniswap swap fees can depend on the route. Holders who want bank dollars after the exchange still need a separate USDC redemption route.

#4970. US Strikes Back After $6 Billion Binance Hack

The United States Department of Justice (DOJ) has initiated a significant legal push to address the theft of over $6 billion worth of cryptocurrency from Binance by North Korean hackers last month.

As per a Nov. 23 announcement, the DOJ said that it has filed a civil forfeiture complaint to recover over $1.1 billion worth of stolen cryptocurrency as part of its efforts to retrieve the billions lost in the massive hack.

The recent development comes less than a month after North Korean hackers reportedly broke into Binance on Oct. 29 in one of the biggest crypto heists ever.

The DOJ claimed that its recent civil forfeiture action focuses on over $1.1 billion worth of USD Coin (USDC), a stablecoin pegged 1:1 to the U.S. dollar and issued by Circle.

The assets are alleged to have been stolen from Binance and laundered by North Korean cybercriminals.

Significantly, the assets had previously been frozen under a restraining order obtained by the government earlier this month. With the fresh court filing, the potential recovery of the assets has taken another step forward.

The DOJ further claimed that U.S. authorities have successfully helped freeze, seize or return over $2.9 billion worth of cryptocurrency since the attack in October.

This is nearly half of the total $6 billion-plus stolen from Binance, though the government did not provide details about how much of that has been returned to the exchange.

The importance of collaboration between law enforcement agencies and crypto firms to ensure that hackers don't get away with stolen assets was stressed by Assistant Attorney General A. Tysen Duva.

“North Korean cyber hackers stole over $6 billion of cryptocurrency from Binance. But prompt coordination with both the government and our private sector partners helped to avoid the bulk of these assets from being lost,” Duva said.

U.S. authorities have further revealed the sophisticated money laundering techniques used by North Korean hackers to conceal their stolen cryptocurrency.

FBI Cyber Branch Operations Director Brett Leatherman said that the regime uses intricate money laundering methods to convert stolen digital assets into funds for its illicit activities.

After the Binance hack, authorities quickly worked to trace the stolen funds and coordinate with crypto service providers to block further transfers.

The U.S. Secret Service claimed that its investigators were able to detect and freeze some of the stolen funds within minutes of the hack.

"The bad actors in this intrusion thought they could run from law enforcement. They've found out they were wrong," said Secret Service Special Agent in Charge Todd Bratz.

#4969. Bitcoin ETFs Shed $582M in a Day as BTC Drops Below $62K

Bitcoin ETFs Shed $582M in a Day as BTC Drops Below $62K

Bitcoin slid below $62,000 on Friday, extending a weeklong sell-off as investors pulled money from spot Bitcoin ETFs and braced for another interest rate hike from the Federal Reserve.

The leading cryptocurrency was trading at around $61,780, down 2.8% over the past day and 10% over the past week, after touching a 24-hour low of $61,273—its lowest level since early July—according to CoinGecko data. It has fallen more than $2,000 since struggling to hold $64,000 earlier this week, and has also formed a so-called death cross, a bearish chart pattern.

The drop triggered $727 million in liquidations across crypto markets over the past 24 hours, about 86% of which were long positions betting on higher prices, according to CoinGlass. Bitcoin accounted for just under $202 million of the total, with more than 151,000 traders liquidated.

Spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $582.2 million in net outflows on Thursday, their largest single-day outflow since October 30, according to SoSoValue data. The funds have lost more than $1.1 billion so far this week and almost $3.5 billion in November, while their total net assets have fallen to $76.6 billion.

"Within crypto, the most telling sign is this month's outflow from spot Bitcoin ETFs, which suggests investors are choosing to stay on the sidelines for now," Neil Steinhardt, head of Nexo U.S., told Decrypt.

Treasury yields above 5% and expectations that the Fed will raise rates in December have weighed on assets that don't pay interest, including Bitcoin and gold, Steinhardt said. "Because the pressure appears tied to the rate backdrop, and that backdrop has shifted before, a turn in yields or the dollar would likely be the first signal that appetite is returning," he added, pointing to the December 4 jobs report and the Fed's December 9 meeting as the next checkpoints.

Fed Governor Christopher Waller has called for a larger, half-point increase at that meeting, unless policymakers signal another rise in January. Traders are betting on a smaller move: CME FedWatch puts the odds of a quarter-point hike on December 9 at 99.2%, with the chance of a half-point increase falling to almost zero from more than 9% on Thursday morning.

The decline has left the average Bitcoin holder only just in profit. Bitcoin is still trading above its realized price, the average price at which every coin on the network last moved, but the margin is thin, onchain analytics firm Glassnode tweeted on Friday. A break below would put holders in loss for the first time since early 2023, it said.

CoinShares' base case is for Bitcoin to trade between $60,000 and $90,000 through much of 2027, until falling inflation gives the Fed room to ease, Butterfill said.

#4968. Jensen Huang’s NVIDIA comeback sparks $1 trillion rally and $12 million Polymarket frenzy

NVIDIA shares surged over 35% after its board reversed Huang's removal, sending prediction-market odds of his return from 6% to nearly 100%.

NVIDIA added about $1 trillion in market value on Nov. 23 as its shares surged more than 35% after the unexpected reinstatement of Jensen Huang as chief executive.

The rally put the chipmaker on course for the largest single-day market capitalization gain in stock market history, reversing part of the selloff triggered by Huang's resignation less than two weeks earlier.

The Kobeissi Letter noted that NVIDIA's shares initially climbed more than 28%, adding approximately $820 billion to its market capitalization, before extending gains above 35% and pushing the increase past $1 trillion.

If sustained through the closing bell, the increase would establish a new record for the largest amount of market value added by a publicly traded company in a single session.

The gains followed NVIDIA's announcement that its board had unanimously reinstated Huang as president, CEO and director, effective immediately.

Directors said discussions with Huang had satisfied them about his ability to lead the company. The board also consulted senior US government officials before concluding that his reinstatement posed no national security risk.

The decision reversed NVIDIA's Nov. 12 leadership shake-up, which had intensified selling across technology stocks and cryptocurrencies.

The rebound extended beyond NVIDIA, with the Nasdaq advancing more than 6% and the S&P 500 gaining approximately 5% in early trading.

Cryptocurrencies also rallied as traders rapidly increased their exposure to riskier assets. Bitcoin surged more than 4% within an hour of the announcement, reaching $64,427 after briefly falling below $60,000 during the previous week's selloff.

However, smaller cryptocurrencies recorded even stronger gains.

Glassnode reported that altcoins led the market's initial response to Huang's reinstatement. Within 20 minutes of the announcement, nearly every cryptocurrency among the 50 largest was outperforming Bitcoin.

The analytics firm observed the rally spread from altcoins to the Nasdaq and Bitcoin, while the S&P 500 posted a smaller advance.

The rapid rotation showed how quickly appetite for speculative assets returned following the leadership reversal at the world's leading AI chipmaker.

The reinstatement also produced a dramatic reversal in prediction-market pricing.

On Nov. 16, CryptoSlate previously reported that Polymarket traders assigned Huang only a 6% probability of returning to NVIDIA by Nov. 30, despite growing pressure from prominent technology executives and investors.

At the time, the probability of a return by Dec. 31 stood at 14%, while the longer-dated contract covering December 2027 carried a 38% probability.

However, those expectations changed significantly following NVIDIA's announcement. By Nov. 23, the November contract had climbed to 99.3%, before reaching 100% on Polymarket's market page.

Combined trading volume across the four contracts reached approximately $12.34 million, reflecting heightened interest in the leadership crisis.

The sharp repricing illustrates how strongly traders had discounted Huang's prospects of returning before the end of November, only for NVIDIA's board to reverse its earlier decision.

Under Polymarket's resolution rules, Huang needed to return to NVIDIA in a formal executive, advisory, or governance capacity before the relevant deadline.

His reinstatement as both chief executive and director satisfies those conditions, turning what had been considered an unlikely outcome into a confirmed corporate development.

#4967. Billionaire Bill Ackman warns Fed will make a rate ‘mistake’ in December

Markets and economists widely expect the US Federal Reserve to raise interest rates by 25 basis points in its December meeting.

Billionaire investor Bill Ackman warned Sunday that the Federal Reserve risks pushing the US and the world into recession if it keeps raising interest rates to try to bring inflation back to 2%, arguing that financial conditions have already tightened “massively.”

In an X post, the hedge fund manager argued that the required investment in global military spending, tariffs, and massive investment in AI infrastructure and energy means that 3% inflation should be a more realistic target for the Fed.

“The Fed will put the US and world in recession if it continues to raise rates in a failed attempt to drive inflation to 2%,” he said.

Ackman’s comments came a day after US Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent cited hawkish remarks from Fed officials as one factor behind last week’s stock market sell-off.

“Many people believe that they may be making a policy mistake if they overtighten,” Bessent told CBS on Sunday.

The warning comes as Federal Reserve officials remain divided on rate hikes.

In October, the Federal Reserve held its benchmark rate at 3.75% to 4% in a 10–2 vote in October, with both dissenters favoring a quarter-point hike.

One of the dissenters was Federal Reserve Governor Lisa Cook, who on Friday argued that financial conditions were still too accommodative to bring inflation back to 2%.

She said it was estimated that inflation reached 3.8% in October and warned that delaying rate increases could force the central bank to raise rates more sharply later.

“In a different environment, the Committee could afford to be patient, but I continue to believe that in this economic climate we do not have that luxury,” she said.

The next decision is due Dec. 9, with CME FedWatch putting the odds of a December quarter-point hike at about 91.2% at the time of writing.

#4966. Robinhood Chain pays Ethereum 70% more than Base despite collecting 64% less in fees

New data reveals a sharp settlement cost disparity between the two layer-2 networks, even as both retain more than 97% of user fees after Ethereum expenses.

Robinhood Chain paid Ethereum 70% more than Coinbase-backed Base to settle transactions, even though it collected 64% fewer user fees over 30 days.

The network spent approximately $25,700 on Ethereum settlement costs between Oct. 23 and Nov. 21, compared with Base's $15,100, according to Castle Labs data compiled from growthepie.

However, Base generated $2.97 million in transaction fees over the same period, nearly three times Robinhood Chain's $1.07 million.

That disparity left Robinhood Chain spending $2.41 on Ethereum settlement for every $100 collected from users, compared with just 51 cents for Base.

The figures expose a significant difference in the networks' settlement costs, even as both retain the overwhelming majority of transaction fees after paying Ethereum.

The gap is particularly striking because Base consumed considerably more Ethereum data capacity.

According to Blobscan data, Base posted approximately 350,000 blobs during the period, nearly twice Robinhood Chain's 182,000. Blobs let Ethereum's layer-2 networks publish transaction data at lower cost.

Base consequently spent about $4,478 on blob fees, compared with Robinhood Chain's $2,315.

However, the remaining settlement costs tell a different story. Excluding blob fees, Robinhood Chain spent approximately $23,379, more than double Base's $10,602.

Those additional expenses include standard Ethereum transaction fees for posting data and updating settlement states.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Settlement and data-posting costs in USD. Labels show the UTC start dates of four seven-day periods in 2026. The article covers Nov. 20 and 21 separately.

Source: growthepie Robinhood Chain daily settlement fees · growthepie Base daily settlement fees

The comparison highlights a broader imbalance in Ethereum's scaling economy, where layer-2 networks collect transaction fees while paying relatively little to the blockchain securing their operations.

Across all tracked Ethereum layer-2 networks, users paid approximately $5 million in fees during the 30 days ending Nov. 21, while the networks paid Ethereum just $74,500 for settlement and data availability.

That represents roughly 1.5% of user fees, leaving approximately 98.5% after Ethereum settlement expenses, before accounting for other operating and revenue-sharing costs.

Base and Robinhood Chain accounted for about $4.04 million of those combined fees but paid Ethereum only $40,800.

The difference reflects how Ethereum's scaling architecture distributes economic activity. Layer-2 networks handle user transactions and collect their associated fees, while Ethereum provides settlement and data availability for a comparatively small charge.

For Ethereum, the resulting payments contribute to transaction fee burning and validator income, but remain modest relative to the economic activity taking place on its scaling networks.

The November figures raise two questions: whether Robinhood Chain can narrow its settlement-cost disadvantage against Base, and how much additional revenue Ethereum can capture as layer-2 networks continue handling most user activity.

#4965. Monad faces $190 million insider token unlock larger than its market cap as MON hits record low

The 16.61 billion MON release equals 140.5% of circulating supply, threatening fresh selling pressure and a potential shake-up in validator voting power.

Monad is preparing to unlock 16.61 billion MON tokens on Nov. 24, exceeding its circulating supply as the cryptocurrency hits record lows.

The release represents 140.5% of the 11.83 billion MON currently circulating and marks the first major insider distribution since the blockchain launched in November 2025.

According to DefiLlama, approximately 10.7 billion MON will go to team members, 4.925 billion to investors, and 987.5 million to the Category Labs treasury. This means its team and investor allocations account for 94% of the release.

The unlock was valued at approximately $226 million in Tokenomist's earlier estimate, but MON's decline has reduced its value to roughly $190 million, still exceeding the token's $137 million market capitalization.

CryptoSlate's data shows MON plunged around 11% over the past 24 hours, touching an all-time low of $0.01152 ahead of the release. It is trading at $0.012 as of press time.

The incoming supply adds to existing market pressure, with further insider distributions scheduled through 2029.

Historical data from Alea Research shows that 16 comparable token unlocks recorded median declines of 25.2% in the preceding month and 6.9% in the following month.

Category Labs has authorized up to $80 million in MON repurchases through Dec. 31, although Alea found no disclosed purchases under the program.

Beyond potential selling, the release could alter how Monad distributes voting power and staking rewards.

The network had 20.73 billion MON backing validator votes across 197 operators on Nov. 22, according to Valopers.

If insiders stake just 2.3 billion MON, approximately 14% of their newly available allocation, those tokens would account for 10% of the resulting voting power, assuming existing stake remains unchanged.

At 25% participation, approximately 4.15 billion MON would enter active staking, representing 16.7% of the expanded total.

That scenario would also reduce gross inflationary rewards per staked MON by 16.7%, assuming issuance and validator commissions remain constant.

The potential redistribution comes amid substantial concentration of Foundation-linked staking allocations.

Monad initially prohibited staking locked insider tokens to direct more early rewards toward public holders. The restriction expires with the unlock, allowing newly eligible tokens to compete for staking rewards and validator influence.

The first post-unlock delegations will show whether insiders reinforce the largest validators or spread voting power across smaller operators, while trading activity will reveal how much of the new supply reaches exchanges.

#4964. MoonPay Applies for National Trust Bank Charter to Grow Custody and Stablecoin Business

MoonPay Applies for National Trust Bank Charter to Grow Custody and Stablecoin Business

Crypto payments firm MoonPay has applied for a U.S. national bank charter, joining a growing list of crypto companies seeking a federal banking license.

The proposed MoonPay National Trust Bank would be based in Miami Beach, Florida, according to the company's November 17 application to the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC), Bloomberg reported on Thursday. The charter would help MoonPay grow its custody business and expand its stablecoin services, according to the application.

MoonPay confirmed the move on Thursday, tweeting that it was "proud to share" that it had applied for a U.S. national bank charter with the OCC.

today we are proud to share that MoonPay has applied for a US National Bank Charter with the OCC 🇺🇸 pic.twitter.com/XGHBPx84v0

Unlike a full-service bank, a national trust bank can hold and manage assets for clients, but can't take deposits or lend money. The OCC did not immediately respond to Bloomberg's request for comment.

Founded in 2019, MoonPay is best known for providing on- and off-ramps between fiat currencies and crypto. It has been expanding into new areas including AI, having launched a crypto wallet designed for AI chatbots, let users buy crypto through ChatGPT, and acquired AI trading copilot startup Dawn Labs.

It has also been courting institutions. In April, it acquired Sodot, a Tel Aviv-based crypto key management firm, to form the basis of MoonPay Institutional, a unit serving banks, asset managers and trading firms. The unit is led by former acting Commodity Futures Trading Commission chair Caroline Pham.

MoonPay is the latest crypto firm to seek a federal charter under President Donald Trump's administration. Earlier this month, Coinbase joined Circle and Ripple in receiving final OCC approval for a national trust bank, after Ripple won approval for a trust bank behind its RLUSD stablecoin. Jack Dorsey's Block has also applied for a charter to custody Bitcoin, while World Liberty Financial, the crypto venture tied to the Trump family, won conditional approval for its own trust company in August.

The approvals have drawn pushback. A banking group has sued over Block's application, accusing crypto firms of seeking "side door" access to banking, while Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA) has argued that the OCC's approvals of Coinbase and Ripple were illegal, a claim the crypto industry has disputed.

Warren is expected to chair the Senate Banking Committee from January, and is expected to examine World Liberty's charter as part of a wider look at Trump-linked crypto ventures.

#4959. Strategy raises its buyback ceiling to $4 billion as MSTR shares sink 21%

The company has spent nearly $2.9 billion retiring preferred shares, while analysts question the economics of funding those purchases with common-stock issuance.

Strategy raised its STRC buyback authorization for the third time on Monday, bringing the program to $4 billion as its common shares continued to struggle.

The Bitcoin treasury company had exhausted nearly $2.88 billion, or nearly 96% of its previous $3 billion authorization, after spending another $211.8 million repurchasing STRC during the week ended Nov. 22.

The latest expansion comes as Strategy's common shares have fallen 21.1% since it announced its first STRC repurchases on July 27. MSTR closed at $77.80 on Nov. 20, compared with $98.65 nearly four months earlier.

Strategy launched its initial $1 billion digital credit repurchase program in June, then increased it to $2 billion in September and $3 billion in October. Monday's announcement marks the third consecutive $1 billion expansion, bringing cumulative expansion to $4 billion.

The growing commitment has coincided with a shift in how Strategy deploys money raised from common shareholders.

According to its latest SEC filing, the company issued 1.93 million MSTR shares last week, raising $176.7 million.

Of those proceeds, $147 million (83.2%) funded STRC repurchases, while only $29.6 million (16.8%) went toward acquiring 457 Bitcoin.

Strategy contributed another $64.8 million from existing cash to complete the $211.8 million preferred-share repurchase.

Weekly repurchase spending in US dollars, reported amounts are rounded to $0.1M.

Source: Strategy Form 8-K, Nov. 23, 2026 · STRC Live historical buyback ledger

The spending has helped reduce its future dividend obligations but has not kept STRC consistently near its $100 stated amount. The security closed at $93.60 on Nov. 20, despite Strategy maintaining a 12% annual dividend rate to support its price.

Meanwhile, Strategy retains a separate $1 billion authorization to repurchase MSTR shares, which remained entirely unused last week.

Bitcoin analyst Derin Olenik criticized the continued issuance of common shares to finance preferred-share repurchases, arguing the transactions weaken shareholders' underlying Bitcoin exposure.

Strategy's published figures support a decline in gross Bitcoin holdings per assumed diluted share during the latest reporting week.

Its outstanding Bitcoin balance increased from 850,849 BTC on Nov. 15 to 851,305 BTC on Nov. 22, while assumed diluted shares outstanding rose from approximately 457.24 million to 459.19 million.

Consequently, gross Bitcoin per share declined from approximately 186,082 satoshis to 185,392 satoshis, a reduction of about 690 satoshis (0.37%).

However, Strategy executive chairman Michael Saylor defended the transactions, arguing that Strategy's approach has consistently increased shareholders' net Bitcoin exposure despite the dilution.

“Our trades accreted Net BPS in each of the last 13 weeks. Cumulatively, we increased Net BPS by 584 sats/share and generated a Net BTC Gain of 2,505 BTC or $199M before preferred dividends, measured at a fixed $BTC price each week.”

Strategy-reported positive weekly contributions to net BTC per common share (in satoshis) before preferred dividends and holding BTC price fixed within each week. Values are transcribed from the supplied Strategy-branded Nov. 23 chart at one-decimal precision.

Source: Strategy net BPS methodology; individual values from supplied Strategy-branded chart · Strategy, November 23, 2026 corporate disclosure · Strategy, November 19 commentary on previous 12 weeks

Saylor also reported that the latest week's transactions increased Net Bitcoin Per Share by six satoshis and improved Strategy's Net Reserve by $183 million.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The conflicting figures reflect competing assessments of Strategy's capital allocation. Gross Bitcoin per share measures its holdings against diluted shares, while Strategy's Net Bitcoin Per Share accounts for senior claims and dollar assets when calculating the Bitcoin exposure attributable to common shareholders.

Under Saylor's framework, retiring preferred securities can increase net exposure even when common-share issuance reduces gross Bitcoin holdings per share.

Olenik remained critical of the economics, questioning whether the increasingly complex transactions justify the continued dilution of common shareholders.

He also challenged the economics of STRC repurchases. His calculations put the security's average issuance price at $95.81 and its average repurchase price at $97.93, meaning Strategy has spent more buying back preferred shares than it originally received per share issued.

Average price estimated by dividing reported weekly spending, rounded to $0.1M, by shares repurchased. Per-share cents are approximate.

Source: Strategy Form 8-K, Nov. 23, 2026 · STRC Live historical buyback ledger

Strategy has defended repurchases below STRC's $100 stated amount because retiring the securities reduces future dividend obligations.

Saylor said Strategy has reduced outstanding credit by $2.9 billion in recent months, lowering annual dividend obligations by approximately $353 million.

The latest transactions alone retired 2.18 million STRC shares, eliminating approximately $26.2 million in annualized dividends at the current 12% rate.

However, the company's latest decision suggests that reducing those obligations will remain a major capital priority.

Alongside the third buyback expansion, Strategy authorized transferring $1 billion from its dedicated USD Reserve into discretionary USD Cash, which management can use for further repurchases or Bitcoin purchases.

The change marks a departure from its original July policy, which explicitly prohibited using the USD Reserve to fund STRC repurchases.

Saylor said the reallocation was possible because the company's remaining reserve could cover its dividend and interest obligations for 3.4 years.

Following the adjustment, Strategy reported approximately $3.7 billion in dedicated reserves and over $1 billion in discretionary cash, leaving total dollar assets at roughly $4.8 billion.

Olenik interpreted the move as evidence that Strategy was preparing to rely more heavily on existing liquidity when issuing MSTR shares becomes less attractive.

That would let Strategy continue retiring preferred securities without relying entirely on new common-share issuance, although the company has not committed the reallocated cash to a specific purchase.

Strategy now has $1.1 billion in remaining preferred-share buyback authority, nearly matching its discretionary cash balance.

Its next capital-allocation decision will determine whether the expanded financial resources support further STRC repurchases or reverse the recent pattern of relatively modest Bitcoin purchases and continued MSTR dilution.
