#4958. Morning Minute: Anthropic IPO Pops, Sinks Markets

Morning Minute: Anthropic IPO Pops, Sinks Markets

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

The largest base IPO deal ever priced at $83 a share Wednesday night, opened Thursday morning at $111.50, and briefly pushed Anthropic’s fully diluted valuation past $2 trillion. The stock finished at $97.17, up 17% from the offer and down 13% from where it opened (it has since fallen about 15% to $88).

A 17% first-day gain on an $81.34 billion raise is a successful listing by any normal standard.

The rest of the market did not fare as well.

The Nasdaq fell 2.06%. The S&P 500 dropped 1.9%. The Dow lost 2.17% and closed below 50,000 for the first time since June. Tech had rallied before the bell on the listing and reversed by the afternoon.

MD fell 7.73%. Nebius dropped 7.8%. CoreWeave lost 5%. Nvidia slid 3.4% despite beating earnings last week. Oracle, Micron, Broadcom and Microsoft all finished lower. The biggest AI listing in history happened, and AI infrastructure was the worst place to be holding.

Joe Maher at Capital Economics put it plainly: “Anthropic’s record-breaking IPO has failed to rekindle enthusiasm for tech stocks.”

Crypto went with it. Bitcoin fell to $61,500, its lowest since early July. That’s a seventh straight weekly loss, which would be the longest run since the nine-week stretch in 2022. Eth hit the $1,750 level, down 28% in thirty days. Gold fell to a fourteen-month low. Silver dropped 3%.

When Bitcoin, equities and gold all sell off on the same session, the common factor is usually the discount rate.

That’s the Fed. Chris Waller spent this week making the case for fifty basis points in December, and the minutes showed he isn’t alone. FxPro’s Alex Kuptsikevich described the market as spooked by “the spectre of a double rate hike” and pointed out Bitcoin is now more than 7% below its 200-week moving average, the deepest it has traded below that line in three years.

So where does this leave us heading into next week?

Start with what an $81 billion IPO actually does to a market. That money came from somewhere. Add the separate $26 billion private placement from Nvidia, Google, AMD and four other suppliers, and roughly $107 billion of capital got committed to a single name in one day. Kuptsikevich raised the possibility that the listing absorbed the riskiest money in the system, and that if so, some of it rotates back out once the allocation settles.

That’s the bull case for crypto over the next few weeks. It requires the Fed to cooperate.

For the bear case, one can simply look at SpaceX.

SpaceX priced at $135 in June, opened at $150, and closed its first day up 19%. Then it kept running, hitting $225.64 four days later, up 67% from the offer. The unwind started the following session. By June 22 it had fallen 16% in a single day and sat 31% off the high. By mid-July it traded below its IPO price. By late July it was down roughly 50% from the peak.

That would project out to a market low around Christmas time, which is actually how the 2018 midterm cycle played out. Will history repeat itself? We will find out soon enough. In the interim, expect volatility.

#4956. Bitcoin’s supply squeeze narrative faces scrutiny after 11,000 BTC moves to Kraken

Coinbase and Binance sent large amounts of Bitcoin to Kraken during the downturn, even as combined exchange reserves declined.

More than 11,000 BTC moved from Coinbase and Binance to Kraken during Saturday's selloff, complicating Bitcoin's supply-squeeze narrative.

The five transactions, identified by Ask The Tape (ATT), came as major exchanges recorded hundreds of millions of dollars in net Bitcoin withdrawals, fueling expectations that fewer coins were available for trading.

However, the transfers revealed substantial movements between trading venues, with Bitcoin leaving some exchanges only to replenish reserves elsewhere.

Coinbase accounted for approximately 4,028 BTC across two transactions. The first delivered 2,177.79 BTC to Kraken-tagged addresses at 03:47 UTC, followed by another 1,850 BTC at 10:04 UTC.

Binance then transferred 7,000 BTC through three transactions: two transfers of 2,000 BTC each and a third involving 3,000 BTC.

The movements coincided with substantial differences in exchange flows reported by CryptoQuant.

Binance recorded $680 million in net Bitcoin withdrawals on Nov. 21, while Coinbase and Bybit registered $373 million and $103 million, respectively. Together, the three exchanges lost approximately $1.16 billion.

Kraken moved in the opposite direction, receiving $478 million in net inflows and offsetting 41% of those withdrawals.

That left the four platforms with a combined net outflow of $678 million, even as substantial amounts of Bitcoin circulated between their wallets.

Kraken also recorded significant outgoing transactions that day, including one involving approximately 4,028 BTC, illustrating the rapid movement of liquidity across exchanges during the downturn.

The exchange movements have become particularly significant as Bitcoin struggles to recover from its recent decline below $60,000.

Although declining exchange reserves can reduce the amount of Bitcoin immediately available for sale, sustained buying demand remains essential to turning that scarcity into upward price pressure.

That demand has weakened considerably among US institutional investors.

According to Farside Investors, US spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $1.7 billion in net withdrawals last week, extending a period of heavy redemptions that has stripped billions of dollars from the funds this month.

While Bitcoin subsequently recovered above $61,000 on Nov. 23, the ETF withdrawals have yet to show a corresponding recovery in institutional appetite.

The next test arrives Wednesday, when the US Bureau of Economic Analysis publishes October's personal consumption expenditures inflation report alongside its second estimate of third-quarter economic growth.

Both releases are scheduled for 8:30 a.m. ET and could influence expectations for the Federal Reserve's December interest rate decision.

Stronger inflation could push Treasury yields and the dollar higher, adding pressure to Bitcoin at a time when ETF investors are withdrawing capital.

That leaves Bitcoin's recovery dependent on two developments: exchange reserves continuing to decline across trading venues and fresh buying demand returning to absorb available supply.

If ETF withdrawals persist while Bitcoin continues circulating between exchanges, the apparent supply squeeze may offer little support against another wave of selling.

Oluwapelumi values Bitcoin's potential. He imparts insights on a range of topics like DeFi, hacks, mining and culture, underlining transformative power.

#4954. Senate Passes Bill Banning Prediction Market Bets on War, Terrorism and Death

Senate Passes Bill Banning Prediction Market Bets on War, Terrorism and Death

The Senate has passed a bill that would ban prediction markets from offering bets on war, terrorism, assassinations and deaths, sending the measure to the House of Representatives.

The Discouraging Exploitative Assassination, Tragedy, and Harm Betting in Event Trading Systems Act, or DEATH BETS Act, passed the Senate by voice vote on Wednesday, November 19, with an amendment, according to Congress.gov. The bill was introduced by Sen. Adam Schiff (D-CA) in March, and is cosponsored by Sen. John Curtis (R-UT).

"With this bill, Congress is making it clear that these bets should be illegal," Schiff tweeted after the vote.

BREAKING: The Senate just passed my bill with @SenJohnCurtis to prohibit prediction markets from listing contracts involving terrorism, assassination, war, military operations, or an individual’s death.

With this bill, Congress is making it clear that these bets should be… pic.twitter.com/Mzu5PWmGsL

— Senator Adam Schiff (@SenAdamSchiff) November 20, 2026

The bill would amend the Commodity Exchange Act to bar exchanges and clearinghouses registered with the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) from listing or clearing event contracts that expressly reference terrorism, assassination, war or U.S. military operations.

It would also ban contracts that, at the time of listing, are "reasonably expected to resolve principally based on a death of an individual." The CFTC would be able to extend the ban to "any similar activity" by rule.

The bill would not affect the status of other types of contracts covered by existing CFTC rules on event contracts, such as those involving gaming, according to its text.

Prediction markets such as Kalshi and Polymarket let users trade on the outcome of real-world events, from elections and sports to geopolitics. Markets on wars and the fates of world leaders have drawn criticism from lawmakers, who have argued they let people profit from tragedy, and could reward insiders with knowledge of military plans. Polymarket users have been accused of insider trading after making $2.4 million on Iran-related predictions. Polymarket pulled a market on whether a nuclear weapon would be detonated after a public backlash.

Senate Democrats have raised concerns that people close to President Donald Trump could profit from war-related markets, and one senator has alleged White House corruption over Iran war predictions. Kalshi was also sued after refusing to pay out on a market tied to the death of Iran's then-Supreme Leader, Ali Khamenei.

The CFTC has also moved to curb such markets. In June, it proposed rules that would ban markets on outcomes that could come about through war or assassination, such as vaguely worded bets on when U.S. adversaries would be "out of office," which Kalshi and Polymarket had used to sidestep existing bans on war-related contracts.

War-related markets have also appeared elsewhere, including on Myriad, the prediction market operated by Decrypt's parent company DASTAN, where, where users traded on whether U.S. troops would enter Iran.

The bill's passage comes as prediction markets face growing legal pressure from states, which argue that some event contracts amount to unlicensed gambling. New York, for example, has sued Coinbase and Gemini over their prediction markets. The CFTC, which first sued three states in April to defend its authority over the markets, has now taken 12 states to court, most recently Louisiana.

#4948. GPT-6 Astra and Opus 5.5 expose a blind spot in AI oversight

In a METR exercise, Opus 5.5 scored GPT-6 Astra’s proposed AI tests about 0.2 points higher on a 0–1 scale than a human scorer did. A gap that large could affect which tests get pursued.

In METR’s test of AI oversight, Opus 5.5 gave GPT-6 Astra’s proposed AI tests higher scores than the human evaluator did. METR said the gap could change whether its scoring rules recommended pursuing a test. In a separate exercise, leading AI agents placed ahead of most human entrants across 15 past programming contests.

Across Al Zimmermann’s programming contests, METR reported median placements from the 96th to the 98th percentile for Fable 5.1, Opus 5.5 and GPT-6 Astra. About four people still beat the strongest models in the median contest.

The agents had 48 hours per contest on a 28-core machine. Each submission gave them a numerical score they could use to see whether their changes improved the result. Human contestants typically had about three calendar months, used their own hardware and could see the public standings. METR compared the agents’ scores with the original contest results.

In a separate exercise, METR asked agents to design tests that would help researchers assess what AI models could do. AI judges and a human scorer had access to project files, scores from trial runs and the agents’ case for why their tests were relevant and feasible. The human scorer assessed whether each proposed test was worth including in METR’s suite. METR said none of the designs was useful enough to include in its report.

The AI judges tended to agree more closely with the human scorer when a weaker model had designed the test. When the model that designed the test was as capable as the judge or stronger, the judges tended to rate the work more highly than the human did. Opus 5.5 agreed most closely with the human scorer, yet rated GPT-6 Astra’s proposals about 0.2 points higher on average on a scale from zero to one.

The comparison came from one exercise, and what counted as useful depended in part on METR’s research priorities. METR said models underused some resources in the judgment tests. It also reported strong model performance on some longer machine-learning tasks with automatic scoring. A separate AI peer review audit tested whether reviewers’ objections changed the findings by running code on the underlying data.

The record was $2.61 billion in the week ended Feb. 28, 2025, according to SoSoValue data. The week ended June 26 ranked second, with about $2 million more in net outflows than last week, based on the reported daily figures.

Investors withdrew a net $4.34 billion over four consecutive weeks, bringing year-to-date net outflows to roughly $3.8 billion.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT), the largest US spot Bitcoin ETF by assets, accounted for $1.37 billion, or about 76% of last week’s net outflows.

On Thursday, IBIT recorded $541.1 million in outflows, surpassing its previous daily high of $528.3 million on Jan. 30.

The fund broke that record again on Friday, with $544.7 million in net outflows, bringing the two-day total to $1.09 billion.

Monthly spot Bitcoin ETF flows. Source: SoSoValue

#4946. Bitcoin Breaks Below $60K as Crypto Fear Index Hits Worst Level Since February

Bitcoin Breaks Below $60K as Crypto Fear Index Hits Worst Level Since February

Bitcoin broke below $60,000 on Friday, plunging through a major psychological price level as the selloff gathered pace after Anthropic’s stock market debut.

The daily chart shows BTC at $59,703.72 , down 3.55% for the session after touching a low of $59,300.

The Fear and Greed Index chart dipped to 9, a reading classified as “extreme fear” and its lowest since February. For reference, the index was at 18 a week ago and a neutral 51 a month earlier.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Bitcoin with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The indicator tracks crypto market sentiment on a scale from zero, or maximum fear, to 100, or extreme greed, using market data rather than individual investor surveys. Its 2026 low was 5 on February 5, compared with a yearly high of 82 on August 26.

The selling went well beyond Bitcoin. Ethereum fell 4.24%, Solana lost 6.36%, BNB slid 4.38% and XRP declined 5.53% over the same period.

Forced selling added to the pressure. As Decrypt reported earlier Friday, roughly $727 million in leveraged crypto positions had been liquidated over 24 hours, about 86% of them bullish bets, while U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs lost $582.2 million on Thursday.

Spot fund withdrawals did not begin with Anthropic’s IPO. By Thursday, roughly $2.9 billion in U.S. Bitcoin ETF outflows for November, alongside higher Treasury yields and expectations of tighter Federal Reserve policy.

Anthropic made its Nasdaq debut Thursday at $111.50, up 34% from the $83 IPO price, before closing at $97.17. Friday brought another decline for the Claude developer, with the stock almost losing all the prime it gained after its IPO.

The listing raised $81.34 billion through the public offering, but enthusiasm for Anthropic shares did not carry over to the companies building the AI boom.

Friday’s market heatmap shows tech stock suffering in the middle of mostly green pastures. Nvidia down 3.78%, Broadcom is down 5.65%, AMD declining 5.06% and memory-chip maker Micron dropping 6.51%. Meta fell 2.21%, adding to pressure on companies spending heavily on AI infrastructure.

Other major stocks were doing better. Alphabet rose 2.81%, Microsoft gained 0.50% and Apple edged up 0.25%, while healthcare stocks Eli Lilly and Johnson & Johnson rose 1.21% and 1.78%, respectively. Walmart added 2.91% and Visa climbed 1.34%.

But it was not a uniform crash across the S&P 500. The tech-heavy Nasdaq was falling more sharply than the broader index, while pockets of healthcare, consumer, and financial shares remained green.

Bitcoin’s daily chart turned more decisively bearish after the break below $60,000. Its 50-day exponential moving average, or EMA, was below the 200-day EMA, a death-cross configuration, and spot prices were trading beneath both lines. That confirms sustained downside momentum rather than a brief intraday dip.

The relative strength index, or RSI, had fallen into the teens, far below the 30 threshold usually associated with oversold conditions. The average directional index, or ADX, climbed to 43.7, signaling a strong trend, with the chart’s directional readings favoring sellers. Oversold does not mean a bounce is guaranteed.

The longer-range chart puts the $60,000 breakdown in context. Bitcoin traded around this area several times in 2024 and returned to it during the mid-2026 retreat, making it a visible historical price zone as well as a round-number threshold.

With $60,000 giving way, the roughly $58,000–$59,000 support area near the mid-2026 lows comes into focus. If that zone fails, the next notable historical demand area is around $55,000–$56,000, followed by the $50,000 region marked by the deeper 2024 selloff.

For bulls, $60,000 is now the first level to hold. The next hurdle is $62,000–$62,500, followed by $65,000 and heavier resistance near $68,000–$70,000. A brief bounce back above $60,000 would not, by itself, reverse the bearish trend.

As of right now, Bitcoin had touched $59,300.13, confirming that sellers had pushed below the $60,000 support zone. The next test is whether that level becomes resistance on a rebound.

#4940. Inkling 2 Mini got 20 questions right but failed 4 out of 5 tests that required deeper thinking

Inkling 2 Mini’s repeated queries on a hidden-pattern test spotlight the need for tighter limits as local AI agents reach Macs.

In a hosted test published by Plotly on Nov. 21, the AI model Inkling 2 Mini got all 20 routine data questions right.

On one question about a hidden pattern, it made 585 queries over about 48 minutes and repeated the same few queries dozens of times, but it still missed the pattern.

In Plotly’s benchmark, Inkling 2 Mini scored 36 of 43 overall and found one of five hidden patterns. Plotly found that on the hidden-pattern questions, the model often added up the biggest totals and stopped.

Plotly ran the model through its own analytics software, Plotly Studio Embedded, using a version hosted by Prime Intellect and accessed through OpenRouter. It reported one scored sample per model, combining results with reruns of 16 Inkling 2 Mini questions that encountered a provider outage.

One scored sample using hosted inference.

On hidden-pattern questions, the model often stopped at overall totals before isolating the local signal.

The test leaves open how often the query loop would recur.

Each square represents one question in Plotly’s published results. Filled squares mark correct answers. The callout reports query count and approximate elapsed time for one failed question.

The test leaves open how often the query loop would recur.

Thinking Machines Lab released the downloadable weights on Nov. 20 under the Apache 2.0 license. Its model card lists support for Apple silicon Macs with at least 32 GB of memory.

The card lists a context window of up to 1 million tokens, which measures how much information the model can handle at once. How much of that window is usable on a particular Mac depends on the memory needed to process the request.

In the Nov. 22 release notes for oMLX 0.8.3, the Mac software’s maintainers reported about 20% faster prompt processing and 10% to 13% faster generation than in 0.8.2 for a reduced-precision build on an M5 Max.

The update also lets oMLX refuse prompts on a 32 GB Mac when it estimates they would exceed the memory budget.

In a Nov. 23 follow-up, an oMLX issue reporter said prompts of about 43,000 and 57,000 tokens were completed on one 32 GB M5 MacBook Pro using the updated software and a reduced-precision build. Reported memory use peaked at 23 GB and 24 GB, respectively.

Once a request fits, a separate limit can stop an agent from spending more turns on the same few queries.

Thinking Machines Lab says it saw the model repeatedly check its work without making progress in internal tests. The lab recommends caps on generated tokens and on the number of turns an agent can take.

That account sold about 364,000 of the tokens to 388 buyers for $13.5 million, then converted the proceeds to ETH. At Bitquery’s last check, about 7,350 ETH sat in three wallets.

Wagyu’s pseudonymous founder, PerpetualCow, blamed a breach at 1984.hosting, an Icelandic web hosting provider.

1984 ehf., the company behind the service, said it had suffered an extensive and serious cyberattack on its IT infrastructure, Icelandic outlet DV reported Friday, citing a joint statement with CERT-IS, Iceland’s national cybersecurity incident response team.

#4932. ‘MoldyRoost’ ads can steal crypto wallet recovery phrases from iPhones

The campaign used at least 480 ad domains to target users in more than 47 countries and could infect a vulnerable iPhone with a single tap.

Hackers are using fake ads to spread malware that can steal crypto wallet recovery phrases from vulnerable iPhones, cybersecurity company Confiant said Friday.

Confiant, which calls the campaign MoldyRoost, linked at least 480 ad domains to it between May 12 and Nov. 17. The ads targeted users in more than 47 countries.

“Just loading the landing page of a MoldyRoost ad on a vulnerable iPhone is enough to get infected,” Confiant said in its report.

Google Threat Intelligence reported in March that hackers were using a tool called Coruna to target visitors to fake crypto sites. MoldyRoost bought ads to spread the same tool on sites unrelated to crypto, where tapping an ad could expose a recovery phrase saved on a vulnerable iPhone.

Coruna can search Apple Notes for wallet seed phrases and read QR codes in saved images. A thief who gets a seed phrase can use those words to open the wallet on another device and move the funds.

The campaign also used DarkSword, another tool that can steal wallet data, which Google researchers also warned of in March.

In a Nov. 6 warning about DarkSword, blockchain security firm CertiK urged iPhone users to update their software and keep seed phrases out of Notes, photos and screenshots. It also advised avoiding unfamiliar links and using a hardware wallet for larger holdings.

#4931. Google Gives Gemini Control of Mac Apps, But Its New AI Agent Comes With Risks

Google Gives Gemini Control of Mac Apps, But Its New AI Agent Comes With Risks

Google wants Gemini to do more than answer questions on your Mac.

The company launched a computer-use feature Thursday that lets its AI assistant open applications, navigate menus, fill out forms, and move information between local files and websites on a user's behalf.

“Starting today, computer use in the Gemini app for Mac helps you get multi-step tasks done across your local files, desktop apps, and the web,” Google announced. “Just describe what you need, and Gemini handles the clicking, typing, menus, and forms for you.”

The new capability runs in what Google calls an isolated background window, leaving the computer's main display and cursor available for the owner. Users can watch the agent's actions through a live preview inside the Gemini chat and interrupt a task if it goes off course.

Google's examples include extracting shipping details from PDF documents and entering them into online forms, editing batches of photos, and building presentations from dashboard data. The company also suggests researching prices and preparing resale listings based on pictures stored on a Mac.

Those jobs involve a different kind of AI interaction than asking a chatbot to write instructions. Gemini is supposed to operate the software itself, carrying out the clicks and keystrokes that would otherwise fall to the user.

The feature is rolling out in English in the United States to adult users with personal paid Google accounts. It requires an Apple Silicon Mac running macOS Sequoia 15.0 or later; work and school accounts are not supported at launch.

To activate it, users enable computer use in the Mac app, open Task mode, and prompt Gemini with @Computer use. According to Google's support documentation, the assistant asks for permission before controlling a new app, with options to deny access, allow it once, or allow it on an ongoing basis.

Users can create lists of allowed and blocked apps, monitor the virtual screen, and stop a task. Google says sensitive actions require confirmation before the agent proceeds, though an app marked "always allow" can be accessed again without another app-level prompt.

Computer use adds a local dimension to Google's agent strategy. Decrypt previously covered Gemini Spark, the company's cloud-based task agent, while competitors have developed desktop-control tools including OpenAI's Codex, Anthropic's Claude, and Kimi Work.

The separate window is also a practical distinction: an agent that commandeers the visible cursor can make a computer difficult to use during a long task. Google's approach lets the person keep working while Gemini handles another workflow.

A background window should not be confused with a guarantee that files or accounts are isolated from risk. Google's own documentation warns that the agent can reach active sessions in desktop applications, encounter authentication codes displayed on screen, and take actions that some apps cannot reverse.

In that case, any error is the user’s fault, not Google’s.

It also warns about prompt injection, an attack in which malicious instructions hidden in a document or website persuade an AI system to do something its user never requested. With permission to operate multiple apps, Gemini could encounter hostile content in one program and act on it in another.

Google offers optional on-demand file backups but says they cannot always be created. Where they work, the temporary copies remain available for 24 hours or until the next prompt, and some applications may prevent their content from being backed up at all.

The launch also comes three days after Apple introduced tighter access controls in the macOS 27.3 beta. Those changes give users more ways to restrict powerful permissions to specific apps or folders and to require fresh approval each time access is requested.

#4927. Cross River Bank Launches Its First Bitcoin-Backed Loans With LendingUSA

Cross River Bank has launched its first bitcoin-backed loans, working with consumer lender LendingUSA to let eligible borrowers take out between $10,000 and $250,000 against their BTC without selling it.

The program is the first time Cross River has originated bitcoin-secured loans itself, according to The Block. LendingUSA runs the application platform, and every loan is made by Cross River, a New Jersey state-chartered bank, according to LendingUSA.

Borrowers can choose a loan-to-value ratio of 30%, 50% or 70%, which sets how much cash they receive against the value of the bitcoin they pledge. LendingUSA defines the ratio as the loan balance divided by the value of the collateral, so a 50% loan against $100,000 of bitcoin would be $50,000. Two repayment options are offered: interest-only or no monthly payments.

On an interest-only loan, borrowers make smaller monthly payments that roughly cover interest, with the principal and any remaining interest due at the end of the term. On a no-monthly-payment loan, the full balance plus interest is due at the end. LendingUSA says borrowers can repay early without penalties or fees.

Applicants go through a soft credit check, and LendingUSA says approval rests on the crypto being pledged rather than a credit score. The company says funding typically arrives within one to three business days after the collateral is verified.

A qualified third-party custodian holds the bitcoin for the life of the loan. Once the loan is repaid in full, the collateral is released back to the borrower, according to the company.

The terms carry the usual risk of borrowing against a volatile asset. LendingUSA says that if the value of the bitcoin falls, the loan-to-value ratio rises, and borrowers may be asked to add collateral or pay down the debt to avoid liquidation. The company says it will try to notify borrowers first but cannot guarantee it during fast price moves.

The timing matters. Bitcoin was trading near $61,000 on Monday morning, so a borrower at the 70% tier would have far less room before a collateral call than one at the 30% tier. Lower ratios leave a bigger cushion but unlock less cash per bitcoin pledged.

LendingUSA markets the loans for uses such as debt consolidation, home improvements and large purchases. The company says it has served more than 200,000 borrowers across its lending business, and the crypto product is its first expansion into digital asset-backed loans.

Cross River President Evan Russo framed the launch as a natural extension of the bank’s work. “Digital assets have become a meaningful part of how many consumers build and hold wealth, and the financial system must evolve to serve them responsibly,” Russo said in a statement.

“Cross River was built on the belief that innovation and trusted infrastructure go hand in hand,” he added. “Launching our first Bitcoin-secured lending program as the loan originator is a natural extension of that mission.”

The move adds a state-chartered bank to a lending market that has been moving closer to traditional finance. In August, Coinbase and Better Mortgage announced general availability of bitcoin-backed mortgages, and in April, Strike’s Jack Mallers announced lending proof-of-reserves and loans designed to withstand volatility.

Neither company has said how much bitcoin has been pledged so far or which custodian holds the collateral. LendingUSA’s page says rates and terms vary by applicant, and that loan amounts, interest rates and terms are disclosed once an application is received.

For now, the page lists the product’s maximum loan-to-value at 70%, and every loan on it is made by Cross River Bank.

#4926. Trump to host second memecoin dinner tonight with TRUMP down 99% from peak

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

One holder nabbed a seat with only $518 worth of TRUMP, a huge drop from the roughly $55,000 worth that holders required for the first dinner in May 2025, according to The Washington Post.

President Donald Trump is set to host a dinner on Sunday night for the largest holders of his Official Trump Official Trump (TRUMP) memecoin at Trump National Golf Club in Northern Virginia, outside Washington, according to the event's website. It is the second dinner and the third event overall for top TRUMP holders since the token launched in January 2025.

Fight Fight Fight LLC, the firm that promotes the memecoin, invited the top 185 holders based on their average TRUMP holdings between Sept. 30 and Nov. 16. One holder reached No. 179 after buying just $518 worth of TRUMP in October and November, according to a Washington Post analysis published Wednesday. It took at least $55,000 worth of the token to qualify for the first dinner, the Post found.

The Post's analysis also found more than 5,000 holders who could have qualified by registering but did not, even after organizers extended sign-ups by four days.

The top 29 holders are invited to a VIP reception with Trump and one of the event's celebrity guests, and the top 120 will receive gift bags that include a Trump watch, Trump fragrance, and a commemorative ticket and poster, according to the site. The top four VIP holders will also get 18-karat gold Trump watches, provided they hold at least 1 million TRUMP, a stake worth about $895,000 at Sunday's price.

Boxer Floyd Mayweather, "Shark Tank" investor Kevin O'Leary and NBA champion Tristan Thompson are scheduled to appear, along with mentalist Lior Suchard, according to the site. The site says there will be no private meetings with the president and that no gifts will be accepted.

TRUMP traded at $0.8950 on Sunday, down 98.8% from its all-time high of $73.43 in January 2025, just after the token launched, according to The Block's Official Trump price page. In the two weeks after this dinner was announced, TRUMP's trading volume fell about 35% and its price dropped 9%, according to Kaiko data cited by Bloomberg. Volume rose to between four and eight times its prior levels after each of the first two events were announced, Kaiko found.

Singapore-linked trading platform FastX, which reached the top of the holder leaderboard for the event, said on X on Thursday that it will attend the dinner "as an independent holder" and bring 10 questions chosen by a vote of its community. FastX has said it is not affiliated with Trump or the TRUMP project.

Morten Christensen, founder of crypto giveaways site Airdrop Alert, also plans to attend, the company said on X. Christensen, who attended both earlier events and qualified for the VIP reception, told The Wall Street Journal he bought 75,000 TRUMP at an average price of $1.70 while shorting a roughly equal amount through perpetual futures on Hyperliquid.

By holding the token and betting on its decline in price, Christensen came away with a profit of about $6, he said, due to funding fees. The same approach cost him about $1,200 for the first event and $500 for the second, he told Bloomberg. He used the same approach to win a seat for his sister.

Tron founder Justin Sun, who was the largest TRUMP holder at the May 2025 dinner, is not attending, the Journal reported, citing a person familiar with the matter. Sun sued World Liberty Financial, the Trump family's crypto venture, which operates independently of the memecoin, in April, accusing it of freezing his WLFI tokens. World Liberty has called his claims "entirely meritless."

Trump hosted the top 220 holders at the same golf club in May 2025, and the promoter held a conference and luncheon for 297 holders at Mar-a-Lago in April. Trump has not posted about the dinner or the memecoin on social media this year, per the Post. The scoring window for the promoter's next contest, a three-day Super Bowl LXI package in Los Angeles for the top 18 holders, opens on Sunday, according to the site.

Eight senators led by Sen. Adam Schiff, D-Calif., asked the White House in a letter last month to publish a list of attendees, saying the event "appears to be a pay-to-play scheme." Democrats, who won control of both chambers of Congress in this month's midterms, have pledged to investigate Trump's crypto ventures.

"The President's attendance at a privately organized event does not make it a White House event or government function," White House spokesperson Davis Ingle told Bloomberg. The White House directed questions about potential conflicts of interest to Fight Fight Fight, which did not respond to Bloomberg's request for comment.

Career staff at the Commodity Futures Trading Commission opened an inquiry into possible fraud involving TRUMP soon after its launch in early 2025, but backed off after concluding the commission would not approve subpoenas, The New York Times reported Friday, citing three former agency officials with direct knowledge of the inquiry who spoke on condition of anonymity.

The officials were concerned about possible insider trading after some early buyers posted large profits, and believed then-acting Chair Caroline Pham would not sign off, according to the Times. The inquiry did not get far enough to determine whether any wrongdoing occurred, and a CFTC spokeswoman declined to comment.

Trump collected at least $636 million in transaction and licensing fees from the memecoin last year, according to his financial disclosures, while nearly a million buyers lost a combined $3.81 billion through the end of June, according to Nansen data cited by The New York Times.

#4924. Cathie Wood Bets $91M on Anthropic IPO While Paring Crypto Exposure

Cathie Wood Bets $91M on Anthropic IPO While Paring Crypto Exposure

Cathie Wood’s ARK Invest jumped in on the Anthropic IPO on the AI company’s first day of public trading, buying roughly $91 million in shares while quietly paring back a sliver of its crypto exposure.

The firm bought 934,968 shares of Anthropic, worth about $90.9 million at Thursday’s closing price, spread across six of its actively managed exchange-traded funds on the Claude maker’s Nasdaq debut.

Roughly 60% of the purchase landed in ARK’s flagship Innovation ETF, known as ARKK, where the shares make up about 0.4% of total holdings.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Anthropic with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

During the same session, ARK trimmed two crypto-linked positions, selling about $2.9 million of the 3iQ Ethereum staking ETF and $1.2 million of the 3iQ Solana staking ETF, a combined $4.1 million, from its ARKW and ARKF funds.

The reductions are modest next to the Anthropic buy, and market observers framed them as routine portfolio rebalancing rather than a change in ARK’s stance on either token.

Wood’s firm frequently repositions its exposure, and earlier this week ARK sold a combined $85 million in Nvidia shares amid the leadership turmoil that saw Jensen Huang resign. At the time, the firm also bought crypto-linked stocks including Coinbase, Circle and Block.

Taken together, the trades show ARK leaning harder into AI equities at a moment when its crypto-adjacent bets have been battered by a market downturn.

Anthropic’s debut was one of the year’s most anticipated listings. The company priced its IPO at $83 per share, and the stock surged before cooling, closing its first day at $97.17, up 17% from the offering price but down sharply from its intraday high, with nearly 850 million shares changing hands.

It slipped again in premarket trading the next day. Even after the purchase, Anthropic sits outside ARK’s top 10 holdings, which are led by Tesla, SpaceX, Tempus AI and Amazon; ARK’s SpaceX stake alone is valued around $1.69 billion.

The timing underscores the crosscurrents buffeting both sectors. ARK’s crypto trims landed as digital assets slid, with Ethereum down about 8% on the day near $1,620, part of a broader selloff that has pressured crypto funds.

For Wood, long one of crypto’s most prominent institutional champions, the week’s trades suggest a tactical tilt toward the AI boom even as she keeps a foot firmly in digital assets, buying crypto stocks one day and trimming crypto ETFs the next.

Nasdaq-100 futures rose 3.1% and S&P 500 futures gained 2.4% ahead of Monday’s US open after Friday’s tech sell-off.

Both the S&P 500 and the Dow Jones Industrial Average ended Friday in correction territory, more than 10% below their recent closing highs. The Nasdaq Composite fell 5.3% to 22,617.73, erasing its gains for the year.

#4920. Nvidia Soars as Huang Returns, Lifting Stocks and Crypto Shares

Nvidia shares jumped about 35% on Monday after the chipmaker reinstated Jensen Huang as president and CEO, restoring roughly $1 trillion in market value at the open. The stock traded near $169.20 at 10:41 a.m. ET, up from Friday’s close of $125.53, and Wall Street rallied with it.

The board voted unanimously to bring Huang back, effective immediately, after what it called a series of discussions and meetings. It said it received input from “senior levels of the United States government” and is confident his return “poses no national security risk.” President Donald Trump had urged the move.

Several items remain open. An independent board committee will finish its investigation into reports that Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort, and Huang said he will cooperate. Nvidia also formed a national security and export control committee and is searching for a chief compliance officer.

Huang resigned on Nov. 11, days after admitting an affair with a woman U.S. officials believe is linked to Chinese intelligence. Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress became interim CEO and Harvey Jones became board chair. Nvidia had said it was searching for a permanent successor.

The stock slid for seven straight weeks, the longest such streak since 2008. On Friday it plunged 14% after Reuters reported that the SEC is examining the company’s accounting practices. Reuters noted that investigations are not evidence of wrongdoing. Huang’s return reverses most of the losses since his affair became public.

The selloff came even after Nvidia reported better-than-expected quarterly results on Nov. 17. For weeks, the board had said it was not considering bringing Huang back, which makes Monday’s announcement a sharp turn for the world’s most closely watched chipmaker.

The Nasdaq Composite rose about 7.4% to near 24,300 at 10:41 a.m. ET, and the S&P 500 gained about 5.4% to near 7,280. The Dow Jones Industrial Average climbed about 3.3%, or more than 1,500 points, to near 49,700. The S&P 500 had fallen seven straight days.

Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent warned on Sunday that the Fed may be making a “policy mistake” if it overtightens. The central bank meets Dec. 8-9, and a quarter-point increase remains the base case.

The bitcoin price climbed to a Monday high near $64,500, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro data, and was near $63,000 at 10:41 a.m. ET. That is about $8,000 above the weekend low of $55,106, hit at roughly 1:17 a.m. ET on Saturday. Bitcoin had slid with stocks on Friday.

Strategy (MSTR) climbed about 13% to near $88 after it disclosed a purchase of 457 bitcoin last week at an average of about $64,898, lifting its holdings to 851,305 BTC.

The rebound follows a rough stretch for bitcoin. It finished last week down about 6%, its seventh straight red weekly candle, and spot bitcoin ETFs shed about $1.79 billion, their fourth straight week of net outflows. Bitcoin has traded closely with technology stocks during the selloff.

Miners posted the biggest gains. IREN soared about 31%, while CleanSpark (CLSK) rose about 17% and MARA gained about 16%. Core Scientific (CORZ) added about 14% and Riot Platforms (RIOT) gained about 10%.

Bitcoin treasury companies also rallied. Strive (ASST) rose about 16%, Nakamoto (NAKA) gained about 6% and Twenty One Capital (XXI) added about 10%.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $63,000.

#4918. A $2 million exploit leads to a $3.7 million free-for-all as traders empty Fusionist’s ACE bridge

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

A hacker minted quintillions of fake ACE tokens on BNB Chain and sold them for $2 million, crashing the price. Traders then bought the cheap tokens and swapped them for real ACE worth $3.7 million.

An attacker made off with about $2 million on Sunday by minting a massive quantity of unbacked ACE Fusionist (ACE) tokens, a creation of blockchain game project Fusionist, on BNB Chain and selling them for USDT while crashing ACE's price on the network, according to The Block's analysis of onchain data.

Traders then took the same cheap tokens, which by then numbered in the billions, and sent them through Fusionist's bridge, emptying it of its 53.7 million real ACE, worth about $3.7 million at current prices, onchain data shows. ACE's circulating supply before the exploit was only around 116 million tokens.

Fusionist is a blockchain gaming studio backed by Binance Labs (now YZi Labs) that makes sci-fi strategy games and runs its own blockchain, Endurance. ACE is the project's native token, which is used to pay transaction fees on Endurance. It trades on exchanges including Binance and OKX. A copy of the ACE token also exists as a separate token on BNB Chain. Fusionist runs a bridge that converts ACE between the two blockchains.

Onchain analytics account Onchain Lens said on X that the attacker "exploited Fusionist's privileged bridge-minter access" to mint unbacked ACE and swap it for USDT, then routed $1.87 million through crypto mixer Tornado Cash. The Block traced about 1,150 ETH, worth roughly $2 million, into Tornado Cash from wallets that either received the minted ACE directly from the bridge or collected proceeds from its sale. The attacker moved the funds from BNB Chain to Ethereum through cross-chain swap services including SwftSwap and Relay.

The first mint, of 3.5 million ACE, came at 12:55 p.m. ET, according to BscScan data. ACE has a maximum supply of 147 million tokens. The mints grew to billions, then trillions, then quadrillions of ACE over the next two and a half hours, ending with a 10 quintillion ACE mint at 3:28 p.m.

The mints were sent from a wallet that Fusionist's deployer funded nearly three years ago, BscScan data shows. Blockchain security firm Blockaid, which first flagged the exploit, listed that wallet among the exploiter addresses.

The attacker sold each batch into decentralized exchange pools on BNB Chain as soon as it was minted. By 1:41 p.m. ET, one trader was able to buy ACE there for about $0.0001, compared with the roughly $0.04 price of ACE on exchanges at the time.

Fusionist's bridge holds ACE tokens on its Endurance network to back the tokens on BNB Chain. When a user sends BNB Chain ACE back to the bridge, it burns those tokens and releases the same amount of ACE on Endurance. The bridge kept working after the attack, so any trader could buy the crashed tokens on BNB Chain and swap them for real ACE tokens, then sell them on exchanges.

From 1:13 p.m. to 3:05 p.m. ET, the Endurance contract paid out about 53.7 million ACE to 24 addresses, according to Endurance block explorer data. One address alone received 26.1 million, nearly a quarter of ACE's former circulating supply.

The bridge held about 53.7 million ACE before the hack; by Sunday afternoon it held only 0.08 ACE. Another 27 attempted transfers, totaling roughly 1.36 billion ACE, failed because the bridge was already nearly empty by that point.

Pseudonymous X user ZAMA_APPLE was the first to post about a user exploiting the arbitrage, sharing a screenshot that showed a trader turning $60 into $17,000 by buying ACE on BNB Chain and selling it on OKX. The Block traced the trade to a single wallet.

The wallet started with about $50 in USDT, withdrawn from Binance around 1:34 p.m. ET. It spent $10 testing the bridge, then used its remaining $40 to buy about 404,000 ACE at 1:41 p.m. Four minutes later, the bridge paid out the same amount of real ACE on Endurance, and the trader sent it straight to the exchange. By 1:53 p.m., it had sold the tokens for about $17,670, a 350x return.

Fusionist told its followers at 2:30 p.m. ET to pause ACE transfers, trades and bridging on BSC while it investigates "an apparent ongoing exploit." Fusionist has not posted an update since, as of publication time.

Crypto exchange Gate said its security team confirmed the bridge was exploited and that it would roll back all ACE/USDT trades executed between 12:55 p.m. and 2:15 p.m. ET. Gate also switched its ACE perpetual futures market to reduce-only and said it would settle and delist the market late Monday.

Some Gate users who sold ACE during that window were left with negative balances after Gate rolled back their trades, returning their ACE and reclaiming the USDT from the sales, Gate's support account said on X.

ACE is down about 20% over the past 24 hours, trading at roughly $0.069, according to The Block's price page. The Block has reached out to Fusionist and OKX for comment.

An editor checks the facts, figures and quotes they produce against primary sources, and every piece is reviewed by an editor before it is published. Sponsored content and press releases are labeled as such and are not written by our editorial team.

#4917. SBF’s 'The Altruists' Plot Twist: FTX’s Anthropic Shares Would Be Worth $43 Billion

SBF’s 'The Altruists' Plot Twist: FTX’s Anthropic Shares Would Be Worth $43 Billion

Sam Bankman-Fried had an eventful Thursday—at least on the internet.

Anthropic went public, Netflix released “The Altruists,” and Crypto Twitter imagined the fortune his collapsed exchange might have made by keeping its AI investment.

At Anthropic’s November 19 closing price of $97.17, FTX’s former shares would have been worth $43.28 billion. At the $111.50 opening trade, their theoretical value would have reached $49.66 billion.

That money never belonged personally to Bankman-Fried. The bankruptcy estate sold the shares to repay creditors after FTX’s 2022 collapse.

The former CEO is serving a 25-year sentence for fraud after using FTX customer funds to make investments and cover losses, plus got hit with $11 billion in forfeiture—a fraction of what his single Anthropic investment would have returned today.

A company linked to Alameda, FTX’s sister company, committed $500 million to Anthropic in October 2021. The money converted into roughly 44.54 million preferred shares during the AI startup’s 2022 Series B, about 13.6% of the company.

Bankruptcy administrators sold the position in two batches in 2024 for approximately $1.34 billion. Buyers included an Abu Dhabi-linked fund and Jane Street, Bankman-Fried’s former employer, as filings showed.

At Thursday’s closing price that investment would have produced a total of nearly $43.28 billion—roughly 32 times what creditors’ administrators actually collected.

Sadly, that fortune is hypothetical. Later financing diluted FTX’s percentage ownership, the shares carried restrictions, and the estate needed liquidity to compensate customers who lost money when FTX went belly up. A judge approved the sale in February 2024.

Tiffany Fong, who rose to Twitter fame after interviewing SBF just before he was arrested, shared an AI-generated video of a crying Bankman-Fried drinking in a car alongside the $40 billion-plus calculation. Another account posted a crying-man meme about him watching the listing from prison.

That’s good for a 1,018% return, and proof SBF knew how to pick his bets. But even the best investments can't justify gambling with other people's money without their consent. He made that bet, too—and lost.

#4914. Solo Bitcoin Miner Wins $191,000 Block Reward With Just 11 TH/s

A solo miner running about 11 terahashes per second has mined Bitcoin block 977,057, collecting a reward of 3.1287 BTC, or about $191,000, on a machine that makes up roughly one-millionth of a percent of the network.

The block was found through the solo service at CKpool on Sunday, Nov. 22, at about 7:54 a.m. ET. The reward is the 3.125 BTC subsidy plus about 0.0037 BTC in transaction fees, and it was worth roughly $191,000 at bitcoin’s price when the block landed.

The block holds 584 transactions. Its coinbase transaction pays 3.0661 BTC to a single address and 0.0626 BTC, about 2% of the reward, to a second output. That split matches the service’s 2% fee, which means the miner’s take is a little over $186,000.

Con Kolivas, the developer behind CKpool, congratulated the miner on X, whose address he abbreviated as bc1q~zy9j. It matches the end of the address that received the payout. Kolivas called it the 323rd block found on the service and “yet another lucky one.”

He said the miner had only about 11.1 TH/s of hashrate on a 3.47 TH worker. “A miner of this size has about a one in 1650 chance of solving a block in a year,” Kolivas wrote.

The block was tagged to Solo CK on the mempool.space block explorer, and its coinbase carries the pool’s signature. CKpool describes itself as a service that lets miners go after full solo blocks without running their own node, since you cannot mine directly to a Bitcoin Core node.

Solo CK has found five blocks since Nov. 6, according to the explorer’s pool page. The most recent before this one was block 976,642 on Nov. 19, followed by blocks on Nov. 11, Nov. 7 and Nov. 6. Each paid between 3.13 and 3.15 BTC.

Earlier this year, a different solo miner won block 943,411 on the same service on April 2, for about 3.139 BTC. In October, a FutureBit Apollo solo miner found block 973,262 and earned 3.154 BTC.

Those wins are the exception. Most of the network’s hashrate sits in large pools and industrial operations that split rewards across many miners, which is how they smooth out the luck. A single small machine can run for years without finding anything, and a lucky block can show up on day one. Miners on the service get nothing unless they find a block, and then they keep the full reward minus the 2% fee.

Bitcoin’s network hashrate was about 1.04 zettahashes per second, or roughly 1,043 exahashes, on a three-day average Monday. At 11.1 TH/s, the winning miner contributes about 0.000001% of that total, which fits Kolivas’s odds. One in 1,650 works out to a chance of about 0.06% over a full year of mining.

Mining difficulty is about 134.8 trillion. The last adjustment cut it by about 9.1%, and the next one, expected around Nov. 26, is estimated to raise it by about 9.2%, according to mempool.space. Difficulty resets every 2,016 blocks to keep blocks arriving about every 10 minutes.

Mining conditions have been tough. Bitcoin plunged to about $55,100 over the weekend before rebounding to near $64,000 on Monday morning, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro. Lower prices cut the dollar value of every block, whether it goes to an industrial miner or a hobbyist running a small solo rig.

The subsidy is fixed at 3.125 BTC until the next halving, so what the solo winner earns depends on fees and the market. A 3.1287 BTC block was worth about $196,000 at Monday’s price near $62,700, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $62,700.

Bitcoin fell to a cycle low near $55,100 over the weekend amid continued risk-off sentiment, macro headwinds, and an AI equities-led stock market downturn.

Spot bitcoin exchange-traded funds in the U.S. reported around $1.79 billion in net outflows last week, the largest weekly withdrawal since the week ended June 26, 2026.

This marked the fourth consecutive week of outflows for the funds, according to data from SoSoValue. BlackRock's iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) led the outflows with $1.37 billion alone during the week.

So far this month, around $4.14 billion has left bitcoin ETFs, trailing June's record monthly net outflows. Total net assets in the bitcoin ETFs sat at $71.35 billion as of Friday, down from around $110 billion in mid-September.

Spot ether Ethereum (ETH) ETFs also reported $314 million in weekly outflows, making it the eighth consecutive week of negative flows.

Laevitas Head of Markets Rick Maeda told The Block that the current net outflow represents "mostly risk reduction" among investors. "Profit-taking usually follows a rally, but these outflows have come into a falling market, with BTC sliding from around $80,000 at the start of the month to the low $60,000s," said Maeda.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell to a cycle low near $55,700 over the weekend amid continued risk-off sentiment, macro headwinds, and an AI equities-led stock market downturn. The Nasdaq Composite fell around 10% last week, and the S&P 500 dropped 8.6% as the scandal involving former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang fueled wider concerns about the sustainability of the AI sector. The Block's Bitcoin Fear and Greed Index currently shows 11 — extreme fear.

"Being on the far end of the risk asset spectrum, whilst also facing its own headwinds such as quantum computing and hacks, crypto has been an easy asset for investors to get rid of from their portfolios," Maeda added.

Dan Khus, chief analyst at LVRG Research, said the current ETF outflow streak appears to be more structural than previous outflows.

"Unlike earlier outflow periods that were often shorter and more reactive to single events, the current four-week streak appears more structural, coinciding with sustained price pressure, elevated liquidations, and a shift in institutional positioning toward capital preservation," Khus commented.

Risk-averse investors are moving further away from crypto products as they expect the Federal Reserve to deliver another interest rate hike in December, analysts said. The U.S. Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE) Price Index, scheduled to be announced this Wednesday, may hint at how sticky inflation remains, and whether the Federal Reserve will proceed with another rate hike in December, BTSE COO Jeff Mei said.

"Bitcoin and ether ETFs may face further outflows as rate-hike concerns and weak risk appetite weigh on institutional demand," Zeus Research Analyst Dominick John said. "Bitcoin is likely to remain more resilient, while Ether faces greater pressure. A sustained recovery will depend on easing macro fears and renewed inflows."

#4911. ECB to let banks pledge more tokenized securities as collateral

The ECB aims to start accepting securities issued on platforms licensed under the EU’s distributed ledger pilot program in June 2027.

The European Central Bank (ECB) approved a plan Monday to let banks borrow from euro area central banks against tokenized securities issued on platforms licensed under an EU pilot program.

The ECB plans to put the change into effect in June 2027, according to its announcement. The EU’s DLT Pilot Regime allows licensed platforms to handle trading or settlement of securities using distributed ledger technology.

Since March 30, banks have been able to pledge qualifying tokenized securities issued through central securities depositories that use distributed ledgers. Under Monday’s decision, a bank holding a qualifying bond issued on a licensed pilot platform could also use it to secure a central bank loan.

The securities must still meet the existing collateral rules of the Eurosystem, which comprises the ECB and the national central banks of the euro area. They must also be available to the Eurosystem through an approved link to a central securities depository that offers settlement on TARGET2-Securities, the Eurosystem’s securities settlement platform.

The Eurosystem expects to publish additional legal and operational requirements for the pilot platforms in spring 2027.

“A future-proof collateral framework must embrace new technologies without compromising on safety or the rigour of our risk assessment,” said ECB executive board member Isabel Schnabel.

The ECB said it is also exploring how banks could pledge securities issued and settled entirely on distributed ledgers. Through its Appia project, the Eurosystem plans to publish a blueprint in 2028 for how Europe’s tokenized wholesale financial markets could operate.

Related: EU taps industry to set standards for tokenized securities

#4909. Nvidia Shares Sink to 52-Week Low After Report SEC Is Probing Accounting

Nvidia Shares Sink to 52-Week Low After Report SEC Is Probing Accounting

Shares of the AI chipmaker fell about 13% to $127.16 in afternoon trading after touching a fresh 52-week low of $126.64.

The drop followed a Reuters report that SEC enforcement staff are examining potential accounting issues at the company. Nvidia’s market value slipped to about $3.05 trillion.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Nvidia with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The Wall Street Journal reported Tuesday that the SEC was investigating Nvidia, including whether its disclosures misled investors. Reuters, citing two people briefed on the matter, said the inquiry centers on potential accounting issues, though it couldn’t confirm when the probe began or exactly what the agency is examining.

The SEC declined to comment, and Nvidia didn’t immediately respond to Reuters. Investigations aren’t evidence of wrongdoing, and many close without enforcement action.

Renewed attention has fallen on a change Nvidia made last year to how it reports revenue by geography. It switched from customers’ billing addresses to their headquarters, which removed Singapore from its regional breakdown.

The Financial Times reported this week that Nvidia made the switch as Singapore sales climbed to nearly half its global revenue. Reuters didn’t say whether the change is part of the SEC’s inquiry.

Investors have also grown wary of circular AI financing deals, in which chipmakers help customers fund chip purchases and data center construction. On Tuesday’s earnings call, interim CEO Colette Kress said Nvidia would be far more selective about such financing going forward.

Jensen Huang resigned as CEO last week following reports of extramarital relationships, including one with Alice Huang.

Alice Huang previously led Singapore-based Megaspeed, a major Nvidia customer, and U.S. authorities suspect her of being a Chinese spy; China has denied she is a spy.

U.S. authorities have been investigating Megaspeed since 2024 over possible export control violations tied to alleged shipments of Nvidia chips to China, according to the Justice and Commerce departments.

Former CEO Huang said Tuesday he would be honored to return if the board believed it would benefit shareholders and employees. Hours later, the board shut that down.

“The Nvidia board does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO,” board chair Harvey Jones said on the earnings call, adding that the company had hired executive search firm Heidrick & Struggles to find a permanent replacement.

The crisis has erased more than a third of Nvidia’s market value, even after the company beat expectations Tuesday with $115.8 billion in quarterly revenue, up 103% from a year earlier.

#4904. Singapore’s OCBC, StraitsX launch pilot for cross-border business stablecoin payments

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The setup uses StraitsX's infrastructure for onchain collection and compliance, while OCBC handles the fiat off-ramp.

OCBC, a major bank in Singapore, has launched a pilot with StraitsX for cross-border business-to-business stablecoin payments.

In a statement released Monday, StraitsX wrote that the trial connects stablecoin settlement with participating OCBC clients' existing bank accounts. The setup uses StraitsX's "Dedicated Wallet Address" infrastructure for onchain collection and compliance, while OCBC handles the fiat off-ramp process.

The pilot is designed to explore how businesses can receive cross-border payments through their existing OCBC bank accounts without directly managing the underlying stablecoins, according to the statement. The pair said they will initially test selected cross-border payment flows.

NTT DOCOMO Asia, a Singapore entity of Japanese mobile carrier NTT DOCOMO, is the first customer participating in the trial.

"As new forms of digital money emerge, businesses are looking for ways to harness their benefits without adding complexity to their day-to-day financial operations," Carmen Chan, deputy head of global transaction banking at OCBC, said in the statement. "Our collaboration with StraitsX allows us to explore how stablecoin-based payments can be integrated with established banking services, while keeping the customer experience seamless."

The latest stablecoin effort came after OCBC, StraitsX, and Standard Chartered announced last week a proof-of-concept that explores interoperability between tokenized deposit networks issued by different banks.

StraitsX, which holds a Major Payment Institution license under the Monetary Authority of Singapore, offers a stablecoin-native settlement layer for global finance. It also issues the XSGD and XUSD stablecoins. OCBC, meanwhile, is the second-largest financial services group in Southeast Asia by assets.

The latest pilot comes as Singapore's financial institutions and crypto players step up efforts to expand the use of tokenized assets. Both OCBC and StraitsX participate in the BLOOM initiative, which the Monetary Authority of Singapore launched in October 2025 to develop use cases for tokenized bank deposits and stablecoins.

The MAS also said last week that it issued S$100 million ($77.5 million) of four-week tokenized MAS Bills on Nov. 6 through its Singapore Dollar Test Network. The bills were settled in wholesale CBDC.

Also last week, DBS Bank and Kinexys by JPMorgan announced an interoperability solution that allows institutions to make 24/7 transfers of tokenized deposits between the U.S. and Singapore.

#4903. Claude Claims Historic Math Breakthrough One Day After Anthropic IPO

Claude Claims Historic Math Breakthrough One Day After Anthropic IPO

An Anthropic mathematician says Claude helped him crack the Riemann hypothesis, a 167-year-old puzzle with a $1 million prize attached.

His claim surfaced Friday, one day after the AI company’s stock-market debut, and has not yet been independently verified.

“I believe I have, working with Claude, discovered a proof of the Riemann Hypothesis,” Levent Alpöge wrote in a four-page note. He says the method also resolves the broader Generalized Riemann Hypothesis, which extends the question to other mathematical functions.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Anthropic with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

Alpöge says he and Claude encoded their argument in Lean, software used to check mathematical proofs step by step. His note links to 14 downloadable parts of the proof archive, but its availability does not amount to acceptance by the mathematical community.

Alpöge says he messaged Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei about a possible announcement “late Sunday night,” November 15. He then consulted company leadership and said there was a constraint to respect, without explaining precisely what it was.

“The precise timing is horrible,” he acknowledged. Alpöge rejected a cynical interpretation of the timing, but did not explicitly say the company’s IPO caused the delay.

Anthropic began trading on Nasdaq Thursday, November 19, after raising $81.34 billion in its offering.

Alpöge figured in September’s AI math wars. OpenAI claimed it had solved the Navier-Stokes Millennium Prize problem as he and NYU mathematician Tristan Buckmaster pursued related fluid-equation work, sparking a credit dispute over research priority.

OpenAI followed in October with 722 manuscripts generated by an unreleased model, grouped into 372 families of results. Only 162 included Lean-checked main results, and mathematicians demanded stronger evidence rather than accepting the company’s flood of claims at face value.

Some mathematicians fear a “mathocalypse,” with AI solving coveted problems before researchers can learn from pursuing them. Toronto mathematician Daniel Litt warned that, without adaptation, “there’s just no more math in 50 years.” At an MIT panel this week, Buckmaster argued mathematics still requires human understanding.

First posed by Bernhard Riemann in 1859, the hypothesis predicts where certain zeros of the Riemann zeta function fall. The question matters because those zeros are linked to the distribution of prime numbers, whose patterns underlie extensive mathematical research.

A proof would be a historic mathematical result, since experts have been trying to solve that for over a century.

Lean can verify that formal deductions obey its rules. Mathematicians still need to reproduce the archive, check the formalized theorem matches the original conjecture, and examine the claimed result before considering it settled.

Anthropic previously reported progress on the zeta function without claiming to solve the full hypothesis. Alpöge’s new assertion goes far beyond that earlier work.

The Clay Mathematics Institute lists the Riemann hypothesis among its Millennium Prize Problems, each carrying a $1 million award. Under the institute’s prize rules, a proposed solution must be published in a qualifying outlet, survive at least two years of scrutiny, and earn general acceptance before the prize can be considered.

#4902. Bitdeer Sells All 281 Bitcoin It Mined Last Week, Holds None

Bitdeer sold every bitcoin it mined last week and still holds none of its own, the company said in its latest weekly update, continuing a nearly year-long strategy of turning production into cash after a weekend plunge in the bitcoin price.

The Singapore-based miner reported output of 281.1 BTC and sales of 281.1 BTC for the week, for a net addition of zero. Its pure holdings, which exclude customer deposits, were zero as of Nov. 20.

The sales came as bitcoin fell to a weekend low of $55,106 early Saturday at 1:17 AM ET, Bitcoin Magazine Pro data shows. The price has since bounced to near $62,700 as of 11:23 AM ET Monday.

Bitdeer’s treasury shrank from roughly 2,000 BTC at the end of 2025 to zero by Feb. 20, when Bitcoin Magazine reported the miner had liquidated its reserve over eight weeks. At the time, the company said the move should not be a concern for the broader market.

Bitdeer’s filings show how little it has kept since. In its third-quarter results, filed with the SEC on a Form 6-K on Nov. 10, the company listed 47 BTC held at Sept. 30, down from 2,029 a year earlier.

Its September production update showed 61 BTC held at the end of August. The company mined 1,355 BTC in September and 3,855 BTC in the third quarter, compared with 1,109 BTC in the same quarter of 2025.

Bitdeer has grown its fleet quickly. Self-mining hashrate reached 82.3 exahashes per second in the third quarter, up from 35.0 a year earlier, with another 28.7 exahashes per second of co-mining at third-party sites, the quarterly report shows.

The economics are tight. Self-mining generated $217.5 million in revenue in the quarter but only $6.2 million in gross profit. Electricity averaged $47 per megawatt-hour, and the fleet ran at 15.4 joules per terahash.

Bitdeer posted record revenue of $320.7 million and adjusted EBITDA of $69.6 million, but a net loss of $125.5 million. That included a $54.6 million non-cash loss tied to a bitcoin-collateralized loan from a related party.

Bitdeer also reported $213.7 million in cash and restricted cash, and its board authorized a new $100 million share repurchase program on Oct. 8.

Not every miner is selling. American Bitcoin secured a $150 million revolving credit line from FalconX on Monday that is backed by its bitcoin. The company said the facility lets it raise cash without drawing down its reserve.

The loan carries a 5.95% fee and an initial collateral ratio of 143%. Fully drawing it would require about $214.5 million in pledged bitcoin. American Bitcoin has not said whether it has drawn on the line, and its release lists the risk that pledged coins could be liquidated if prices fall.

American Bitcoin, which is majority-owned by Hut 8, said on its Nov. 4 earnings call that it held 8,427 BTC at the end of September. The company has also sold part of its production to cover operating costs.

Other miners have gone the other way. MARA sold 13,597 bitcoin earlier this month, as miners facing thin margins turned to their treasuries for cash.

Shares of Bitdeer (BTDR) were trading near $3.44 at 11:23 AM ET, up about 16% from Friday’s close of $2.96. The stock has ranged from $3.26 to $3.71 on the day as a broad market rally lifted bitcoin-linked stocks after Nvidia reinstated CEO Jensen Huang.

Bitdeer has not said whether it plans to rebuild a bitcoin position. It said in February that selling was a liquidity measure tied to evaluating powered land acquisitions and scaling its infrastructure.

The company is also shifting toward AI cloud services, and it reported an AI cloud revenue backlog of about $2.9 billion in its third-quarter release.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $62,700.

#4899. South Korea to publish draft of comprehensive crypto legal framework this week

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The draft is expected to include a licensing regime for crypto businesses and a bank-centered issuance system for local stablecoins.

South Korea's Financial Services Commission is preparing to release the government's draft for the Digital Asset Basic Act later this week, according to multiple local news reports.

The act is expected to become the country's second major piece of crypto legislation following the Virtual Asset User Protection Act, which took effect in 2024. While that earlier legislation focused primarily on protecting user assets and preventing unfair market activities, the upcoming bill aims to provide clearer rules for the industry.

Authorities have previously indicated that the Digital Asset Basic Act would cover the legal definition of digital assets and comprehensive rules governing issuance and distribution. The draft is also expected to introduce a licensing regime for crypto businesses, shifting from the current notification system.

A more contested element of the bill is a proposed cap on ownership stakes held by major shareholders of crypto exchanges, widely reported to be in the range of 15% to 20%, to curb concentration risk. The measure has drawn strong opposition from the industry, with critics arguing that it would undermine free-market principles and property rights.

After the FSC releases the draft, it is expected to be merged with proposals from lawmakers and proceed to the National Assembly for further review and discussion.

​​Meanwhile, stablecoins are another major focus of the legislation. This follows South Korean President Lee Jae-myung calling for a local currency-based stablecoin market to protect the nation's monetary sovereignty in the digital age.

The government draft is expected to stipulate a bank-centered consortium model for issuing Korean won-pegged stablecoins, where banks would hold more than 50% in the consortium.

The country's central bank has pushed for this bank-first structure, having previously expressed concerns that unchecked stablecoin issuance could undermine monetary stability. This has also drawn backlash from local industry players who argued that setting this rule would deter innovation.

Other provisions in the bill are expected to further clarify different types of businesses, governance and internal controls of crypto projects, and establish conduct standards comparable to those for traditional financial companies. It would also further strengthen user protection requirements, including rules on asset segregation and operational safety.
