#4896. Is the AI Bubble Finally Popping? Nvidia Crashes 48% as Wall Street Experts Sound the Alarm

Is the AI Bubble Finally Popping? Nvidia Crashes 48% as Wall Street Experts Sound the Alarm

Wall Street's artificial intelligence boom is facing its biggest test yet.

Nvidia plunged 14% on Friday, the Nasdaq Composite lost 5.3%, and the broader S&P 500 entered correction territory as investors questioned whether the trillions of dollars being poured into AI infrastructure will ever produce the expected returns.

The Nasdaq closed at 22,617.73, approximately 18% below its October 6 record closing high of 27,599.79. Its 10% weekly decline was the worst since 2020, while the index has now erased its gains for 2026.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Nvidia with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

Nvidia, the company that powered much of the AI rally, finished Friday at $125.53, down 47.5% from its October closing record of $239.24.

The chipmaker lost nearly 23% during the week as the fallout from former CEO Jensen Huang's departure and reports of an SEC accounting investigation added to concerns about the AI business model.

Other AI-linked companies have suffered even steeper losses. CoreWeave, which rents computing capacity to AI developers, closed at $39.51, almost 80% below its 2025 peak, while Oracle has lost roughly 70% from its highs and finished the week at $101.19.

AMD shed 20.5% over the week, closing at $424.39, while Alphabet lost 10.3% to $265.29. The iShares Semiconductor ETF, SOXX, dropped 17.4% during the same period, showing that investors are selling across the companies supplying the AI industry, not merely its most speculative businesses.

The selloff accelerated just as Anthropic completed its historic $81 billion IPO. The Claude developer priced its shares at $83 and closed its first trading session 17% higher, but its stock fell another 9% on Friday as enthusiasm for the offering failed to restore confidence in the wider sector.

With so many stocks in the red, it’s logical that many AI fanboys are starting to get nervous.

Traders on Polymarket have become increasingly pessimistic. Contracts assessing whether the AI industry will experience a defined downturn showed approximately 54% odds by December 31, 2026, and 65% by June 30, 2027, according to Friday's market snapshot.

The market has attracted approximately $4.1 million in trading volume, but its definition of a bubble bursting is more demanding than a falling stock market. At least three specific events must occur within a single 90-day period, including Nvidia closing 50% below its all-time high, SOXX falling 40%, major AI suppliers experiencing similar collapses, or severe financial problems at AI developers.

SOXX has already fallen approximately 43% from its split-adjusted record closing price, satisfying one of the specified price thresholds on paper. Nvidia is approaching another, although crossing either threshold alone would not settle the contract.

The damage has also spread into cryptocurrencies, undermining expectations that 2026 would bring a sustained recovery. Bitcoin finished the week near $58,895, down 9.8% over the period and more than 50% below its 2025 peak.

The total cryptocurrency market capitalization fell to approximately $2.01 trillion, down from more than $4 trillion at its peak. As Decrypt reported Friday, the Crypto Fear and Greed Index dropped to 9, signaling extreme fear and its weakest reading since February.

This was supposed to be the year of the usual pre-halving moon. Instead, for the first time in the history of Bitcoin, the coin is on its way to have two consecutive red years.

Bitcoin has increasingly behaved like other speculative assets sensitive to liquidity conditions, particularly when investors expect borrowing costs to remain elevated.

The pressure is also visible in investment flows. U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $582.2 million in net withdrawals on Thursday, while forced liquidations added to the selling as Bitcoin broke below the psychologically important $60,000 level.

Owen Lamont, a portfolio manager at Acadian Asset Management and former University of Chicago finance professor, believes the warning signs have become difficult to ignore. After arguing in January that AI stocks were not yet in a bubble, he told Fortune this week that he now assigns a 70% probability to the U.S. stock market already being in one.

Lamont tracks four warning signs: excessive valuations, widespread belief in a speculative boom, new stock issuance, and investors pouring money into the market. His latest assessment suggests all four are now present, although his issuance figures are heavily influenced by SpaceX's massive IPO earlier this year.

Bank of America's November fund manager survey offers another indication of deteriorating confidence. Investors reduced their technology exposure by the most since 2006, and 52% expect at least one major AI hyperscaler to announce a capital spending cut within six months, Fortune reported.

The European Central Bank has also raised concerns about how AI development is being financed. Its November review estimates that capital expenditure by major American technology companies could reach $5.5 trillion cumulatively by 2030, with growing reliance on debt as infrastructure spending outpaces internally generated cash.

The similarities to the dot-com era are difficult to dismiss: investors are assigning enormous valuations to companies based on projected demand, businesses are racing to build infrastructure ahead of proven profitability, and financial markets are increasingly dependent on a small group of technology companies.

During the original dot-com collapse, the Nasdaq lost approximately 78% between March 2000 and October 2002. The present decline is considerably smaller, but history also offers a reminder that a technology can succeed commercially while the companies financing its expansion lose enormous amounts of market value.

There are important differences, however. Many of today's largest AI beneficiaries, including Nvidia, Alphabet, and Microsoft, operate profitable businesses with substantial existing revenues, unlike numerous internet startups whose valuations depended almost entirely on future growth.

Nvidia's latest quarterly results illustrate the contradiction. The company beat earnings estimates, reported record revenue and net income, and issued stronger-than-expected revenue guidance, yet its shares continued collapsing.

Richard Saperstein, chief investment officer at Treasury Partners, described the downturn as an "AI repricing" in comments to Reuters, citing slowing data center construction and tighter funding conditions. Lawrence Gillum, chief fixed income strategist at LPL Financial, offered a less alarming interpretation, saying the decline was "not a bubble bursting" and arguing that the AI investment cycle still has room to run.

Higher interest rates complicate both arguments. Investors are increasingly expecting the Federal Reserve to raise rates again in December, increasing financing costs for data centers while reducing the appeal of stocks whose valuations depend on profits years into the future.

The balance of evidence points to more than a routine bout of nerves, but less than a confirmed systemic bust. Stocks have repriced faster than the underlying technology has changed.

What happens next depends on whether actual demand for AI can justify the data-center spending spree—and whether the companies financing it can withstand tighter credit. The real test isn't simply whether Nvidia can keep selling chips. It's whether its customers can turn all that computing power into profits.

Right now much of the AI hype is just… hype. Markets are moving more by the perceived potential of AI in the future and not so much by what AI is actually doing right now.

Crypto investors have seen this before with ICOs, the TPS wars, the metaverse, the NFT fever and more. For many of them, the distinction may offer little comfort.

Bitcoin's slump shows how quickly markets can stop distinguishing between promising technologies and overpriced bets when confidence disappears. The dot-com crash didn't kill the internet; it killed the assumption that every internet company would make its investors rich.

AI may be the next world-changing technology to prove that being right about the future is not the same as making money from it.

#4895. Visa launches experimental AI agent wallet for eligible US users to earn OUSD from paid tasks

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

U.S. users with eligible Visa cards can verify AI agents to access paid assignments on Taskmarket.

Visa Crypto Labs launched an experimental new version of its Visa CLI agent wallet, allowing approved U.S. users with eligible Visa cards to verify AI agents and earn OUSD from paid tasks.

In a statement on Sunday, Cuy Sheffield, Head of Visa Crypto Labs, said users can connect the wallet, add a Visa card on file, and use that credential to verify their agent and register it in Visa's Agent Directory. The wallet is built on Tempo using the Machine Payments Protocol.

Users can earn rewards when agents complete various tasks, such as paid surveys, music composition, market research, and gaming, according to the statement. Taskmarket, one of the platforms Visa is integrating with, allows buyers to post tasks and pay agents in OUSD or USDC on Tempo and Base if their submissions are accepted. Its website showed more than 25 open tasks for verified agents on Monday, with rewards ranging from 10 to 50 OUSD.

Sheffield said the product is intended to help users expand their productive capacity, and not replace them entirely.

"Rather than agents replacing individuals, we believe they extend an individual’s productive capacity to turn their thoughts and ideas into tangible insights and outputs that can create value in the global economy," he wrote.

The launch expands Visa's Trusted Agent Protocol and Agent Directory beyond their earlier applications in e-commerce and business payments, according to the statement.

Visa launched the protocol last October as a tool to help merchants establish whether an AI agent was authorized to transact on behalf of a person or business. Cloudflare, Akamai, and Billtrust were among the first companies to pilot the system.

Sheffield said Visa is extending the same verification tool to agent wallets and task marketplaces, starting with Visa CLI and Taskmarket. Expansion to other platforms is planned for later.

#4892. Critics of Netflix’s FTX drama point to Obama’s producer credit

Series creator Graham Moore said he invented a scene in which Changpeng Zhao urges Alameda Research to trade ahead of FTX customers’ orders.

Critics who say Netflix’s new FTX drama favors Sam Bankman-Fried over Binance co-founder Changpeng “CZ” Zhao have pointed to former US President Barack Obama’s role as an executive producer.

DRW founder Don Wilson said Sunday that Netflix’s FTX drama The Altruists portrayed Bankman-Fried too sympathetically at the expense of Zhao.

In the fourth episode, Zhao’s character urges Bankman-Fried and Caroline Ellison to give Alameda Research advance access to FTX customers’ orders so it can trade ahead of them.

Series creator Graham Moore identified the scene as fiction in a Friday essay for Vulture. Moore wrote that he believed the pressure the pair faced was real, though “there’s no evidence that CZ ever specifically pressured FTX to front-run.”

Zhao called a clip from the series “complete BS” on Friday, a day after the eight-episode drama premiered. Investment research firm Farside Investors also singled out the front-running scene, saying the show cast Zhao as the villain.

Wilson called the clip an “epic fail by Netflix.” In a second post, he cited the former US president’s executive producer credit. Netflix lists both Barack and Michelle Obama as executive producers and names Moore and Jacqueline Hoyt as showrunners.

In a Sunday post on X, an account called StarPlatinum pointed to the Obamas’ producer credits and Bankman-Fried’s donations to Democrats. The post did not offer evidence that the donations influenced the show.

Related: Sam Bankman-Fried wrote to actor portraying him in Netflix series

Bankman-Fried made about $40 million in publicly reported political donations during the 2022 election cycle, mostly to Democratic candidates and groups. Federal prosecutors later said he also used FTX customer funds to make political contributions to candidates from both parties.

In November 2022, Zhao announced that Binance would sell its holdings of FTT, FTX’s token.

At Bankman-Fried’s 2023 trial, Ellison, Alameda’s former CEO, said she thought Zhao’s announcement “contributed to the situation.” She said FTX collapsed primarily because Alameda could not repay more than $10 billion in FTX customer funds.

Bankman-Fried was sentenced to 25 years in prison in 2024 for orchestrating a fraud that took more than $8 billion from FTX customers.

FTX’s bankruptcy estate sued Binance and Zhao in November 2024, seeking $1.76 billion paid in a 2021 share repurchase. The estate also sought damages over Zhao’s public statements. Binance called the claims “meritless.”

In July, US Bankruptcy Judge Karen Owens dismissed the claims over Zhao’s statements because FTX’s own misconduct barred them. She allowed the estate to pursue its separate claim over the buyback.

#4888. Signal Calls OS-Level AI an 'Existential Threat' and Blocks Screenshots to Fight It

Signal Calls OS-Level AI an 'Existential Threat' and Blocks Screenshots to Fight It

Signal is moving to wall off its messages from the AI assistants now baked into smartphones, restricting screenshots so that systems like Google’s Gemini and Apple’s Siri can’t quietly scan and store users’ private conversations.

The encrypted-messaging nonprofit said Thursday it will block the latest agentic AI features built into iOS 27 and, on Android, automatically enable a “screen security” setting whenever it detects an AI assistant trying to read a user’s Signal screen.

The move extends a tactic Signal first used last year against Microsoft’s Recall, the Windows feature that captured periodic screenshots; the company blocked it to keep Recall from storing images of Signal messages by default.

The concern, Signal says, is that operating-system AI agents work by “scanning” whatever is on a user’s screen, which means Signal data gets captured and handed to those systems to process.

Once that happens, the content lands in whatever local database or remote server the AI is using, outside Signal’s control and beyond the encryption guarantees that have defined the app for a decade.

Users who still want to take screenshots can disable the protection in Signal’s settings, though the company warns that doing so could expose their messages, and their contacts’ messages, to the AI agents.

Signal also conceded the fix is incomplete: It doesn’t stop sneakier methods of access, such as pulling data straight from a device’s filesystem, and it can’t yet be implemented on macOS, where Signal says Apple lacks the documented tools to block screenshots reliably.

Signal also flagged third-party agents like Meta’s Muse, warning they exploit accessibility permissions meant to help people, not to serve as “a skeleton key” for AI.

The worry isn’t hypothetical: A journalist found that Muse had accessed his private iMessages despite his settings and then gave a false explanation of how it knew their contents, an episode that crystallized fears about how much agents can reach once granted deep device permissions.

Signal’s move lands amid a broader reckoning over AI agents accessing data they shouldn’t, from rogue agents breaching systems to banks scrambling to keep autonomous software out of sensitive accounts.

#4886. Bitcoin Price Fades Near $61,000 as Stocks Soar on Huang’s Return

The bitcoin price slipped back to near $61,100 on Monday afternoon, giving up nearly all of its morning spike even as Wall Street held one of its biggest rallies in years on Nvidia’s decision to reinstate Jensen Huang as CEO.

Bitcoin jumped above $64,000 shortly after the Nvidia news broke before 10 a.m. ET and topped out near $64,500. The move reversed within hours, and the bitcoin price dipped to around $60,800 by 2:45 p.m. ET. It remains up only modestly on the day.

The round trip was costly for leveraged traders. Bitcoin liquidations reached about $321 million, with short bets making up $224 million of that total. Liquidations across the broader crypto market topped $762 million.

Stocks kept climbing while bitcoin slid. The Nasdaq Composite was up about 6.9% near 24,170 at about 3:40 p.m. ET, while the S&P 500 gained about 5% and the Dow rose about 3.2%. The S&P 500 had fallen seven straight days before Monday.

Nvidia (NVDA) led the move, trading near $165.66, up about 32% from Friday’s close of $125.53. The board voted unanimously to bring Huang back as president, CEO and director, 12 days after he resigned.

A stronger dollar may be weighing on the bitcoin price. The dollar index rose about 0.65% to 104.40, its strongest level since April 2025, as the stock rebound put more Federal Reserve rate hikes back in play. The Fed meets Dec. 8-9, and a quarter-point hike remains the base case.

Adam Haeems, head of asset management at Tesseract Group, pointed to steady selling during U.S. market hours since Huang first stepped down. “Bitcoin has fallen in seven of the eight U.S. sessions since Huang went, today’s included, and last week each session’s fall lined up with that day’s ETF outflow,” he said.

Crypto stocks held up far better than the bitcoin price. Strategy (MSTR) was up about 9.3% near $85.03 in afternoon trading, after it disclosed a purchase of 457 bitcoin last week. Coinbase (COIN) gained about 6.7% to near $108.78, Robinhood (HOOD) rose about 8.8% to near $67.64 and Circle (CRCL) added about 8% to near $51.33.

Miners with AI data center businesses posted the biggest moves. IREN (IREN) jumped about 23% to near $14.57, CleanSpark (CLSK) rose about 12.6% and MARA Holdings (MARA) gained about 10%. Core Scientific (CORZ) climbed about 9.4% and Riot Platforms (RIOT) added about 7.1%.

Monday’s spot bitcoin ETF flows are the next test. The funds lost about $1.79 billion last week, their fourth straight week of net outflows, as bitcoin slid toward $58,000 on Friday and hit a weekend low near $55,100.

“Tonight’s ETF flow figure is the first real test of this bounce,” Haeems said. Strong inflows would suggest buyers are returning, while another round of outflows would raise doubts about how far the rebound can run.

Traders also face Wednesday’s inflation data and thin trading around Thanksgiving in a holiday-shortened week. Wintermute OTC trader Jasper De Maere said he isn’t ready to call a bottom for the bitcoin price. “A few days of data do not confirm a trend and these series turn slowly,” he said.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $61,100.

#4884. ‘The dots aren’t done,’ Michael Saylor says as Strategy buys another 457 BTC for $30 million

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Strategy acquired another 457 BTC for approximately $29.6 million last week, with its total holdings now at 851,305 BTC.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) treasury company Strategy Strategy, Inc. (MSTR) bought another 457 BTC for $29.6 million at an average price of $64,898 per bitcoin between Nov. 16 and Nov. 22, according to a Monday filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

The acquisitions increase Strategy's total bitcoin holdings to 851,305 BTC, bought for a total cost of approximately $64.2 billion at an average price of $75,446 per bitcoin, according to the filing. For perspective, that's equivalent to more than 4% of bitcoin's maximum supply cap, but reflects around $12 billion in paper losses at current prices.

The purchases were made using proceeds from at-the-market sales of its Class A common stock, MSTR. Strategy sold 1,925,676 MSTR shares for approximately $176.7 million last week. Approximately $17.9 billion worth of MSTR shares remain available under the ATM program, according to the filing.

Strategy also repurchased 2,183,747 STRC preferred shares between Nov. 16 and Nov. 22 for $211.8 million, using proceeds from its MSTR sales USD Cash reserve. The firm also used $17.6 million from its USD Reserve to pay for dividends on its preferred stocks.

On Friday, during the company's investor Q&A livestream, Strategy co-founder and Executive Chairman Michael Saylor said "natural, organic" demand for the company's STRC preferred stock at its current 12% dividend rate is likely between $5 billion and $7.5 billion.

Saylor described the current environment as a credit-market contraction, adding that the company is responding by reducing the amount of STRC outstanding until it reaches an equilibrium level that the market will support at the existing dividend rate. President and CEO Phong Le said the company has bought back about $3 billion of the preferred stock at prices between $95 and $99, below the $100 issuance price level.

As of Nov. 22, Strategy's USD Reserve and USD Cash balances stood at $3.73 billion and $1.09 billion, respectively, the firm said in the filing, after its board of directors authorized management to reallocate up to $1.0 billion from the USD Reserve to USD Cash.

Bitcoin fell another 6.3% last week, continuing a seven-week slump that saw the largest cryptocurrency drop to its lowest level since September 2024 over the weekend, wicking as low as $55,000 at one point. Bitcoin is currently trading for around $61,400 on Monday.

Despite the continued slide, Saylor posted yet another bitcoin acquisition tracker chart on Sunday stating, "The dots aren't done," indicating the potential acquisition announcement ahead of time.

MSTR fell 30.6% last week, closing on Friday at $77.80, its lowest level since February 2024, according to The Block's Strategy price page.

#4882. Fraudsters Used Fake Crypto Exchanges to Steal $37 Million, SEC Alleges

Fraudsters Used Fake Crypto Exchanges to Steal $37 Million, SEC Alleges

The Securities and Exchange Commission has charged eight entities with running a sprawling scheme that used fake crypto trading platforms and sham investment advisers to steal more than $37 million from U.S. investors.

The case targets eight Colorado-registered entities that the SEC says were likely operated by individuals overseas.

According to the complaint, filed in Colorado federal court, the defendants lured victims into believing they were working with a legitimate investment adviser and crypto trading platform, when both were entirely fake, “simply fronts to steal investor money.”

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Bitcoin with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The mechanics will sound familiar to anyone who has followed the rise of “pig butchering” scams. The SEC said the fraudsters advertised on social media to funnel investors into group and private chats, where operators posing as advisers first coached them to trade stocks on real U.S. markets before steering them toward a bogus crypto platform and pressing them to send as much money as possible. Victims believed they were buying real crypto assets; in reality, the funds were stolen.

To fake legitimacy, the operators went to unusual lengths, the SEC said, fabricating phony SEC certificates and even making filings with the SEC, FINRA and FinCEN, then pointing skeptical investors to those documents as proof they were real.

The scheme ran from August 2024 through May 2026 across four adviser-and-platform pairs, with the perpetrators abandoning one pair and spinning up the next every few months.

“The SEC continues to seek out and expose fraudulent investment scams that prey on U.S. investors,” said Laura D’Allaird, chief of the agency’s Cyber and Emerging Technologies Unit, urging the public to keep reporting such schemes.

The charged entities, carrying officious-sounding names like Convergent Wealth Advisors and Essential Crypto Exchange, face allegations of violating federal antifraud provisions, with several also accused of breaching the Investment Advisers Act.

The SEC is seeking injunctions, disgorgement and civil penalties. It credited cooperation from regulators in Hong Kong, Singapore, China and Switzerland, underscoring the cross-border nature of the fraud.

The case reflects a persistent threat, as fake platforms and social-engineering scams continue to drain billions from victims worldwide.

Chainalysis and others have documented how such operations, often run by organized crime from Southeast Asia, have scaled with the help of AI-generated personas and scripts, turning romance and investment cons into one of crypto’s costliest categories of fraud.

#4881. AI agents are using credit cards. Where does that leave crypto?

AI agents are already using credit cards to transact, raising questions about where crypto fits in the emerging agent economy.

The idea that crypto will become the default payment choice for autonomous AI agents is widespread in the industry. The thesis is that because AI agents can’t open bank accounts and need to make lots of transactions, they will inexorably gravitate to instant, decentralized and low cost crypto payments.But the credit card industry has no intention of letting that happen.

The Wall Street Journal reported on Nov. 19 that AI agents are already using credit cards to make purchases on consumers’ behalf, and banks and payment companies are racing to accommodate them. Crypto trader and co-host of the 1000x podcast Jonah Van Bourg posted a screenshot, and commented:

But experts argue the jury is still out. Professor emeritus of finance at Marquette University, David Krause, tells Magazine that credit cards offer some advantages, but high costs could be their downfall in certain scenarios.

Stablecoins, he says, make much more sense for “those situations involving micropayments.”

Credit card payments are the default for most human-initiated transactions on the web. So an agent tasked with booking a flight or a hotel room may also default to this type of payment as well.

Related: DeepSeek developing AI agent requiring less human input: Report

“When an agent books your flight or reorders your groceries, it’s acting for a person, at a merchant that already accepts cards, with consumer protections people expect, like chargebacks,” Lewis Nurcombe, chief revenue officer at WalletConnect, tells Magazine.

Visa’s recent paper on the rise of agentic commerce says tokenization can give agents permission to transact without needing to expose the cardholder’s underlying payment details, and spending controls can restrict the purchases they can make.

So when the merchant already accepts cards and has familiar mechanisms for handling disputed transactions, it’s hard to argue the virtues of introducing an entirely new payment system.

But the economics start to change when agents begin paying other machines.

AI agents may create a new economy of machine-to-machine transactions, where agents buy access to a dataset, request computing power, or pay to access a single piece of information. Many of these transactions may only be worth a fraction of a cent and potentially need to occur thousands of times without a human approving each one.

“If an agent wants to pay a few cents for one API call or one piece of data, card fees don’t make sense,” Aitor Argomaniz, chief executive of AI payments company Nevermined, tells Magazine.

That’s because card networks were designed around transactions big enough to absorb fixed processing fees, rather than micro-payments.

Stablecoins offer a different model. Agents can hold funds in a wallet, make payments programmatically and settle transactions onchain without going through a conventional checkout process.

Nurcombe says that stablecoins are most useful “wherever the transaction is too small, too frequent or too global for cards to make sense.”

x402 is an open, neutral standard for internet-native payments. Source: x402

Nevermined compared 100 API calls priced at 10 cents each. Using a conventional card via Stripe would not be an option as the payments infrastructure network rejects charges under $0.50. But using stablecoins, the company estimates the same 100 payments would cost around 10 cents in total using Coinbase’s x402 facilitator on Base.

That doesn’t mean crypto payments will automatically win however, as a number of microtransactions could be aggregated into a single card payment. Stablecoin transactions also carry costs related to infrastructure, conversion, custody and liquidity.

The Bank for International Settlements (BIS) found that stablecoin transfers can still incur trading, conversion and withdrawal fees, while limited liquidity between stablecoins and local currencies can add further friction.

In the transition to agentic finance, there’s a more fundamental question to overcome: who is responsible when an agent gets something wrong?

Related: Banks are banning AI agents over security... and competition concerns

“I tell my students the real question isn’t ‘crypto or cards,’ it’s who solves the agent identity and liability attribution issues,” Krause says.

One partial solution is a separate wallet funded with a limited amount of money that could prevent an agent from accidentally draining a user’s main account.

WalletConnect is developing ways for users to give agents access to dedicated wallets while retaining control over the rest of their funds.

“The most an agent can ever spend is what the person chose to give it,” Nurcombe says.

But spending limits only mitigate the damage. They can’t determine who is liable when an agent makes an unauthorized purchase, buys the wrong service or falls victim to manipulation. Nor do they provide the kind of resolution consumers expect from established payment providers.

Nevermined compared 100 API calls priced at 10 cents each. Source: Nevermined.

That may mean banks and card networks have an advantage beyond just having more merchants on their systems; they already have relationships with customers, businesses and regulators, as well as established processes for handling disputes.

Krause says that stacks the odds in traditional finance’s favor. “My bet is the large TradFi players will ultimately win,” he says. “They’re the ones with funding access and are positioned to straddle the divide and offer a hybrid version.”

Nevermined supports payments using both cards and stablecoins, and Argomaniz expects the two systems to coexist, “at least for the next few years.”

This is where Van Bourg’s argument becomes harder for the crypto industry to dismiss — even if stablecoins are useful for machine-to-machine payments, established payment networks could just incorporate them without handing over control of the customer relationship.

So an agent might interact with Visa or Mastercard while the networks use blockchain infrastructure behind the scenes.

That would effectively turn blockchain into payment plumbing without businesses or consumers needing to hold crypto or interact with a decentralized protocol.

Mastercard completed its acquisition of stablecoin infrastructure provider BVNK in August, in a deal announced at up to $1.8 billion, giving Mastercard the infrastructure to connect fiat and onchain payments.

So, while AI agents could use blockchain-based payments where existing rails struggle, crypto could find itself powering the agent economy without capturing much of its value.

Magazine: How to preserve wealth and access funds during a national emergency

#4879. Solo bitcoin miner beats the odds to win $191,000 block reward

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The miner reported just 11.1 TH/s on Solo CKPool, with an estimated one-in-1,650 chance of finding a block in a year.

A solo bitcoin miner beat the odds to solve a block carrying nearly $191,000 in rewards on Sunday.

The miner collected a total of 3.129 BTC ($190,764) for mining block 977,057, using solo bitcoin-mining software from CKpool, according to data from Bitcoin explorer Mempool. That comprised the 3.125 BTC block subsidy reward ($190,538) and 0.004 BTC ($226) in transaction fees, before CKpool's 2% fee.

"Congratulations to miner bc1q~zy9j for solving the 323rd block - yet another lucky one at EU solo.ckpool.org with only ~11.1 TH of hashrate on a 3.47 TH worker," CKpool developer Con Kolivas posted on X. "A miner of this size has about a one in 1650 chance of solving a block in a year."

The reported 11.1 TH/s is comparable to the combined output of a few home desktop miners, significantly smaller than top mining machines like Bitmain's U3S23H Antminer, with 1,160 TH/s. In comparison, top public bitcoin miners Bitdeer (BTDR), MARA Holdings (MARA), and Riot (RIOT) manage a hashrate of approximately 82.3 EH/s, 71.2 EH/s, and 37.2 EH/s, respectively, according to Ziven data. Those figures are approximately 7.4 million times, 6.4 million times, and 3.4 million times greater than the solo miner's hashrate.

The solo miner's hashpower is equivalent to about 0.000001% of the Bitcoin network's total estimated hashrate of 1.07 ZH/s on Nov. 23, per Mempool's data, which measures the total computational power dedicated to the network.

Smaller Bitcoin miners usually join shared pools to earn steady, proportional rewards since solo mining has an extremely low chance of winning a block. However, some try solo pools instead for a shot at the full block reward, hoping for a big, lottery-esque win despite the odds.

While CKpool is technically a mining pool, it is not used in the conventional sense where miners combine their hashrate for a better chance at block discovery by collaborating and sharing the rewards. Instead, miners using the solo CKpool work independently, keeping the full block reward if they find one, minus CKpool’s 2% fee. It avoids the need to run your own full node and pool infrastructure, making it a simple setup compared to true solo mining.

However, it's not the first time a solo miner has won Bitcoin blocks.

In April, another miner using CKpool in a solo mining configuration collected roughly $210,000 for mining block 943,411. A miner of that size has roughly a 1-in-28,000 chance of finding a block on any given day, Kolivas said at the time.

In November 2025, another solo miner reporting just 6 TH/s won a block worth around $265,000 at the time.

Solo miners with more substantial managed or rented hashrates have also found several Bitcoin blocks in recent years.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) is currently trading for around $61,221, up 1.2% over the past 24 hours, per The Block's BTC price page.

#4878. Strive (ASST) Buys No Bitcoin Last Week, Holdings Stay at 43,404 BTC

Strive (Nasdaq: ASST) bought no bitcoin last week and sold no stock to fund new purchases, leaving its holdings unchanged at 43,404 BTC, according to a Monday 8-K filing.

The Dallas-based company reported that its bitcoin count was unchanged between Nov. 13 and Nov. 20. It sold no Class A common shares, no SATA preferred shares and no SATS preferred shares in its at-the-market programs from the Nov. 13 close through the Nov. 20 close.

The pause follows a slowdown from the October sprint that built the stack. Strive bought 251 bitcoin for $17.5 million the prior week at an average of $69,853 per coin, after adding just 29 coins the week before that.

Bitcoin traded near $61,000 on Coinbase on Monday afternoon. That is about 12.6% below the $69,853 average Strive paid for its most recent purchase. At that price, the 43,404-coin stack is worth roughly $2.65 billion.

The filing gives no reason for the pause, and the company has not said whether it plans to resume weekly buying. Strive has typically disclosed its holdings on Mondays, so next week’s update will show whether the stack grows again.

Most of the stack was built quickly. Strive held 28,000 bitcoin on Sept. 30 and acquired 15,124 more between Oct. 1 and Oct. 30, including a single 6,182-coin purchase. The company closed a registered offering of 7 million SATS shares on Oct. 20 for about $609 million in net proceeds, which funded much of that run.

Strive’s cash and cash equivalents slipped to $462.4 million as of Nov. 20 from $465.3 million a week earlier, a decline of $2.9 million. The filing does not break out what drove the change.

The company also still holds 505,000 shares of Strategy’s variable-rate STRC preferred stock. The fair value of that position fell to $47.3 million from $50.5 million, a drop of about $3.2 million, with the share count unchanged.

Share counts were also flat. Strive reported 118,133,002 Class A shares and 9,198,107 Class B shares, for 127,331,109 effective common shares. Outstanding preferred stood at 14,030,176 shares of SATA and 7,141,037 shares of SATS, the 15% perpetual preferred it has used to fund most of its purchases.

Strive’s holdings rose from 43,153 bitcoin on Nov. 6 to 43,404 on Nov. 13, and the filing shows no change since. With no common or preferred stock sold, the company raised no new capital through its offering programs last week, which left cash as its main resource for dividends and operations.

Strive shares were trading near $15.55 at 3:56 p.m. ET on Monday, up about 7.1% from Friday’s close of $14.51, according to Yahoo Finance. The stock opened near $17.64, a gain of more than 20%, and gave back much of it through the session.

The swing tracked bitcoin-linked stocks broadly after Nvidia reinstated CEO Jensen Huang and sent equities sharply higher, a move Bitcoin Magazine covered earlier Monday. Bitcoin spiked above $64,000 in the morning before sliding back toward $61,000 during U.S. trading hours.

The stock has lost much of its October run. ASST closed at $30.75 on Oct. 21, so Monday’s price is about 49% lower. It is still roughly 5% above its 2025 year-end close of $14.76.

Strive’s preferred stocks remain a central part of its model. On Nov. 12, the board raised the SATA dividend rate to 13.25% for periods beginning Dec. 1, while SATS continues to pay 15%. Both pay daily cash dividends, so the company’s cash balance funds a steady outflow even in weeks without purchases.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $61,000.

#4876. GTA 6 Leaker Dangles a PC Port Behind a $15 Million Crypto Pump

GTA 6 Leaker Dangles a PC Port Behind a $15 Million Crypto Pump

A notorious Grand Theft Auto 6 leaker is holding a potential PC version of the game hostage to a cryptocurrency payday, promising to release the final file needed to make a leaked build playable once its meme coin hits a $15 million market cap.

The leaker, who goes by Cyberleek, has released what it claims is a near-complete 209 GB Xbox build of GTA 6 from November 2026 via torrent, according to a report from PC Gamer.

The one piece withheld is the main executable, the file that would let modders begin emulating the game on PC, potentially bringing it to the platform long before Rockstar Games intends.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Bitcoin with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

Here’s the crypto catch. Cyberleek says it will release the executable on Nov. 23 regardless, but will drop it early if its token, called CYBERLEEK, reaches a $15 million market cap. That’s half the $30 million target the leaker floated in October.

The pressure tactic doesn’t appear to be working: the token is trading around $0.002 as of the weekend, with a market cap of roughly $1.3 million, more than $13 million short of the goal. The coin is down some 20% on the week, but the numbers will fluctuate wildly given the very thin liquidity on a small cap coin.

The setup has drawn open skepticism that it’s a cash grab, or worse. Observers have warned it could be an attempt to pump the coin before a rug pull, the crypto term for abandoning a token after luring buyers in. Cyberleek has also claimed to hold a PS5 build as a fallback if the Xbox files can’t be made to work.

The episode sits at the increasingly busy intersection of crypto, piracy, and AI. Modern Xbox games are architecturally closer to PC software than PlayStation titles, and AI coding tools have accelerated the kind of decompilation work that makes fast ports plausible, the same dynamic behind a wave of AI-assisted game ports this year.

It also caps a chaotic launch window for GTA that crypto has repeatedly crashed. One trader banked around $66,000 on a GTA 6 meme coin amid ticker confusion, and the game’s disc-free, digital-only release has fueled broader debate, with GameStop’s CEO arguing that digital-only games have hurt consumers.

None of the leaked files have been independently confirmed as genuine, and there is still no official GTA 6 PC version; Rockstar, famously litigious, could respond with legal action rather than a port.

#4874. Pump.fun draws criticism over private key imports as it retires external wallet sign-in on web

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Former Mt. Gox CEO Mark Karpelès questioned Pump.fun’s description of the process as end-to-end encrypted.

Solana-based trading platform Pump.fun’s plan to end external wallet sign-in on the web has drawn criticism over its request that users import private keys to keep their existing wallet-linked accounts.

Users can sign in with an email address or opt for social logins through Google, Apple, or GitHub instead of connecting an external wallet such as Phantom, Solflare, or Ledger, according to a statement.

After Nov. 30, users who access Pump.fun through an existing wallet session will be prompted to add an email or social login, the platform said, adding that their accounts and funds will remain intact.

Pump.fun said private keys imported during the migration process are encrypted in the browser and held by its login provider, Privy.

Meanwhile, Google sign-in will not work inside wallet browsers, including Phantom's in-app browser, and users might need to sign in with email or switch to Safari or Chrome, Pump.fun said.

Former Mt. Gox CEO Mark Karpelès objected to the migration in posts on Sunday and Monday. According to his analysis, Privy’s infrastructure decrypts and holds imported keys, which he argued makes Pump.fun’s description of the process misleading.

"Calling this 'end-to-end encryption' is misleading at best," Karpelès wrote in a post. "End-to-end encryption means only you can decrypt. Here the encryption does happen in your browser, but the decryption key belongs to Privy: their infrastructure (a TEE) decrypts your private key, keeps it, and signs with it for whoever passes their login check "

Karpelès argued that requiring users to copy and paste their private keys was illogical because the platform already uses standard "Sign in with Solana" cryptographic messages to verify users.

The migration affects only web sign-in. It does not alter users’ wallet addresses, positions, callouts, followers, or usernames, according to Pump.fun. The mobile app, API sessions, and trading are unaffected.

The Block reached out to Pump.fun and Privy for comment.

#4869. OKXICE launches tokenized securities venue with 113 assets confirmed for 24/7 trading

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The current lineup of issuers and asset managers includes eToro, Fidelity, Strive, BitGo, Circle, Coinbase, Robinhood, and Strategy.

OKXICE, the joint venture between OKX and Intercontinental Exchange, has launched its tokenized securities venue (TSV) for eligible U.S.-based OKX Inc. customers through the OKX app.

The platform launches with 113 assets, including issuer stocks and exchange-traded funds. The current lineup of issuers and asset managers includes eToro, Fidelity, Strive, BitGo, Circle, Coinbase, Robinhood, and Strategy.

Every token traded on the venue represents a security entitlement backed 1-to-1 by an underlying share, and offers applicable shareholder rights and economic benefits. This includes dividends, voting rights, and corporate actions.

"With OKXICE TSV, we're breaking down the barriers of traditional trading and bringing real securities into a 24/7 marketplace — without sacrificing the shareholder rights and protections investors expect," OKXICE co-Chair Andrew Cuomo said in a statement shared with The Block. "We're not just talking about the future of Wall Street. We're building it."

Trading of the tokens is supported by AMM liquidity pools on Uniswap v4 that are built on OKX's Ethereum Layer 2, X Layer, while Securitize provides the tokenization infrastructure. Trading and liquidity provision are limited to eligible wallets by smart contracts, and the tokenized securities remain in customers' self-custodial wallets.

OKXICE TSV operates under the Securities and Exchange Commission's Innovation Exemption, which is a temporary five-year order allowing limited onchain trading of tokenized U.S.-listed stocks without the venues having to register as exchanges.

"We’re grateful to our first-mover issuers for helping us turn this vision into a live market, and we look forward to expanding access as the venue grows,” said Trabue Bland, co-Chair of OKXICE.

Meanwhile, the OKXICE TSV launch was preceded by widespread issuer pushback. According to the venue’s latest public notice, 67 companies have filed a Notice of Issuer Objection, preventing tokenized versions of their shares from being offered on the platform. These include major issuers such as Apple, Microsoft, Alphabet, Nvidia, Amazon, Tesla, Meta, JPMorgan Chase, Goldman Sachs, Walmart, Costco, Boeing, and Netflix, among others.

#4868. Google's Quantum Chip Beats Classical Limits in 'Impossible' Game

Google's Quantum Chip Beats Classical Limits in 'Impossible' Game

Google says its Willow quantum processor won a mathematical coordination game 98.3% of the time, above the 88.9% maximum attainable by classical strategies, in a Science study published Thursday.

The experiment tests quantum contextuality, so measurement outcomes depend on what else is measured alongside them. Google researchers explained that “you cannot assign fixed, pre-existing values to everything you might measure.”

Don’t panic. The result does not mean Google has a commercial quantum computer, nor that its largest experiment beat every classical machine. The 98.3% figure came from four qubits—the basic unit of information used to encode data in quantum computing—the 105-qubit run tested a different, more limited challenge.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Google with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The first test used the Mermin-Peres magic square game, which asks two players to fill intersecting parts of a three-by-three grid without communicating. Their answers must obey conflicting row-and-column rules and match at the shared square.

A prearranged classical strategy can succeed on no more than eight of nine assignments, or 88.9%. Entangled qubits can theoretically win every round, while Google’s physical processor won 98.3% across 900,000 trials, according to the researchers.

The researchers wrote that “our qubits coordinated better than any pre-written table allows.” Unavoidable hardware noise prevented the result from reaching the quantum ideal.

Google also measured contextuality. Any model with predetermined outcomes scores at most 4; quantum theory allows 6. Willow registered 5.590, with a reported uncertainty of 0.008.

A separate GHZ parity game asked up to 71 entangled qubits to deliver coordinated binary answers. Success was measured against the strongest classical winning rate, which approaches 50% with more players; Google remained above that limit throughout its tests, despite accumulating hardware noise.

Researchers also verified genuine entanglement across 45 qubits. In the hidden linear function task, a shallow quantum circuit had to produce a binary string satisfying mathematical constraints. The processor reached roughly 60% success using 105 qubits.

Google says the tasks exposed small, systematic errors that conventional randomized tests can hide. Its corporate blog called contextuality testing “a sensitive new method to catch subtle hardware errors,” potentially helping engineers evaluate circuits used for chemistry and materials research.

The work follows Google's October report of a 40-fold error reduction in preparing magic states, a resource needed for fault-tolerant quantum computing.

For Bitcoin, the looming danger is that a future fault-tolerant quantum computer could break digital signatures, but that is still far from happening. Google’s 105-qubit task was instead a shallow mathematical circuit, and its largest instance still failed in about four out of 10 runs.

#4865. Bitcoin, crypto and tech stocks rally as Nvidia reinstates Jensen Huang as CEO; NVDA jumps 30%

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Bitcoin climbed back above $63,000 and Nvidia jumped 30% pre-market as broader crypto and tech stocks rallied.

Crypto and tech stocks rallied early Monday as Nvidia's board announced it had reinstated Jensen Huang as president and CEO.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) climbed back above $63,000, up 3.4% for the day as the GMCI 30 index of leading cryptocurrencies saw a 5.5% jump amid broader tech market gains.

Just ahead of the announcement, Polymarket put the odds of Huang rejoining Nvidia by the end of the year at just 10.5%, and 2.5% before the end of this month.

Nvidia (NVDA) climbed more than 30% at one point in pre-market trading to around $164, a move that continued into early morning trading on Monday. The move helps alleviate a torrid trading period for the company, which has seen its stock fall around 43% over the past month amid Huang's resignation on Nov. 12, amid reports that he had been the target of a foreign intelligence effort.

Nvidia had appointed Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress as interim CEO while the board considered internal and external candidates, and as recently as Tuesday's quarterly earnings call, said it did not intend to reinstate Huang despite growing calls for him to return as its stock price plunged.

President Donald Trump was one of Huang's strongest supporters during the period, and said Monday that the board had "made the correct and proper decision" to reinstate him as CEO.

"The Country needs Jensen's leadership at NVIDIA in order to ensure the Success and Prosperity of not only the Company, but the American Economy," President Trump wrote Monday on Truth Social. "The United States Government has no reason to believe that Jensen's return to NVIDIA poses any risk to National Security. The Attorney General of the United States has confirmed that the U.S. Government does not possess evidence that he has engaged in any unlawful conduct."

In a statement on Monday, Nvidia said the reinstatement was effective immediately and was the result of a unanimous decision by the board.

"In the process of reaching its decision, the Board received input from senior levels of the United States government, and based on that input, the Board is confident that Mr. Huang's reinstatement poses no national security risk," it said.

Chair of the board Harvey C. Jones said Huang is the right person to lead Nvidia, and his reinstatement is in the best interests of the company's stockholders, employees, partners, and customers.

“I am honored to return to Nvidia," Huang said. "I take this responsibility with the greatest humility and renewed commitment."

The board confirmed that, in light of reporting that Huang had been the target of a foreign intelligence effort, the independent investigation it had already launched would continue. Huang fully supports the investigation and is committed to cooperating with it, the board said.

#4863. Bitcoin Rebounds to $61K After Weekend Plunge to Two-Year Low

Bitcoin Rebounds to $61K After Weekend Plunge to Two-Year Low

Bitcoin recovered to around $61,300 early on Monday after a turbulent weekend that saw it fall as low as $55,298, its lowest level since September 2024, as a slide in tech stocks and expectations of tighter Federal Reserve policy rattled risk assets.

The leading cryptocurrency was up 0.5% over 24 hours at time of publication, but down 4.5% over the past week, according to CoinGecko data. It remains about 51% below its all-time high of $126,080.

The weekend low came a day after Bitcoin broke below $60,000 on Friday, as AI stocks slumped following Anthropic's IPO.

"Bitcoin's sharp volatility over the weekend was primarily the result of a steep pullback in U.S. tech stocks, expectations of further monetary policy tightening by the Federal Reserve, and deleveraging across global risk assets," Tim Sun, senior researcher at HashKey Group, told Decrypt.

If growth expectations keep falling while inflation keeps the Fed hiking, "two key pillars that previously supported risk-asset rallies—ample liquidity and strong growth expectations—could be weakened simultaneously," Sun said. More than $2 billion in leveraged liquidations over the weekend had released some short-term selling pressure, he added, creating conditions for a possible rebound.

Spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $669.2 million in net outflows on Friday, their largest single-day outflow since June 25, according to SoSoValue data. That brought last week's net outflows to $1.79 billion, the funds' third consecutive week of withdrawals topping $1 billion, and November's total so far to $4.14 billion, by Decrypt's calculation. Total net assets fell to $71.3 billion, the lowest since June 30.

"The weekend correction looks less like a Bitcoin-specific event and more like part of a wider market correction," Neil Steinhardt, head of Nexo U.S., told Decrypt, noting that gold and equities also fell. ETF outflows had worsened the drawdown, he said, adding that equities appeared to have led the sell-off.

It is now below its median since launch, while outflows run near $260M per day.

The last stretch this quiet, in mid-2024, came before the run to $100K. Periods of apathy tend to be better times to accumulate than periods of hype. pic.twitter.com/xiJ3HF9cLj

— glassnode (@glassnode) November 22, 2026

Onchain analytics firm Glassnode took a longer view. ETF trading volume has fallen below its median since the funds launched, while outflows are running near $260 million a day, the firm tweeted on Sunday. The last stretch this quiet, in mid-2024, preceded Bitcoin's run to $100,000, it said.

BitMEX co-founder Arthur Hayes struck a more bearish note. Bitcoin had "decisively" broken below its previous local low, he tweeted on Saturday, adding that "$50,000 will be the next real test" if the Nasdaq kept falling. On Sunday evening, as Nasdaq futures weakened in Asian trading, Hayes tweeted that Bitcoin could retest $55,000, and noted that South Korea's Kospi index had fallen 5%.

$BTC just decisively broke below the previous local low. If Nasdaq keeps puking, $50,000 will be the next real test. pic.twitter.com/vn0VSpuWAz

— Arthur Hayes (@CryptoHayes) November 21, 2026

On Decrypt Predict, users put a 17% chance on Bitcoin falling to $55,000 before the end of November, as of 5:56 a.m. ET on Monday. The market settles on Binance's BTC/USDT price, which fell as low as $55,130 last week, according to the Binance chart Hayes shared. Users put a 1% chance on Bitcoin climbing back to $82,500 this month.

Traders now turn to October's personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation data, due on Wednesday, followed by the November jobs report on December 4 and the Fed's December 9 meeting.

The core PCE report "could help show whether the Fed is leaning toward 25 or 50 basis points," Steinhardt said, with markets then guided by the December meeting and comments from Fed Chair Kevin Warsh and other officials.

Traders are leaning toward the smaller move. As of 6:00 a.m. ET on Monday, CME FedWatch put the odds of a quarter-point hike on December 9 at 96.2%, up from 88.6% on Friday, with a 3.8% chance that rates stay unchanged. The odds of a half-point increase stood at zero, from 4.5% a week earlier.

U.S. spot selling remained heavy last week, according to a note from Bitfire Research shared with Decrypt. The Coinbase Bitcoin Premium Index, which tracks the gap between Bitcoin's price on Coinbase and on Binance, fell as low as negative 0.23%, the firm said.

Sun said he saw "no clear bullish catalyst" over the next one to two weeks, and expected Bitcoin to stay in a "high-volatility consolidation phase." Cooler inflation, steadier stocks and a return of ETF inflows could support a more sustainable recovery, he said, while hotter inflation and further tech losses could send Bitcoin back to its prior lows.

#4862. The 7 Best Electric Razors for Women in 2026, Tested by Beauty Editors

It's time to ditch the disposable before your holiday party.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

My interest in hair removal started in elementary school when I snuck into my mom's medicine cabinet and dragged her razor across my leg like I was coloring with my favorite box of markers. Needless to say, I've learned the right way to shave since then (namely to apply shaving cream and gently glide my razor toward the grain with light pressure).

I've had a lot of help perfecting my technique—as a beauty editor with a decade of testing experience, I have access to the best dermatologists and aestheticians. Yet, no matter how on-point my new shaving technique is now, most women's razors never get my legs quite as smooth. And other hair removal methods are super painful, ripping strands out at the root. Ouch!

But then, I upgraded to electric razors—and I've never turned back.

“A closer shave can feel nice, but because the surface of our skin is not perfectly smooth, it inevitably results in removal of some skin cells,” says dermatologist Caroline Robinson, MD. This can lead to microscopic nicks and irritation. “Electric razors can help avoid this. They’re typically less sensitizing,” says Dr. Robinson, because the shave they provide is not as close. At-home laser hair removal devices, waxing, sugaring, and threading are all great options for hair removal if you’ve got the time (and the coin), but if silky-smooth skin sans irritation is what you’re after, the answer is electric.

Over the past two years, Women's Health editors have been testing popular electric razors to determine which seven are worth the investment. Ultimately, we found that the Philips Beauty Series was the best multi-use product due to its five-blade razor and dual-sidedness that allows for a super close shave. Meanwhile, the Remington Smooth & Silky On-the-Go Shaver delivers on quality for quick touch-ups while being budget- and travel-friendly. Read on to see our other winners from our testing process.

A favorite of our editors, this hair removal device hits all of the targets we're looking for when buying an electric razor. For starters, it literally has 40 micro-grip tweezers in its motor to provide a super close shave, keeping your skin smooth for weeks at a time. While that might seem intimidating, it upgrades your in-shower shaving routine in a major way.

You can hit even the trickiest spots on your body. From your underarms and belly down to your ankles, this electric razor has you covered quite literally from head to toe. And with an impressive 50-minute runtime you can eliminate all of my unwanted hair with this device at least a couple of times before recharging it for just an hour. You’ll also appreciate that it's lightweight but not flimsy like it'll slip out of your grip. Plus, it's waterproof so you can shave in the shower without worrying about your new razor cranking out mid-shave.

An upgrade from the original Braun model, the more expensive but higher-tech Flex comes with a flexible head (hence, the name) that can uniquely adapt to your body's curves and contours—even the complicated corners of your bikini line. "The face and body brushes are especially useful, and I love how you can interchange the heads depending on what type of cleansing you want to do," says Women's Health contributor Isabel McMahon.

And yep, this little shaver is super lightweight to carry which is always a win.

It does a great job for the price, but you might notice that it seems to work best when the hair is longer, so it may not be ideal for targeting stubble. With the multitude of options available on Amazon, Ulta, and Dermstore, there are a couple of features to consider the next time you're in the market for a fresh shave:

More is not always better. In fact, a larger number of blades can sometimes increase irritation, says dermatologist Lauren Ploch, MD. On the other hand, fewer blades mean more passes over the skin, which can also cause irritation. It's about finding a happy medium that works for you. This may take some testing, so remember to try out your razor on a small patch of skin to see how you react before going all in.

Think about how well a razor feels and fits in your hand. You want something that’s well-balanced and not too heavy for the most comfortable shaving experience. Also consider if it will shave wet or dry. Not all electric razors can be used in the shower, Dr. Ploch points out. So, if you're a shower shaver, make sure to grab something waterproof and cordless.

You also want to make sure your razor head can contour to your body's curves, especially when you're targeting areas like the bikini line, underarms, toes, and even legs. Some of the razors on this list, like the Panasonic Close Curves Electric Razor, can uniquely adapt to the body's curves with their rotating heads.

If a razor is rechargeable (big plus already), make sure to check how long it takes to charge and the battery life. If you don't want to deal with a cord or annoying charge time, some of the options on this list—hi, Philips Series 5000 Electric Facial Hair Remover—are battery-powered, which may be more convenient while traveling.

Some razors come with trimmers, blade guides, and changeable blade heads for different areas of the body. If you're looking for more of a Swiss Army knife-type, multi-tool situation, take a look at the available attachments. Then, see if those are compatible with the parts of your body you want to tackle.

Believe it or not, many razors have a warranty, so if you're unsatisfied with your purchase, you can most likely return it for a longer period than you might think. Most razors on this list have a 30-day warranty, which gives you ample time for testing.

Our team tests a wide range of products and services to provide our readers with reliable, honest, and trustworthy information. It's our belief that our readers deserve the best, which is why our recommendations have been thoroughly tested and vetted by our very own editors. For the past year, Women’s Health editors, including myself, and contributors Jasmine Gomez and Sabrina Talbert, tested the bestselling, most popular, and top-rated electric razors. Our team also consulted four dermatologists, and then evaluated thousands of customer reviews and ratings to narrow down the seven best electric razors for a wide range of needs and preferences.

Ready for smooth, bump-free skin? Look no further than these editor-tested and expert-approved electric razors for women, below.

If you struggle with bumps and irritation, an electric razor might be your new best friend. Of course, you still need to make sure you use that electric razor properly. Dermatologists say you should follow the below best practices for a smooth shave every time.

Make sure your skin is well hydrated before shaving because using an electric razor over dry, irritated skin can make matters worse, says dermatologist Joshua Zeichner, MD. Dr. Robinson adds that you should also use a gentle cleanser prior to shaving. And, avoid fragrance or products known to cause irritation immediately before and after shaving, she says.

And please, do so in the direction of the hair growth, says Dr. Zeichner. Repeated strokes over the same area increase the likelihood of irritation and ingrown hairs.

Hydration is key. "I advise patients that they do not need a dedicated post-shave or aftercare lotion—a gentle moisturizer is just fine in these areas," says Dr. Robinson, who likes light, fragrance-free formulas. Aside from that, Dr. Robinson recommends making sure you follow any specific instructions on the product package—in other words, you should actually read the directions.

At the end of the day, everyone's going to have their personal preference, and it's not worth, ahem, splitting hairs if you prefer one type of razor over another. That being said, if your skin is prone to irritation, it might be time to ditch the traditional razor. With an electric razor, you’re not scraping against the skin really hard like a traditional razor does, says dermatologist Cameron Rokhsar, MD. This means that you won't see a lot of the cuts and burns that sometimes result from a manual blade, says Dr. Rokhsar. There are also areas of the body that might be better suited to shave with an electric razor. "Most women's electric razors are designed for our curvy areas," says Dr. Ploch. "I prefer electric razors around the ankles because manual razors cut this area easily."

Keeping your electric razor clean is incredibly important, says Dr. Rokhsar. Many derms will recommend changing a manual razor every time you shave, but you can't do that with an electric one, which makes running the blades under warm water after use a crucial step. This will keep it free of hair, shaving cream, and other residues.

Dr. Rokhsar also recommends swiping over the blades and the outside of the razor with a rubbing alcohol-soaked cotton ball regularly. "Especially if it sits in your bathroom moisture, bacteria will accumulate on the blades," he says. "It's an easy entry point for bacteria to go down the hair follicle." That's a big no-no if you want to stay free of irritation or infection.

Our team of editors follow Dr. Rokhsar's advice and it hasn't led us astray. And despite evaluating some of these razors for close to a year, we are pleased that we haven't noticed any rusting after using the alcohol-soaked cotton ball.

Another tip is to actually read and follow the maintenance instructions of your device. (Admittedly, we threw ours away since we've been using these for several months and like to keep the limited space of our beauty closet as streamlined as possible.) You can also prioritize using devices like the Philips Series 5000 Electric Facial Hair Remover that's easy to clean, as the head can easily be removed so you can dump shaved hair and dead skin cells straight into the garbage.

#4860. Strategy buys 457 Bitcoin, spends $212M on preferred share buybacks

Strategy raised its preferred stock buyback limit to $4 billion and authorized the transfer of up to $1 billion from its dollar reserve.

Michael Saylor’s Strategy bought 457 Bitcoin for $29.6 million last week and spent $211.8 million buying back its STRC preferred shares, more than seven times what it spent on Bitcoin.

The company paid an average of $64,898 per Bitcoin (BTC) in the week ended Sunday, bringing its holdings to 851,305 BTC, according to Monday’s filing with the US Securities and Exchange Commission. Strategy paid $64.23 billion for those holdings, or an average of $75,446 per Bitcoin.

Strategy raised $176.7 million in net proceeds from sales of its common stock. It used $29.6 million for Bitcoin purchases and $147 million to buy back STRC shares. It funded another $64.8 million in buybacks with cash.

Strategy’s board also raised the limit on preferred stock buybacks from $3 billion to $4 billion, leaving $1.12 billion available for further purchases. The board authorized management to move up to $1 billion out of the reserve it keeps for dividends and interest payments. Strategy can use that cash for other purposes, including buying Bitcoin.

Bitcoin fell to $55,081 on Saturday, its lowest price since September 2024, before rebounding above $61,000 on Monday. Crypto investor Kyle Chassé estimated Saturday that Strategy’s Bitcoin holdings were worth roughly $47 billion, about $17 billion less than the company paid for them.

#4859. Bitmine adds 7,819 ETH as Tom Lee makes the ‘hard money’ case

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The Ethereum treasury company added 7,819 ETH last week, bringing its holdings to 6.09 million tokens worth approximately $10.4 billion.

Bitmine Immersion Technologies Bitmine Immersion Technologies, Inc. (BMNR) increased its Ethereum holdings to 6,085,564 ETH after acquiring 7,819 ETH over the past week, the company said in a statement on Monday.

The holdings represent 4.98% of Ethereum's total supply of 122.2 million tokens and were worth approximately $10.4 billion as of Nov. 22, according to the Norwalk, Connecticut-based company.

Bitmine said it has staked 5,227,037 ETH worth $9 billion. The company projects $245 million in annualized staking revenue at current levels and up to $286 million once fully staked at its 2.74% seven-day yield.

The company reported combined crypto, cash, marketable securities and "moonshots" holdings of $11.1 billion, including $384 million in cash and marketable securities, 216 BTC, a $180 million stake in Beast Industries and a $54 million stake in Eightco Holdings.

Bitmine Chairman Tom Lee reiterated the company's conviction in ether, pointing to the asset's issuance metrics to argue that it functions as a store of value alongside Bitcoin.

Lee said ETH supply had grown 1.26% since June 30, 2025, when Bitmine adopted its Ethereum treasury strategy. He compared that figure with 0.82% annualized growth for bitcoin, while Solana (SOL), XRP and Cardano (ADA) recorded annualized rates of 7.6%, 5.4% and 2.9%.

"In other words, ETH and BTC have essentially the same inflation rate and both are 'hard money' measured in supply growth," Lee said.

ETH traded at $1,717.20 on Monday, up 0.89% over 24 hours but down 27.97% over the past month.

Bitmine shares gained 8.94% in pre-market trading after closing at $12.98 on Friday. The stock is down 44.43% over the past month and 53.64% year to date.

#4855. Solana Meme Coin for Fiona the Hippo Raises $100K for Cincinnati Zoo Charity

Solana Meme Coin for Fiona the Hippo Raises $100K for Cincinnati Zoo Charity

Fiona the Hippo, a fan-favorite pygmy hippo at the Cincinnati Zoo, died last week after swallowing a hat that accidentally ended up in her enclosure. Crypto bros have created a meme coin in her honor—raising $100,000 for charity in the process.

The tragic death came after a visitor tossed their knitted hat in the air, which then got caught by a ceiling fan and flung it through a small window into Fiona's habitat. The pygmy hippo lost her appetite as the hat got stuck in her small intestine, and veterinary staff tried their best to help Fiona pass the hat naturally—as surgery was deemed too risky. Unfortunately, Fiona died on Thursday, November 19, at nine years old.

As is often the case, crypto bros rushed to create a Solana meme coin that surged to a $3.52 million market capitalization the day after Fiona's death. It has since dropped more than 50% to $1.5 million, per DEX Screener.

Fiona the hippo has died after she ate a visitor's hat that fell into her enclosure at the Cincinnati Zoo. pic.twitter.com/YHbjoEEDhb

The Pump.fun-created token is donating 100% of the generated creator rewards to the Zoological Society of Cincinnati, the non-profit that runs the zoo and its scientific programs. So far, according to Donate.gg, $103,600 has been donated.

But, of course, profits are at the forefront of crypto traders' minds. One of the top traders turned a $1,637 investment into $43,800 in a matter of days, according to DEX Screener.

"I feel like animals are often treated more like attractions for people than living beings. Their safety and lives should come first, not just entertaining visitors," pseudonymous trader Athena Alpha told Decrypt. "This isn’t even the first time something like this has happened."

The most famous example happened in the same zoo. Endangered gorilla Harambe was fatally shot at the Cincinnati Zoo a decade ago, after a young boy fell into his habitat. This sparked global outrage, as the gorilla was killed through no fault of his own. In turn, Harambe became one of the most viral memes of the 2010s.

"I think stricter action needs to be taken against both visitors and zoo officials to make sure things like this don’t happen again," the trader said. "Honestly, I feel like a big part of the problem is the lack of accountability and consequences. Unless people are held responsible, nothing is really going to change."

The Cincinnati Zoo needs to ensure something like this can never happen again. https://t.co/vWeb8ekyVw pic.twitter.com/hqt35SOcP6

— Nati Sports (@Nati_Sports) November 19, 2026

This story has hit particularly hard, as Fiona was a beloved animal whose premature birth in 2017 drew attention. At birth, zoo keepers had to care for her because she was too small for her mother to look after. Fortunately, she survived this tough start to life and went on to become one of the zoo's most famous animals.

People's love for Fiona may have been boosted by the rise of Moo Deng, a cute pygmy hippo who went mega viral in 2024. As with Fiona, crypto traders made an unofficial meme coin for Moo Deng, but this one went much higher—peaking at a $610 million market cap.

Naturally, traders have made the comparison between the two pygmy hippos, with some suggesting that the Moo Deng meme coin's success should mean similar gains for the Fiona token.

With Fiona's meme coin sitting at a $1.5 million market cap, that thesis has yet to be proven.

"If Fiona goes to $1 billion, her legacy will live forever all around the world," one trader tweeted. "Do it for Fiona!"

#4854. DeFi Development extends Solana buying streak to 11 straight weeks, SOL treasury hits 2.62 million

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

DeFi Development Corp. increased its Solana treasury by approximately 5,180 SOL and equivalents to 2,624,491 SOL.

DeFi Development Corp. increased its Solana treasury by approximately 5,180 SOL and SOL equivalents to 2,624,491, marking its 11th consecutive week of accumulation since Sept. 14.

The Nasdaq-listed company said it aims to increase SOL per share through continued accumulation, staking, validator operations, and other onchain activities.

"The treasury update follows Solana’s Breakpoint conference in London, where announcements highlighted the network’s technical progress and expanding role in financial services," the firm said in a release, noting the network's mainnet beta designation was formally retired, recognizing advances in performance and resilience.

DeFi Development Corp. (DFDV) also highlighted its preferred stock, CHAD, which currently carries a variable 14% annualized dividend rate paid daily when declared by its board.

The firm added 9,482 SOL the previous week after recently reporting strong third-quarter results, with NAV per share more than quadrupling. It remains the second-largest publicly traded Solana treasury behind Forward Industries Solana (7.55 million SOL).

DFDV shares popped more than 12% in Monday's trading session but remain down 54% year to date. The price of Solana is down nearly 40% in 2026.
